# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance for Claude Code when working with the Pythia codebase. CLAUDE.md must be kept as a comprehensive, up-to-date description of the Pythia system. **Update this file at the end of every session where Claude Code changes the repo** — include any new architectural decisions, environment variables, failure modes, workflow changes, or component behavior that future sessions need to know.

## Project overview

Pythia is an end-to-end AI forecasting system for humanitarian crises. It scans countries for emerging hazards, produces triage signals, generates research briefs, runs LLM ensembles for probabilistic forecasts (SPDs), scores and calibrates them, and serves outputs via a FastAPI API and Next.js dashboard.

## Invariants

Hard-won rules, each learned from a specific production failure. **Read this section before
changing the subsystem it names.** Every rule here is also stated inline in the post-mortem that
produced it — in `## Known failure modes` below, or in
[`docs/archive/claude_md_failure_modes.md`](docs/archive/claude_md_failure_modes.md) for older
entries. When you add a failure-mode entry with a `**Rule:**` line, add the rule here too.

**Batch pipeline and LLM cost**
- Any provider batch must contain exactly one model — group by `(provider, model_id)`, never by provider alone.
- Any key used to replay stored LLM output across process boundaries must be scoped to the execution that created it.
- A batch-pipeline stage must never depend on batch-submission SUCCESS to find its own identity — the sync fallback is only a fallback if the stage can still start.
- A batch that yields nothing must record the provider's terminal state; "collected" with zero results is indistinguishable from success in every downstream view.
- "Lost discount" must be measured against what this pipeline actually batches — a provider is not a unit of batchability, a call family is.
- `POLLER_MAX_CHAIN × POLLER_TICK_MINUTES` must always exceed `PYTHIA_BATCH_MAX_WAIT_H` — never hardcode the cap, and never raise the wait ceiling without re-deriving it.
- Every workflow input that shapes a stage's cost or scope must be carried in the batch-state artifact — the poller, not a human, launches stages 2..N.
- In `pythia_pipeline_stage.yml`, never write a bare truthiness test on a boolean input — compare against `true` and `'true'` (or `!= true && != 'true'`), because the poller, not the UI, launches most stages.

**Verification and telemetry**
- A cache flag that is on in the workflow proves nothing — only a non-zero `cache_read_input_tokens` does.
- A feature flag that changes REQUESTS must be verified by response telemetry, not by the flag being set.
- Any diagnostic that exists only in a job log does not exist — logs expire, artifacts are the record.
- A parse failure downstream of a billed LLM call must be logged — `llm_calls.status='ok'` means the HTTP call succeeded, not that the output was used.
- A skip is not a failure; any status roll-up must run over the same list that decided what to actually do.
- Never map `sibyl` to `other`; any new phase must be added to both `phase_group`/`CANONICAL_PHASES` and the frontend `PHASE_ORDER`/`PHASE_LABELS`/`PHASE_COLORS`.

**Test-mode and dashboard correctness**
- An `is_test` parameter must default to None-and-inherit, never to `False` — a `= False` default converts a forgetful caller into silent production data.
- Any query that picks a "latest" run, or joins a test-stampable table (`questions`, `hs_runs`, `hs_triage`, `forecasts_ensemble`, `scores`) for a default dashboard view, must thread `include_test` and filter every table it touches.
- On the dashboard, a soft-fail must never look identical to "no data" — degrade visibly.

**DuckDB connection handling**
- Inside the pipeline, never open a raw read-only `duckdb.connect` on the Pythia DB path — use `pythia.db.schema.connect` (or `duckdb_io.get_db`).
- Never assume a `duckdb_io` handle survives a call into forecaster/pythia loader code — re-acquire it, don't reopen once and hope. Anything holding a `get_db()` connection across `_load_structured_data` (or the ipc_phases/acaps loaders) must reopen afterwards: those loaders `close_db()` the shared cached handle.
- `TransactionContext Error: Current transaction is aborted` is a statement about the *previous* statement — never convert it into an answer about the object being probed.

**Prompts and model configuration**
- When adding an `sd[...]` inject, grep `prompts.py` for a consumer — loading without rendering fails silently.
- Never hardcode a coverage-era label in a prompt — derive it from the rows.
- Never rebuild a `ModelSpec` field-by-field — use `dataclasses.replace()`, or new params are silently dropped at that seam.

**Connectors and upstream data**
- Never trust an ACLED request-side filter — verify by the returned rows' own country fields.
- Attribute ACLED events with the multi-key + name-fallback resolver, never a single `.get("iso3")`; and test connectors against the REAL response shape (`#country+code`/name-only), not a mocked `iso3` key.
- When a "0/N events resolve" warning appears for a country, check the ACLED name form against `countries.csv`/aliases first.
- Keep `_password_grant`'s params in lockstep with ACLED's documented curl example — the gateway reports missing/malformed params as 415, not 400.
- On a monthly publication, staleness must be measured in EDITIONS, not days.
- When a fallback silently changes WHICH edition of a monthly publication you get, it is not a retry — treat it as data loss and be patient first.
- When copying a fix for a parse-then-format crash, sweep ALL sibling scrapers for the same pattern.

**Ground truth and base rates**
- A question's base rate must be drawn from the same source class and quantity definition that will resolve it. Precedence may operate WITHIN a definitional class; it must never operate ACROSS classes.
- `in_need` is modelled population exposure (GDACS: hazard footprint × population), never reported impact — it must never enter a PA series, in a base rate or in a resolution.
- When collapsing revisions of one fact, the LATEST informed report wins: source priority, then non-null value, then most-recent publication. `drop_duplicates(keep="first")` after an all-ascending sort keeps the earliest — check the sort direction before trusting it.
- An adapter can only carry what `CANONICAL_COLUMNS` lists (`resolver/transform/adapters/base.py`), and `load_and_derive` drops anything outside its own copy of that list — check both before assuming connector metadata reaches `facts_resolved`.

**PA resolution machine (`resolver/hazard_resolution/`)**
- "The source said nothing happened" and "we could not read the source" are different facts — a connector that blurs them manufactures a zero out of an outage. Every connector returns an outcome, never raises, and an unread rung is recorded as unread.
- A hazard is DETECTED in every month its date range overlaps, but its figure is attributed WHOLLY to the start month. Never split a figure across months — a source states one number for the event, and apportioning it invents a breakdown nobody reported.
- Absence of a figure before the freeze deadline is PENDING (write nothing), not NO_DATA. EM-DAT lands weeks late; declaring "no data" the week of an event records our impatience as a fact about the world.
- A sanity check flags, it never rewrites. The ladder's answer survives the GDACS ceiling, the population cap and every conflict rule — `conflict_rule: ladder_with_flag` means exactly that.
- A displacement figure is a LOWER BOUND on people affected, labelled at every layer (`value_type`, `rule_fired`, provenance). Losing the label turns a floor into an answer.
- An LLM-extracted figure is only admissible if its quote is FOUND in the document text that was actually sent. A prompt forbidding fabrication is not a mechanism; quote verification is.
- The extractor never converts units and never sums. A `households` figure stays households until `figures.py` converts it with a rulebook multiplier it records — coercing an unknown unit to `people` understates by ~5×.
- The extracted rung is ranked by attributed AUTHORITY then recency, not by size — carried as `Candidate.preference_rank`, which `_best_on_rung` honours over its largest-wins default. Several bodies quoting one flood is not a reason to take the biggest number.
- A budget-capped cell has UNREAD documents, not silence — the cap must be recorded on the row, never allowed to look like "ReliefWeb had nothing".
- The rulebook names an extraction ROLE, never a model id; model ids live in `pythia/config.yaml`'s registry. The rulebook's credential guard only fires on STRING values, so a numeric `*_tokens` budget is config, not a secret.
- IPC states how many people are in Phase 3+, never WHY — so a deterioration is only drought impact where an independent indicator says the country was in drought. Without that gate the machine files conflict-driven food insecurity as drought.
- A covering IPC analysis with no baseline resolves NO_DATA, never its own Phase 3+ population: that figure is a STOCK, and publishing it as people-affected-this-month overstates by the entire pre-existing caseload.
- A drought-indicator feed is cached as VALUES, never verdicts — thresholds are applied at evaluation time, so retuning the rulebook changes behaviour with no re-fetch. A feed where 0 of N records resolve to a country has changed shape; refuse it, never cache "no drought anywhere".
- Under `month_attribution: analysis_window` the same delta repeats in every month the IPC window covers. These are stocks and must NEVER be summed across months; the row says so in its own provenance.
- The backcast runs the SAME functions a live run uses; only `run_type` differs. A backcast-specific detection or ladder path would let a rulebook change move the live answers without moving the base rates they are compared against, and nothing would fail.
- A base-rate denominator counts cells ASSESSED, not cells in the calendar — a country-month with no trigger row was never looked at, and counting it as quiet manufactures a low base rate out of an ingestion gap.
- The acceptance rate divides by cells assessed, never by rows written; a hazard with zero assessed cells is "not assessed", never 0%. An absence of evidence is not a failure to resolve.
- The DFO archive is calibration only: it writes to `haz_raw_dfo` and the report, never to `haz_triggers`/`haz_impact_candidates`/`haz_resolutions`, and its comparison window must not overlap `backcast.flood`.
- A resolution-machine sampling report must be seeded, never `ORDER BY random()` — a page that changes on every run cannot be cited in a review.
- Every rule the base-rate prompt block states is GENERATED from `rulebook.yaml` at prompt-build time, never written into the prompt text. A prompt describing last quarter's thresholds is confidently wrong, which is worse than one describing none.
- The machine's base rates may anchor `PA` questions on its three hazards and nothing else — `PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED` is a stock where the machine's drought value is the monthly increase in it, and `EVENT_OCCURRENCE` resolves from GDACS alert levels, not from this machine's detection.
- "Assessed and quiet" and "never assessed" get different renderings: occurrence rows with no severity row say "no historical events in record"; no rows at all get NO block. Claiming an empty record for a cell nothing ever looked at states a fact about the world out of an ingestion gap.
- A prompt block over its declared `max_chars` budget is logged and fails a test, never truncated — a base rate cut off mid-table still reads as a complete one.
- An extraction cache entry answers ONE cell's question: the key carries (iso3, hazard, month) because the prompt does, and an error row is never a cache hit — a failed call is retried on the next run, never inherited forever.
- Only calls that billed tokens consume the extraction budget — a call that never reached the provider (missing key, breaker cooldown) spent nothing and must cost nothing.
- Every paid machine call writes a rich `llm_calls` row (`phase='hazard_extraction'` → the `resolution` bucket) — spend that exists only in `haz_doc_extractions` is invisible to every cost view.
- One bad cell must not end the month, and one bad month must not end the run — failures are recorded per cell, surfaced in the exit code, and retried by the next run.
- Severity base rates admit FROZEN values only — a provisional row is still revisable, and a quantile that moves when a live month is revised is not a base rate.
- A canonical-DB producer that resumes its own work must include itself in its discovery candidates — otherwise every run silently starts from an older producer's artifact and discards its own progress.

**Interpreter report**
- "Worsening" is a claim about DIRECTION, so it must be ranked on the signed `log_ev_ratio`, never on `js_vs_baserate` — the divergence score is undirected and would promote a forecast for sitting far BELOW its base rate.
- A forecast that clears the worsening threshold is never relabelled "roughly stable" because a cap crowded it out; it is left out of both sections.
- The PACK decides which forecasts the report covers and under which heading; the model copies that decision and the validator holds it to the list. A report that promotes an entry is making a claim on the model's authority, not the system's.
- A question record budget is spent in REPORT order, never attention order — otherwise a row the report must cover loses its record to a question the report never mentions, and any covered row that still loses one is named rather than counted.
- The report prints names, never codes, and figures as sentences: no "% of maximum", no "1/87.5x". Both were shipped and both confused readers.
- The printed map is drawn by `interpreter/mapviz.py` from the same table, preference order and scaling as `/v1/interpreter/attention_map` — the dashboard's choropleth is JavaScript and a PDF cannot run it, so the two must be kept honest by construction, not by hand.

**Release publishing**
- The published DB is built by `scripts/ci/build_release_db.py`, never uploaded raw — it strips `haz_raw_*` and compacts, and its non-zero exit is the guard that stops a publish BEFORE anything is clobbered.
- Never let a `--clobber` upload be the first thing that discovers an asset is unpublishable: validate size before the destructive step, because `gh release upload --clobber` deletes the old asset first.
- Only the API's own consumers belong in the published DB — the canonical ARTIFACT is the system of record, the release is a serving copy.

**CI wiring**
- A lint that guards directory A must run when directory A changes — check the path filters of the workflow that owns the test, not just where the test file sits.
- When adding a test under `resolver/tests/` that imports another package, wire it into that package's workflow in the same PR — `test_ci_shell_sanity.py` will fail the PR otherwise. Known blind spot: nothing runs `pythia/tests/` broadly, so 8 of the 9 `pythia/tests/test_api_*.py` files run in no workflow.
- CI runs explicit test-file lists — **a new test file runs in NO workflow until it is named in one.**
- A red publish run does not mean the release wasn't updated — check the release asset timestamp before re-dispatching; and any workflow step that `python -m`-imports repo modules needs a checkout, unlike the inline-heredoc steps around it.

## Post-Edit Documentation Requirements

After making any code changes, evaluate whether the following files need updating and update them if so:

- **CLAUDE.md** – Update if you've changed architecture, environment variables, failure modes, component behavior, workflow steps, or database schema. This is the authoritative reference for future Claude Code sessions.
- **README.md** – Update if you've changed setup steps, dependencies, usage instructions, file structure, or how to run the project.
- **docs/fred_overview.md** – This is a plain-English description of the system for non-technical readers. Update it if you've changed what the system does, how it behaves, its inputs/outputs, or its overall logic. Avoid technical jargon; explain changes in terms of what the system now does differently from a user perspective. Assume readers understand forecasting and humanitarian data, but not code.

Do not update these files for trivial changes (e.g. formatting, minor refactors with no behavioral change). Use your judgment.

### How to update CLAUDE.md (so it stays usable)

CLAUDE.md is loaded into every session, so its size is a real cost and its contradictions are
real bugs. Three rules, each learned from what actually went wrong in this file:

1. **When a fix supersedes existing prose, EDIT THAT PROSE.** Appending a new "Known failure
   modes" entry is not a substitute — that is exactly how this file came to assert both the old
   and the new behaviour of the batch poller in three different places, while a reader who
   consulted only the reference section acted on refuted facts.
2. **Every new failure-mode entry's `**Rule:**` line also goes in `## Invariants`.** The rules
   are the durable part; the post-mortem is the evidence for them.
3. **When an entry's subsystem is rewritten, move the entry** to
   `docs/archive/claude_md_failure_modes.md` (verbatim, original order) and leave its rule in
   Invariants. Prefer archiving over deleting — the diagnosis is often still the fastest route
   to a regression.

**Core pipeline:**
```
Resolver facts/base rates -> Horizon Scanner per-hazard pipeline (RC grounding → RC → triage grounding → triage)
  -> Structured data connectors (ReliefWeb, ACAPS, IPC, ACLED political, ENSO, seasonal TC, HDX Signals, ACLED CAST, GDACS, GDACS event history, FEWS NET IPC)
  -> Adversarial checks (RC L1+) -> Forecaster SPD ensemble
  -> Sibyl parallel track (top-10 volatile questions, agentic web research)
  -> DuckDB -> FastAPI API -> Next.js Dashboard + CSV/Excel exports
```

**Post-forecast pipeline:**
```
compute_resolutions (ground truth from Resolver) -> resolutions table
  -> compute_scores (Brier, log loss, CRPS per horizon) -> scores table
  -> compute_calibration (weights per hazard/metric) -> calibration_weights
  -> generate_calibration_advice (per-hazard/metric prompt guidance) -> calibration_advice
```

## Repository layout

```
Pythia/
  horizon_scanner/     # Country/hazard triage via LLM (per-hazard RC + triage, reliefweb)
    enso/              #   ENSO state/forecast scraper (IRI/CPC Quick Look)
    seasonal_tc/       #   Seasonal TC forecast scrapers (TSR, NOAA CPC, BoM) + country-basin mapping
    rc_prompts.py      #   Per-hazard RC prompt builders (ACE, DR, FL, TC; an HW builder exists but HW is blocked)
    hs_triage_prompts.py  # Per-hazard triage prompt builders
    rc_grounding_prompts.py  # RC-specific grounding queries
    hs_triage_grounding_prompts.py  # Triage-specific grounding queries
    hdx_signals.py     #   HDX Signals (OCHA automated crisis monitoring) connector
    conflict_forecasts.py  # Unified conflict forecast loader (VIEWS + CF.org + ACLED CAST)
  forecaster/          # LLM ensemble for SPD forecasts (structured data + SPD phases)
  sibyl/               # Parallel deep-research forecasting track (agentic open-web research)
  resolver/            # Fact ingestion, resolution, and ground truth DB
    connectors/        #   Connector protocol + registry (ACLED, IDMC, IFRC Montandon, GDACS, FEWS NET IPC, IPC API) + forecast registry (VIEWS, CF.org, ACLED CAST)
    hazard_resolution/ #   PA resolution machine (rulebook.yaml, haz_* schema, population loader; Phase 1: cyclone detection + zeros — IBTrACS, ReliefWeb silence sweep, vendored NE boundaries; Phase 2: GDACS flood detection + the impact ladder — EM-DAT/IFRC GO/IDMC IDU connectors, deterministic reconciler; Phase 3: ladder rung 2 — ReliefWeb document selection, LLM figure transcription, deterministic post-processing; Phase 4: drought — IPC Phase 3+ delta gated by drought indicators, no ladder and no LLM; Phase 5: backcast + base rates + DFO cross-check + acceptance report; Phase 6: the base-rate block injected into PA forecast prompts)
    ingestion/         #   Source-specific fetch/normalise clients (acled_client, idmc/)
    transform/         #   Adapters, normalisation, source resolution
    tools/             #   Pipeline orchestrator (run_pipeline.py), precedence, deltas, enrichment
    db/                #   DuckDB schema + helpers
  pythia/
    api/               # FastAPI service (50+ endpoints)
    db/                # DuckDB schema definitions + migrations (schema.py is authoritative)
    tools/             # Post-forecast compute scripts (resolutions, scores, calibration, calibration advice)
    web_research/      # Shared retriever backends (Brave [primary], OpenAI, Gemini, Claude). NB: the Exa/Perplexity
                       #   branches in web_research.py are unimplemented stubs returning `backend_unimplemented`
    prediction_markets/  # Prediction market signal retriever (Metaculus, Polymarket, Manifold)
    acaps.py           # ACAPS unified connector (INFORM, Risk Radar, Daily Monitoring, Access)
    acled_political.py # ACLED event-level political data connector
    adversarial_check.py # Counter-evidence checks for RC Level 1+ cases
    ipc_phases.py      # IPC food security phase connector (legacy, requires API key — currently unused)
    food_security.py   # Unified food security loader (FEWS NET primary, IPC API fallback)
    fewsnet_food_security.py  # Backward-compat shim → food_security.py
    market_snapshot.py # Manifold prediction market snapshot utility
    tests/             # Pythia-specific tests
  interpreter/         # Plain-language report generator (Opus 5 explains, never calculates; Phases 0-6 built and fully wired — see docs/interpreter.md)
  web/                 # Next.js 14 dashboard (TypeScript, Tailwind)
  tests/               # Cross-module integration tests
  docs/                # Component documentation
  scripts/             # Operational scripts
  .github/workflows/   # CI + scheduled pipelines
```

## Key files

- `pythia/db/schema.py` — Authoritative Pythia DuckDB schema (tables, migrations, bucket defs)
- `resolver/db/schema.sql` — Resolver DuckDB schema (facts_resolved, facts_deltas, snapshots)
- `pythia/buckets.py` — SPD bucket definitions (PA, FATALITIES, PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED) and the **`NUM_HORIZONS = 6` forecast-window constant** (single-sourced; imported by `compute_resolutions.py` and by `forecaster/cli.py`'s month-label window checks — do not redefine locally). `BUCKET_SPECS` maps metric names to `BucketSpec` tuples. **Every SPD metric leads with a dedicated "0" bucket** (exactly zero impact, centroid 0, range [0,1)) so the next bucket starts at 1. PA has 6 buckets (0, 1-<10k, 10k-<50k, 50k-<250k, 250k-<500k, >=500k), FATALITIES has 7 (0, 1-<5, 5-<25, 25-<100, 100-<500, 500-<1000, >=1000), DR_PHASE3_BUCKETS has 6 for IPC Phase 3+ population (0, 1-<100k, 100k-<1M, 1M-<5M, 5M-<15M, >=15M). Helpers: `n_buckets_for`, `max_bucket_count` (=7, drives the wide SPD_1..SPD_7 CSV export), `centroids_for`, `label_index_map(lowercase=...)`, and `bucket_schema_version(metric)` (12-hex digest over the ordered bucket BOUNDARIES; stamps `bucket_centroids.schema_version` so EMA centroids learned under an old boundary layout are refused by `compute_scores._load_centroids` — NULL rows grandfathered; retuning centroid seeds/labels does NOT change the version, changing any boundary does) alongside the existing `thresholds_for`/`interior_thresholds_for`/`labels_for`.
- `pythia/config.yaml` — Central configuration (LLM profiles, hazards, bucket specs)
- `pythia/llm_batch.py` — Provider Batch-API execution layer (OpenAI/Anthropic/Gemini adapters, enqueue/submit/poll/collect/replay; see the Batch-API staged pipeline section)
- `horizon_scanner/hs_batch.py` — Staged-HS stage state + batch seam (family modes, enqueue/replay, grounding-pack reload, hs_stage_state carry, run-id/pipeline resolution)
- `scripts/ci/emit_batch_state.py` / `scripts/ci/poll_llm_batches.py` — Batch-state artifact emitter (submit stages; also records the stage's effective run-shaping settings as `dispatch_inputs`) + the 15-min poller that dispatches the next pipeline stage (and re-supplies those inputs, so smoke-run scoping survives the hand-off)
- `scripts/compare_prompt_order_runs.py` — A/B harness for the V3 prompt-order validation gate (test-mode run vs baseline: SPD JS-divergence, parse failures, RC/triage distributions)
- `horizon_scanner/horizon_scanner.py` — HS main entrypoint (~1700 LOC)
- `horizon_scanner/regime_change.py` — RC scoring (score = likelihood x magnitude, 4 levels)
- `horizon_scanner/regime_change_llm.py` — Per-hazard RC LLM pipeline (single pass by default since July 2026; `PYTHIA_HS_RC_PASSES=2` restores 2-pass, pair with a distinct `PYTHIA_RC_MODEL_PASS2` — two identical passes at temperature 0 are deterministic duplicates and the mean merge is a no-op)
- `horizon_scanner/triage.py` — Per-hazard triage LLM pipeline (single pass by default since July 2026, `PYTHIA_HS_TRIAGE_PASSES=2` restores 2-pass; gemini-3.5-flash, ACLED low-activity filter, RC-promoted skip for L1+ hazards)
- `horizon_scanner/rc_prompts.py` — Per-hazard RC prompt builders (ACE, DR, FL, TC with calibration anchors; HW builder retained as dead code)
- `horizon_scanner/hs_triage_prompts.py` — Per-hazard triage prompt builders (scoring anchors, RC context injection)
- `horizon_scanner/rc_grounding_prompts.py` — RC-specific grounding queries (TRIGGER/DAMPENER/BASELINE signals)
- `horizon_scanner/hs_triage_grounding_prompts.py` — Triage-specific grounding queries (SITUATION/RESPONSE/FORECAST/VULNERABILITY)
- `horizon_scanner/enso/enso_module.py` — ENSO state/forecast scraper (IRI/CPC, 7-day cache)
- `horizon_scanner/seasonal_tc/seasonal_tc_runner.py` — Seasonal TC forecast orchestrator (TSR + NOAA CPC + BoM + Météo-France La Réunion SWI + IMD/NIO climatology)
- `horizon_scanner/seasonal_tc/__init__.py` — Country-to-basin mapping + cached TC context reader
- `horizon_scanner/hdx_signals.py` — HDX Signals connector (OCHA automated crisis monitoring, indicator-to-hazard mapping)
- `horizon_scanner/conflict_forecasts.py` — Unified conflict forecast loader (VIEWS + conflictforecast.org + ACLED CAST)
- `forecaster/cli.py` — Forecaster main runner (the largest module in the repo, after the July 2026 decomposition). **Decomposed (pure code motion, zero behavior change)** into three sibling modules, with cli.py re-importing every moved name as a backward-compat re-export (`forecaster.cli.X` and `from forecaster.cli import X` still work; the mutable `_CALIB_WEIGHTS_CACHE`/`_CALIB_WEIGHTS_LOCK` keep object identity): `forecaster/month_utils.py` (month/window arithmetic: `_anchor_month_for_question`, `_month_index_for_label`, `_expected_months`, `_parse_month_key`, `_add_months`, `_sanitize_month_series`, etc.), `forecaster/history_loaders.py` (IFRC/IDMC/ACLED PA history loaders, `_load_idmc_conflict_flow_history_summary`, `_build_gdacs_event_history`, `_format_base_rate_for_prompt`, `_load_country_names`, `NATURAL_HAZARD_CODES`/`IDMC_HZ_MAP`), and `forecaster/aggregators.py` (calibration-weight loading/caching + `_pythia_db_url_from_config`/`_pythia_db_path_from_config`). **Intentionally NOT moved** because tests monkeypatch cli-module attributes that the callees resolve at call time (moving them would silently break the patches): the DB writers `_write_spd_outputs`/`_write_binary_outputs`/`_write_spd_members_v2_to_db` (tests patch `forecaster.cli.connect`; writers also read the module-global `_IS_TEST`), `_build_history_summary` + `_build_natural_hazard_seasonal_profile` + `_build_conflict_base_rate` (patch `cli.connect`), `_load_fewsnet_phase3_history` (patch `cli.duckdb` + `cli._pythia_db_path_from_config`), `_resolve_member_weights` (patch `cli._load_calibration_weights_db`), and `_build_bayesmc_spd_obj` (patch `cli.aggregate_spd_v2_bayesmc`) — do not move these without reworking those test seams. **Seam caveat for the MOVED loaders**: the three history loaders now in `history_loaders.py` resolve `duckdb`/`_pythia_db_path_from_config` in the `forecaster.history_loaders` namespace — a test written by analogy to `test_fewsnet_history.py` (which patches `forecaster.cli.*` and works only because `_load_fewsnet_phase3_history` stayed in cli) must patch `forecaster.history_loaders.*` instead, or it silently hits the real DB. `_forecast_month_keys_from_question` in `prompts.py` is a thin wrapper over `month_utils._anchor_month_for_question` + `_expected_months` (July 2026 follow-up audit; sibyl already used the same pair — one implementation of the month-anchoring invariant). Supports both SPD (per-metric bucket count × 6-month; 6 buckets for PA/PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED, 7 for FATALITIES) and binary (per-month probability) forecast pipelines. Binary questions (metric=EVENT_OCCURRENCE) are detected and routed to `_run_binary_forecast_for_question()`, which uses `build_binary_event_prompt()` for prompting and `parse_binary_response()` for output parsing. Binary storage convention: bucket_1 = P(yes), bucket_2 = P(no) = 1-P(yes), remaining buckets = 0 (EVENT_OCCURRENCE is not in BUCKET_SPECS; the binary writer is independent of the SPD bucket schemes). History builders: `_build_history_summary` dispatches to `_load_fewsnet_phase3_history` for DR/PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED (null-aware, coverage-tracked), `_build_natural_hazard_seasonal_profile` for natural hazard PA, `_build_conflict_base_rate` for **both** ACE metrics (PA and FATALITIES — see below), and `_build_gdacs_event_history` for FL/DR/TC event occurrence (binary 1/0 + alertlevel from facts_resolved). `_format_base_rate_for_prompt` handles `fewsnet_phase3` type with null-aware formatting showing "null" for FEWS NET analysis cycle gaps, and takes an optional `metric` that only affects `conflict_trajectory`: it leads with the series the question actually resolves against (PA → IDMC displacement, FATALITIES → ACLED) and marks it "THIS QUESTION'S SERIES"; **omitting `metric` renders byte-identically to the pre-July-2026 output**, so any caller that doesn't pass it is unchanged. **ACE/PA used to route to `_load_idmc_conflict_flow_history_summary`, whose dict carries no `type` — so the formatter fell through to its legacy ```json dump and ACE/PA was the one hazard/metric with no curated base-rate block; that loader also never excluded the current partial month.** It is retained only as a `forecaster.cli` re-export and is no longer on any prompt path. GDACS event history is loaded in `_load_structured_data()` for FL/DR/TC hazards and injected into both SPD prompts (via `_format_gdacs_event_history_for_prompt` in prompts.py) and binary prompts (via `gdacs_event_history` parameter on `build_binary_event_prompt`).
- `pythia/api/app.py` — FastAPI application assembly (thin, after the July 2026 decomposition). **The former ~5,450-line monolith was split (pure code motion)**: shared infrastructure lives in `pythia/api/core.py` (the cached read-connection singleton `_READ_CON`/`_ensure_read_connection`/`_maybe_refresh_db`/`_con`, `_execute`, `_test_filter`, table/bucket helpers, `_run_filter_cte`, `_validate_iso3_param`, heavy-request semaphore, `_stream_csv`), and endpoints live in route-group modules under `pythia/api/routes/` (`questions.py`, `performance.py` [calibration+performance], `forecasts.py`, `risk_index.py`, `diagnostics.py` [diagnostics + HS debug], `resolver_explorer.py`, `downloads.py`, `costs.py`, `sibyl.py`, `interpreter.py` [interpreter reports + attention map, Phase 5 Aug 2026]), each exposing `router = APIRouter()`. app.py keeps app creation/CORS, the startup hook, `include_router` calls (original registration order preserved), and the routes entangled with connection state or test patch seams (`/v1/admin/force_sync`, `/v1/health`, `/v1/version`, `/v1/run`, `/v1/ui_runs/{id}`, `/v1/countries`). `/v1/run` is gated by `PYTHIA_ALLOW_INPROCESS_RUN` (default off → 503) and imports `pythia.pipeline.run` lazily inside the handler — the module-scope import would pull the full HS tree into the API process. **Back-compat**: app.py re-exports every moved name, and forwards the rebindable state names (`_READ_CON`, `_READ_CON_MTIME`, `_LAST_SYNC_CHECK`, `_POPULATION_BY_ISO3`, `_COUNTRY_NAME_BY_ISO3`) to core via module-class properties — so legacy seams like `pythia.api.app._READ_CON = None` (used by several tests) still reset the real singleton in core. Route modules must never import app.py (core's `_run_filter_cte` resolves `_table_has_columns` late through `pythia.api.app` when loaded, keeping the monkeypatch seam in `pythia/tests/test_api_input_validation.py` working). **Connection retry-once resilience**: `core._execute` catches connection-level DuckDB errors only (`duckdb.ConnectionException`, `duckdb.FatalException`, or generic errors whose message signals an invalidated/closed connection — never CatalogException/BinderException/etc.), closes the cached connection via `_reopen_read_connection()`, reopens from the same DB path, and retries exactly once (tests: `pythia/tests/test_api_connection_retry.py`). Phase 4 additions: `_get_risk_index_binary()` serves EVENT_OCCURRENCE probabilities (bucket_1 = P(event)), `/v1/diagnostics/resolution_rates` endpoint returns resolution rate by (hazard, metric). Risk index supports EVENT_OCCURRENCE (binary), PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED (SPD), PA, and FATALITIES metrics. KPI scopes accept all four metric scopes. **Resolver accordion endpoints**: `/v1/resolver/source_inventory` returns per-source metadata (label, category, last_updated, row counts); `/v1/resolver/source_data` returns rows for a specific source with curated or all columns. Source registry (`_SOURCE_REGISTRY`) maps ~23 source keys to tables, filters, curated columns. `facts_resolved` sources filtered by `publisher` column (IFRC, IDMC, ACLED, GDACS / JRC, FEWS NET). Last-updated dates use ingestion-time columns (`created_at`, `fetched_at`) not data-period columns. **`/v1/version`** computes staleness fields from the synced DB (cached per `(DB-mtime, include_test)` via `_staleness_probes()` — the probes are full-column MAX scans and the dashboard homepage calls this endpoint on every page view; the cache invalidates when the sync layer's `os.replace` advances the mtime): `latest_forecast_month`/`latest_forecast_run_id` plus `latest_scores_at`, `latest_calibration_at`, and `latest_data_at` (= `MAX(created_at)` across `latest_hs_created_at`/`forecasts_ensemble`/`resolutions`/`scores`/`calibration_weights`/`calibration_advice`, excluding the manifest's publish-time `created_utc`). **Test-aware since 2026-07-15**: the endpoint accepts `include_test` (default false → probes exclude `is_test` rows, and a dedicated hs_runs probe overrides the manifest's unfiltered `latest_hs_run_id`/`latest_hs_created_at` with the latest production run; the homepage passes the Test toggle through). The dashboard homepage "Last updated" renders `latest_data_at` so the scoring/calibration loop counts as a data refresh even when it creates no new HS run; the HS-run date is shown separately as "Latest forecast scan".
- `web/src/app/questions/[questionId]/BinaryPanel.tsx` — Binary event forecast display: per-month probability bars, resolution outcomes (event/no event), per-horizon Brier scores.
- `pythia/tools/compute_resolutions.py` — Resolves forecasts against Resolver ground truth. Supports PA, FATALITIES, EVENT_OCCURRENCE, and PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED metrics. Source-aware null handling: FATALITIES+ACE/ACO and EVENT_OCCURRENCE default to 0 when no data; PA and PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED leave horizons unresolved (null) when no data exists. FEWS NET IPC resolution via `_try_fewsnet_ipc()` queries only `phase3plus_in_need` metric (not projections), guarded to DR hazard only.
- `pythia/tools/compute_bucket_centroids.py` — Computes data-driven bucket centroids from historical facts_resolved. Metric-aware SQL filter: PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED queries `phase3plus_in_need`, PA queries `affected/people_affected/pa/displaced`, FATALITIES queries `fatalities`.
- `pythia/tools/compute_scores.py` — Brier/log/CRPS scoring per horizon
- `pythia/tools/score_views.py` — **External benchmark scoring (undocumented until 2026-08-03).** Converts the ingested VIEWS point forecasts into synthetic FATALITIES-bucket SPDs (bucket count derived from `pythia/buckets.py`) and scores them with the same Brier/log/CRPS functions as `compute_scores.py`, writing rows under `model_name='__ext_views'`. Runs every cycle in `compute_scores.yml` (right after compute_scores) and surfaces on the Performance page as "ViEWS (external benchmark)" via the `__ext_` prefix convention in `web/src/app/performance/PerformancePanel.tsx`; `generate_calibration_advice.py` also reads it. `AGGREGATE_MODEL_NAMES`-style exclusion is NOT what keeps it out of the calibration softmax — it is excluded because weights are computed over ensemble members only. The `__ext_` prefix is the extension point for any future external benchmark.
- `pythia/tools/base_rate_spd.py` — **Base-rate anchor as a bucket distribution (interpreter Phase 1, Aug 2026).** `base_rate_spd(con, iso3, hazard_code, metric, as_of) -> (probs, source, detail)` reconstructs the base rate the forecaster was shown at prompt time as a probability distribution over the metric's buckets, routing by (hazard, metric) exactly as `forecaster/cli.py::_build_history_summary` does (ACE/FATALITIES → `acled_monthly_fatalities`; ACE/PA → IDMC flow rows in `facts_deltas`; DR/PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED → `phase3plus_in_need` 36m; FL/TC PA → GDACS occurrence × reported-PA severity mixture; FL/DR/TC EVENT_OCCURRENCE → binary `[p, 1-p]` with per-month rates in `detail.probs_by_month`). Only history STRICTLY BEFORE `as_of` (the question's window_start month) is used — no outcome leakage — and empirical counts get a Jeffreys 0.5 pseudo-count so no bucket is ever exactly zero. Pairs with no prompt-time base rate (CU/PA, DI/PA, unknown) return `([], "NONE", reason)`; **callers must treat that as "no anchor", never invent one**. Severity uses `_pa_metric_in_clause` semantics (never `in_need` — the montandon class of bug). Tests: `pythia/tests/test_base_rate_spd.py`.
- `pythia/tools/compute_deviation.py` — Writes `forecast_deviation`: one row per (run, question, aggregate model ∈ {ensemble_mean_v2, ensemble_bayesmc_v2, track2_flash, sibyl}) with `js_vs_baserate` (JSD, reuses `_js_divergence` from generate_calibration_advice — the repo's single implementation), `log_ev_ratio` (signed, `ln(EV_f/EV_b)`), `eiv_nominal` (surge blend `max + 0.1*(sum−max)`, matching `/v1/risk_index`'s alpha; `centroids_for` seeds), `eiv_per_100k` (population from `resolver/data/population.csv`), `baserate_source`/`baserate_json` provenance, `score_family` (binary rows have NULL eiv fields). A question with no base-rate anchor gets NO row. Idempotent per run (delete-then-insert). Runs `continue-on-error` in `pythia_pipeline_stage.yml` fc_collect_finalize (after forecaster collect, scoped to `FORECASTER_RUN_ID`) and in `run_sibyl.yml` after Sibyl (unscoped recompute, which also covers the legacy sync pipeline that Sibyl chains off). Tests: `pythia/tests/test_compute_deviation.py`.
- `pythia/tools/score_baselines.py` — Reference forecasters `__ext_climatology` (the `base_rate_spd` anchor — "what you would have said with no model") and `__ext_uniform` (flat; 0.5 for binary), modeled on `score_views.py`: same scoring functions, `run_id IS NULL`, delete-then-insert, `is_test` inherited from the question, audit table `baseline_scored_forecasts`. Binary questions get binary Brier only — score families never blended. Skill (computed downstream, never stored) = `1 − fred_score/climatology_score` per (score_family, score_type). Runs in `compute_scores.yml` right after `score_views` (`continue-on-error`). Persistence is deliberately NOT implemented (one-hot → infinite log loss; smoothing is an arbitrary-parameter argument). Both sentinels are excluded from the calibration softmax by the existing `NOT LIKE '__ext_%'` filter and render generically as "climatology (external)"/"uniform (external)" on the Performance page. Tests: `pythia/tests/test_score_baselines.py`. **All three Phase-1 test files are named explicitly in forecaster-ci's "interpreter Phase 1" step** (nothing runs `pythia/tests/` broadly).
- `interpreter/run.py` — **Interpreter runner (Phase 3, Aug 2026; wired into production by Phase 6 — `run_sibyl.yml` runs `--kind combined` after the current-run bundle and BEFORE the canonical upload + publish dispatch, `compute_calibration_pythia.yml` runs `--kind scored` after the scored-bundle step; both `continue-on-error`, both with `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`).** `python -m interpreter.run --db ... --kind {current,scored,combined} --pack <bundle zip>`: loads a Phase 2 bundle (`interpreter/packs.py`), assembles versioned templates (`interpreter/templates/v2/`, `prompts.py` — v2 carries the Orwell house-style rules, the banned-word list and the em-dash ban), calls Opus 5 once (role `interpreter`, `ModelSpec.thinking` → `output_config.effort`, default `high`, 32k output ceiling, temperature never sent, `log_call=False` + a rich `llm_calls` row with `phase='interpreter'`), runs the validator (`interpreter/validate.py` — six separately-reported checks: jsonschema shape incl. kind-conditional requirements; referential — every cited question_id exists in the pack and every figure reference resolves; numeric — referenced figures re-derived with INDEPENDENT SQL from `forecast_deviation`/`hs_triage` and compared at rtol 1e-6, skipped-with-reason when no DB/tables; prose — bare-numeral lint with a calendar whitelist, lexicon band agreement with longest-first consumed phrase matching and ambiguous sentences skipped, per-field length cap; **style** — em/en dashes, banned phrases, "not X, but Y", and bare ISO3/metric codes in prose; **categories** — the pack decides which forecasts the report covers and under which heading, and the model may not promote, demote, invent or drop an entry (skipped when the pack has no categorised rows); a crashed check is a failed check; `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_STRICT_VALIDATION=1` suppresses the publication artifacts while the row still stores), renders markdown with every `{{fig:...}}` placeholder substituted from pack-derived figure maps (`render.py` — the model's own numbers are never printed; unresolved refs render `[figure unavailable]` and are counted), and stores a versioned `interpretations` row (`store.py`, is_test None-and-inherit; `--force` appends a new version, default skips when an `ok` row exists; failures store as `failed_generation`/`failed_validation` and remain inspectable; `main()` always returns 0). The combined kind folds in the latest stored `scored` interpretation — its `figures_json` is persisted precisely so combined rendering never needs the scored pack. Model seam for tests: `interpreter.run._call_model`. The probability lexicon (`lexicon.py`) is fixed IPCC-style bands, printed in the report appendix. **Test-mode derivation (Phase 6, the Sibyl pattern)**: the runner probes `hs_runs.is_test` for the interpreted run and exports `PYTHIA_TEST_MODE=1` when it's a test run (the workflow step can't carry the upstream test_mode input) — an explicit env always wins, and the derivation only ever turns the flag ON. Full doc: `docs/interpreter.md`. Tests: `interpreter/tests/` (model mocked, temp DuckDB), run by `interpreter-ci.yml`.
- `interpreter/selection.py` + `interpreter/names.py` + `interpreter/charts.py` + `interpreter/mapviz.py` — **What the report covers, what it calls things, and its pictures (Aug 2026).** `selection.py` stamps `category` + `category_rank` onto the attention rows and is a pure function over plain dicts (no DB, no IO): four boxes — worsening/stable_major × climate(DR,FL,TC)/conflict(ACE,ACO) — with a **dual gate** per box (`min_per_category`=3 always shown so a section is never empty and always names the month's worst, plus anything at or above `worsening_multiple`=2.0× the historical expectation, capped at `max_per_category`=6). Ranking is on the **signed `log_ev_ratio`** with a ±5% deadband; `js_vs_baserate` is undirected and would promote a forecast for sitting far BELOW its base rate. `select_stable_major` excludes rows that clear the worsening threshold, so a crowded-out entry is left out of BOTH sections rather than mislabelled. `names.py` maps ISO3 (from `resolver/data/countries.csv`, `utf-8-sig` for the BOM) / hazard / metric codes to words and is the SINGLE naming implementation — the markdown, the PDF and the API's `_name_attention_entries` all call it, so the dashboard carries no second copy of the country table. `charts.py` draws a forecast distribution as inline SVG in Fred's palette (drawn from the PACK, never from model output — a chart that disagreed with the numbers beside it would be worse than none). `mapviz.py` is the printed attention map (see `pdf.py`). Tests: `interpreter/tests/test_selection_names_charts.py`.
- `interpreter/pdf.py` — **PDF renderer (Phase 6, Aug 2026).** `python -m interpreter.pdf --db ... --kind combined --out-dir interpreter_out`: reads the newest `interpretations` row (test-filtered by default; `--interpretation-id` pins one), converts `content_md` to self-contained HTML with a small deterministic subset converter (only what `render_markdown` emits; inline-code spans protect underscored question ids from emphasis parsing), and renders via **WeasyPrint** (lazy import; test seam `interpreter.pdf._render_pdf`). The page carries Fred's masthead and palette, an inline SVG of each entry's forecast distribution (`interpreter/charts.py`, drawn from the PACK not from model output), absolute question links (`PYTHIA_PUBLIC_BASE_URL`), and the **attention map** — `interpreter/mapviz.py` projects the vendored Natural Earth boundaries the PA machine ships and fills countries from `forecast_deviation` with the SAME table/preference/ln2 scaling as `/v1/interpreter/attention_map`, so the printed and dashboard maps cannot disagree (drawn here because the dashboard's choropleth is JavaScript and a PDF cannot run JavaScript; coordinates rounded to the pixel and duplicates dropped, or the 1:50m source would put megabytes into every PDF; a missing boundary file degrades to no map, never a failed render). Writes `report__{YYYY-MM}__v{n}.pdf` + the constant-name `interpreter_report_latest.pdf`. A render failure keeps the HTML as evidence and exits 0; a non-ok row renders behind a validation banner unless `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_STRICT_VALIDATION=1` suppresses the PDF (row stays stored); `main()` always returns 0. Runs in `run_sibyl.yml` right after the combined report (apt pango top-up + `pip install weasyprint`, `continue-on-error`), uploads as the **`pythia-interpreter-report`** artifact. `publish_latest_data.yml` fetches that artifact best-effort from the source run (the calibration-chain publish has none — expected; the release keeps the previous PDF), uploads the PDFs to the `pythia-data-latest` release with `--clobber`, and stamps `interpreter_report_asset`/`interpreter_report_url`/`interpreter_report_versioned_asset` into `manifest.json` — reflecting what is downloadable AFTER the publish (fresh or carried over). `/v1/version` passes manifest keys through (`result = dict(manifest)`), which is where the dashboard's Download PDF button reads the URL. A backfilled report is named for the month it is ABOUT — `month_label` parses `hs_run_id` and only falls back to `created_at` — so July's report generated in August is not filed as August's. Tests: `interpreter/tests/test_pdf.py`.
- `.github/workflows/interpreter_backfill.yml` — **Manual interpreter backfill/recovery (Aug 2026).** Runs the interpreter over already-completed forecast runs: `compute_deviation --run-id` + `build_current_run_bundle --run-id` + `interpreter.run` + `interpreter.pdf`, per run, nothing special-cased (it rebuilds exactly what the live path would have had). Inputs: `run_ids` (blank = latest production run), `kind`, `force`, `include_test`, `db_run_id`, `upload_canonical` (default true — without it the rows die with the runner), `publish` (default **false**), `force_during_pipeline`. Gated by `check_pipeline_active` and in the `pythia-resolver-db` concurrency group (a run landing mid-pipeline would write rows the pipeline's final canonical upload discards). **It re-uploads the canonical DB, so it is a canonical-DB producer: named in all three discovery lists and self-included via `extra-workflows`** (otherwise a second backfill starts from a DB predating the first). Per-run PDFs go to the `pythia-interpreter-backfill` diagnostics artifact; one top-level render of the newest row goes to `pythia-interpreter-report` so the publish step finds exactly one `interpreter_report_latest.pdf`.
- `pythia/api/routes/interpreter.py` + `web/src/app/interpreter/` — **Interpreter API + dashboard (Phase 5, Aug 2026).** Endpoints: `/v1/interpreter/latest` (highest version of the newest run per kind; `has_interpretation:false` on a pre-interpreter DB, never a 500), `/versions`, `/{interpretation_id}` (declared LAST so the literal routes win), `/attention_map` (per-ISO3 max `js_vs_baserate`/ln2 from `forecast_deviation` via the bayesmc→mean→track2 preference; every table test-filtered). **Placeholders are resolved SERVER-side** via `interpreter/render.py::resolve_content` (`content_resolved` beside the raw `content`) so figure formatting has exactly one implementation, and `_name_attention_entries` stamps `country_name`/`hazard_name`/`metric_name` on each entry from `interpreter/names.py` — the alternative was a second copy of the country table in TypeScript. Frontend: `/interpreter` (Nav "Fred's Monthly Risk Report") renders `content_resolved` grouped into the report's **four named sections** (`groupAttention`/`sectionHeading`/`entryHeading` in `lib.ts` mirror `interpreter/selection.py`'s SECTION_ORDER; anything the model failed to place lands under "Other situations of note" rather than disappearing) with full names in every heading, question links, a version selector, visible warn/error banners for non-ok statuses, an explicit no-report empty state, a **run selector defaulting to the most recent run** (`groupVersionsByRun` in `web/src/app/interpreter/lib.ts`, labelled by the month the report is ABOUT via `runMonthLabel`, which parses `hs_run_id` exactly as `pdf.py::month_label` does; the per-run version selector shows only when a run has >1 version), the shared glossary, and the **attention map** — `RiskIndexMap` gained optional props (`valuesByIso3`/`colorFor`/`valueLabelFor`/`legendItems`/`onCountryClick`/`showRcOverlay`, all optional so existing call sites are byte-untouched) coloured by attention with click-to-scroll to the country's report section; `/interpreter/print` is the print/PDF fallback (no nav, page breaks) — the CI PDF renderer (`interpreter/pdf.py`) deliberately does NOT load this page (that would need a running Next.js + API in CI; it builds equivalent HTML from the stored row instead), and the page gained a **Download PDF** button linking to the release asset via `/v1/version`'s `interpreter_report_url` (hidden when no asset exists). **When the release advertises a report PDF but the API serves no report, the page says "still syncing" rather than the no-report empty state** — the API's DB lagging the release it just read the manifest from is a soft-fail, and a soft-fail must never look like "no data". **Navigation is one dropdown at every breakpoint**, driven by the single `NAV_LINKS` array in `web/src/components/Nav.tsx` (it was previously an inline desktop row plus a separate mobile list — two hand-maintained copies that could drift); the interpreter entry is labelled "Fred's Monthly Risk Report". `web/scripts/ci/ui_guardrails.py`'s /about check accepts both `href="/about"` and the data form `href: "/about"`. Score/deviation tooltip copy is single-sourced in `web/src/lib/score_glossary.ts` (PerformancePanel imports the same constants — the AboutScores drift rule). Tests: `pythia/tests/test_api_interpreter.py` (named in interpreter-ci — nothing runs `pythia/tests/` broadly) + `web/src/app/interpreter/__tests__/lib.test.ts` (web-ci vitest).
- `docs/interpreter_phase0_discovery.md` — Phase 0 discovery report for the interpreter module (base-rate coverage per pair, the unwired Anthropic thinking knob and the `output_config.effort` change needed, the map that was never actually deleted → extend `RiskIndexMap` via optional props, the release-asset mechanism for the future PDF, and the calibration-advice/AboutScores reuse map). Read it before building interpreter Phases 2–6.
- `pythia/tools/_db_utils.py` — Shared rollback-safe DuckDB helpers (`rollback_quietly`, `table_exists`, `row_count`, `column_exists`, `add_column_if_missing`) imported by all four post-forecast tools. Do not re-implement these locally — the DuckDB ≥1.5 aborted-transaction ROLLBACK scrub lives here.
- `pythia/tools/source_coverage.py` — Rebuilds the `source_coverage` table (PK: metric, iso3, ym) from the metric source tables at the start of every `compute_resolutions` run; `months_with_source_data` / `countries_with_source_data` readers gate zero-defaulting (month gate for FATALITIES + EVENT_OCCURRENCE; country-universe gate for FATALITIES only — EVENT_OCCURRENCE is deliberately exempt because GDACS is satellite-global). Extend `_COVERAGE_QUERIES` together with compute_resolutions' zero-default rules.
- `docs/audit_2026-07.md` — July 2026 full-codebase audit: what was fixed, deferred recommendations, and investigated-but-NOT-bugs (check it before re-reporting a previously-assessed issue). The deferred S4 items (decompositions, source_coverage, centroid versioning, precedence consolidation, test gaps) were implemented in the S4 follow-up PR. A **follow-up audit section (2026-07-07)** covers the post-audit PRs #780–#783 (Sibyl, API memory, model registry, lineup): fixes FU1–FU9, its own not-bugs list, and carried-forward deferred items. A **post-batch-pipeline audit section (2026-07-28)** covers PRs #808–#812 (batch-state pipeline scoping, cost-accounting integrity, the prompt-cache wiring, reporting fixes) with its own not-bugs and deferred lists — check ALL sections before re-reporting.
- `docs/audit_2026-08_pa_machine.md` — August 2026 full-integration audit of the PA resolution machine PRs #843–#849: what was unwired, the extraction-path bugs fixed before first production run, and the deliberately deferred items (the shadow-mode resolution-bridge flip above all). Check it before re-reporting a machine issue.
- `docs/montandon_assessment.md` — Assessment of IFRC Montandon/GO as a PA resolution source (Aug 2026). Explains why PA resolves at ~4%/2%/0% (FL/ACE/DR), and records the naming trap: the `ifrc_montandon` connector talks to the **IFRC GO operational API** (`goadmin.ifrc.org/api/v2/`), not Montandon's STAC Global Crisis Data Bank — which we have never evaluated. §3 is the improvement list to send IFRC; §4 is what is ours to fix. Read it before changing anything on the PA resolution path.
- `pythia/tools/compute_calibration_pythia.py` — Calibration weights + LLM advice
- `resolver/hazard_resolution/` — **PA resolution machine (Phase 0 scaffold + Phase 1 cyclone path + Phase 2 flood path & impact ladder + Phase 3 ReliefWeb LLM extraction + Phase 4 drought via IPC + Phase 5 backcast/base rates/acceptance + Phase 6 prompt integration, Aug 2026; see the dedicated section below).** Replaces the ~4%-coverage IFRC-GO-only PA resolution path with a two-layer machine: Layer 1 detection (IBTrACS/GDACS/IPC — did a qualifying hazard occur?), Layer 2 impact (fixed ladder EM-DAT → ReliefWeb LLM-extracted → IFRC GO → IDMC IDU lower bound). Target ≥80% of country-month-hazard cells resolved to a number or credible zero. **Hard rules**: every resolution carries provenance (source, record ids/URLs, retrieval timestamp, rule fired); reconciliation is deterministic (no LLM calls); LLM extraction transcribes stated figures only; resolutions freeze at month-end + `freeze_days` and are never reopened (revisions audit to `haz_revisions`); GDACS is detection + plausibility ceiling only, never a resolution value; all thresholds live in `rulebook.yaml` (never hard-code one — the loader validates strictly and rejects credential-looking keys, since API keys are env-only: `EMDAT_API_KEY`, `IDMC_API_KEY`, `RELIEFWEB_APPNAME`, existing `IPC_API_KEY`). Files: `rulebook.yaml` (single source of truth for behaviour), `rulebook.py` (load/validate, dotted `rb.get("cyclone.buffer_km")`), `rules.py` (deterministic predicates: cyclone buffer/wind, GDACS level ordering, freeze deadline, sanity ceiling + population cap, ladder rank, lower-bound membership, order-of-magnitude conflict, event months / attribution month), `schema.py` (idempotent `haz_*` DDL + `_COLUMN_MIGRATIONS` for columns added after a table shipped; spec ENUMs as TEXT+CHECK; JSON as `*_json TEXT`), `migrate.py` (CLI `python -m resolver.hazard_resolution.migrate`), `population.py` (loads `resolver/data/population.csv` into `haz_raw_population`, filters World Bank aggregates via `countries.csv`); Phase 1 adds `ibtracs.py`, `geometry.py` + `data/` (vendored NE boundaries), `detect.py`, `reliefweb_sweep.py`, `resolutions.py`, `cli.py` (the `resolve-hazards` poetry script); Phase 2 adds `sources.py` (shared raw-cache plumbing), the four ladder connectors `gdacs.py`/`emdat.py`/`ifrc_go.py`/`idmc_idu.py`, `candidates.py` (builds `haz_impact_candidates`), `reconcile.py` (the pure-function ladder engine), and `impact.py` (per-month orchestration); Phase 3 adds `reliefweb_docs.py` (document selection + caching), `extract.py` (the one LLM step + strict parser + `haz_doc_extractions` cache/cost ledger), and `figures.py` (deterministic households conversion, dedup, authority precedence, ceiling rejection); Phase 4 adds `ipc.py` (IPC/CH current-period ANALYSES into `haz_raw_ipc`, two acquisition paths), `drought_indicators.py` (the ASAP/tabular feed layer + `haz_raw_drought_indicators`), and `drought.py` (the pure-function drought rule + per-month orchestration — no ladder, no LLM); Phase 5 adds `backcast.py` (the historical replay driver + `haz_backcast_progress` resume ledger), `base_rates.py` (occurrence + severity into the `haz_base_rates_*` tables, plus the shared `last_frozen_month`/`backcast_window`/`months_back_window` helpers), `dfo.py` (the Dartmouth Flood Observatory calibration cross-check + `haz_raw_dfo`), and `acceptance.py` (the one-page markdown acceptance report); Phase 6 adds `prompt_block.py` (the base-rate block the forecaster is shown, generated from the rulebook — the machine's only outward-facing module, and the only one imported from outside the package). Tests: `resolver/tests/test_hazard_resolution_{rulebook,schema,population,ibtracs,geometry,sweep,cyclone,ladder,sources,flood,extraction,drought,backcast,base_rates,dfo,acceptance,prompt_block}.py` (run by resolver-ci-fast, which runs `resolver/tests` wholesale; `resolver/hazard_resolution/**` is in its path filters). `rulebook.yaml` and `data/*.geojson.gz` (the vendored boundaries) are named in pyproject's poetry `include` list (packaged data files, like `series_semantics.yml`). Poetry scripts: `resolve-hazards`, `haz-backcast`, `haz-base-rates`, `haz-acceptance`, `haz-migrate`. **Wired into production 2026-08-05** (resolver_update Phase 2.5 + nightly `haz_backcast.yml`, shadow mode — see the machine section's "Production wiring" block).
- `resolver/connectors/protocol.py` — Connector protocol (21-column canonical schema contract)
- `resolver/connectors/__init__.py` — Connector registry (discover_connectors: ACLED, IDMC, IFRC Montandon, GDACS, FEWS NET IPC, IPC API — six entries) + FORECAST_REGISTRY (VIEWS, CF.org, ACLED CAST)
- `resolver/connectors/fewsnet_ipc.py` — FEWS NET IPC Phase 3+ population connector (DR hazard; Current Situation + Most Likely scenarios)
- `resolver/connectors/acled_cast.py` — ACLED CAST connector (event-count forecasts by type: total/battles/ERV/VAC)
- `resolver/tools/run_pipeline.py` — Pipeline orchestrator (fetch -> validate -> enrich -> precedence -> deltas -> DuckDB)
- `resolver/tools/enrich.py` — Enrichment (registry lookups, ym derivation, defaults)
- `resolver/tools/precedence_config.yml` — Precedence tier policy
- `pythia/prediction_markets/retriever.py` — Prediction market signal retriever (disabled in production via `PYTHIA_PREDICTION_MARKETS_ENABLED=0`, pinned in both workflows — note the *code* default is ON, see the env table; Metaculus returns 403, Polymarket returns 422, each times out at 30s)
- `scripts/print_forecaster_ensemble.py` — Ensemble diagnostic script (must be invoked as `python -m scripts.print_forecaster_ensemble`, not directly)
- `pythia/acaps.py` — ACAPS unified connector (4 datasets: INFORM Severity, Risk Radar, Daily Monitoring, Humanitarian Access)
- `pythia/gdelt.py` — GDELT conflict indicators connector (media-derived conflict intensity for ACE hazard). Downloads GDELT 1.0 daily event exports from `data.gdeltproject.org` (no auth, no cost), classifies events by CAMEO tier (T1 direct violence 18/19/20, T2 escalatory posture 15/17, T3 political deterioration 14/16), computes per-country daily aggregates and rolling 7/30/60-day windows. Provides `fetch_and_store_gdelt_indicators()`, `load_gdelt_conflict_indicators(iso3)`, `format_gdelt_for_prompt(iso3)`, `format_gdelt_for_rc_prompt(iso3)`. Wired into ACE RC, ACE triage, and ACE SPD prompts. Self-storing source. FIPS→ISO3 mapping is a static dict (GDELT uses FIPS 10-4 country codes). Env: `GDELT_EVENTS_DAYS` (default 90), `GDELT_EVENTS_REQUEST_DELAY` (default 1.0).
- `pythia/food_security.py` — Unified food security loader (FEWS NET primary, IPC API fallback); provides `load_food_security()`, `format_food_security_for_prompt()`, `format_food_security_for_spd()`. Routes: tries FEWS NET (publisher='FEWS NET') first, falls back to IPC (publisher='IPC'). Wired into RC, triage, and SPD prompts.
- `pythia/fewsnet_food_security.py` — Backward-compat shim that re-exports from `food_security.py`. Deprecated; existing callers still work.
- `pythia/ipc_phases.py` — Legacy IPC food security connector (requires IPC_API_KEY — dead code; replaced by `resolver/connectors/ipc_api.py`)
- `resolver/connectors/ipc_api.py` — IPC API connector for non-FEWS NET countries. Fetches Phase 3+ data from api.ipcinfo.org, excludes FEWS NET countries, writes to facts_resolved. Env: `IPC_API_KEY`, `IPC_API_MONTHS` (default 24), `IPC_API_REQUEST_DELAY` (default 1.0).
- `pythia/acled_political.py` — ACLED event-level political data (protests, riots, strategic developments); wired into bulk ingest via `_bulk_fetch_acled_political` in `ingest_structured_data.py`. Country attribution is by each event's OWN returned country (never the request param — the ACLED API silently ignores unknown filters), resolved robustly via `_event_iso3` (tries `iso3`/`country_iso3`/`#country+code`, then the `country` name via `resolve_iso3`) since the live API's ISO3 key is not always literally `iso3`; server-side filter uses the documented numeric `iso` param via pycountry.
- `pythia/adversarial_check.py` — Counter-evidence searches for RC Level 1+ (devil's advocate)
- `horizon_scanner/reliefweb.py` — ReliefWeb humanitarian reports connector
- `forecaster/hazard_prompts.py` — Hazard-specific reasoning guidance for SPD prompts. DR/PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED dispatches to `_DR_PHASE3` (FEWS NET IPC-specific); DR/PA still uses `_DR` (IFRC Montandon). FEWS NET projection context injected into DR/PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED prompts via `_load_fewsnet_projection()` in prompts.py.
- `forecaster/scoring.py` — Scoring utilities: `multiclass_brier()`, `log_score()`, `binary_brier()` (Brier score for binary forecasts: `(forecast_p - outcome)^2`)
- `forecaster/trace_validation.py` — Diagnostic validation of structured reasoning traces from SPD ensemble members. Checks prior consistency, delta arithmetic, and magnitude consistency. Produces `trace_quality_score` (0-1) per model. Never blocks forecasts.
- `scripts/ci/snapshot_prompt_artifact.py` — Prompt version snapshot script
- `scripts/ai_bundle/build_scored_forecast_bundle.py` — Scored-forecast analysis bundle builder (one AI-consumable zip per calibration round joining forecast reasoning to outcomes; see the "Scored-forecast analysis bundle" section)
- `scripts/ci/inspect_resolver_db.py` — Resolver DuckDB comprehensive inspection report generator (extracted July 2026 from the ~1,400-line heredoc formerly embedded in `inspect_resolver_duckdb.yml`; the workflow is now a thin wrapper). Sections: Data Freshness at-a-glance (observations split from `phase3plus_projection` so the newest real observation isn't masked by future-dated projections; ACAPS `%b%Y` snapshot labels parsed chronologically — SQL MIN/MAX sorted them alphabetically producing "Apr2026 → Sep2025"), **Inject Readiness** (per-source coverage of the 122-country `hs_country_list.txt` target list with missing-country details — the direct "are all injects ready for a full run" view), pipeline stage completeness, per-table detail, data-quality checks (conflict-forecast staleness >45d; the IDMC IDU note is informational since the forecaster matches `IN ('ACE','IDU')`; the dead `ipc_phases` table is excluded from empty-table warnings). Stdlib + duckdb only (the inspect workflow installs no pandas). Tests: `scripts/ci/tests/test_inspect_resolver_db.py` (run by `resolver-ci-fast.yml`).
- `scripts/refresh_crisiswatch.py` — CrisisWatch scraper, two acquisition paths sharing one parser: `--source wayback` (used in CI by `refresh-crisiswatch.yml` — fetches the server-rendered page from the Wayback Machine, no browser needed) and `--source live` (local Playwright path, kept for manual runs). Writes `crisiswatch_latest.json`.
- `scripts/purge_acled_political_events.py` — Purge cross-contaminated `acled_political_events` rows (pre-July-2026 global-fetch bug); `--force` wipes unconditionally. The weekly ingest also self-heals via `purge_contaminated_events()`.
- `pythia/tools/generate_calibration_advice.py` — Per-hazard/metric calibration advice generation
- `tools/compare_gdacs_ifrc.py` — GDACS vs IFRC Montandon PA comparison diagnostic (side-by-side coverage, ratios, blind spots)
- `tools/probe_montandon_stac.py` — Read-only evaluation spike for the Montandon (Monty) STAC API, which we have never consumed (the `ifrc_montandon` connector polls the IFRC GO operational API instead). Answers eight gates and recommends one of four outcomes. **G1 (can impact records be filtered by upstream source?) is a KILL gate**: Montandon ingests GDACS, whose figures are modelled population exposure, so an unfilterable feed would blend exposure with reported impact in one column. **G4 (latency vs the resolution cutoff) decides resolution-vs-base-rates** and is the gate most likely to fail, since Montandon leans on the slow EM-DAT. Always exits 0 — "the endpoint is down" is a finding, not a build failure. Needs egress to `*.ifrc.org`; run via the `Probe Montandon STAC` workflow. Tests: `resolver/tests/test_probe_montandon_stac.py` (gate/verdict logic only — the network path is untestable in CI).
- `scripts/create_questions_from_triage.py` — Creates forecast questions from HS triage output. Supported hazards: ACE (FATALITIES+PA), CU (PA), DR (PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED+EVENT_OCCURRENCE), FL (PA+EVENT_OCCURRENCE), TC (PA+EVENT_OCCURRENCE), DI (PA). HW excluded (no resolution source). DR/PA for food-security-monitored countries is remapped to metric=PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED with IPC Phase 3+ wording (via `_build_dr_phase3_question_wording`); other countries get no DR/PA question (binary DR/EVENT_OCCURRENCE still generated). **Food-security eligibility (July 2026)**: `_is_fewsnet_country` (curated git-tracked `resolver/data/fewsnet_countries.json`, fail-open if missing) OR `_is_ipc_country` (`resolver/data/ipc_countries.json`, fail-closed — the file is connector-written and never committed, so it typically doesn't exist) OR the **DB fallback** `_db_food_security_countries(con)`: distinct iso3 with `phase3plus_in_need` rows in facts_resolved within the last 36 months (cached per connection) — this is what actually enables DR questions for IPC-only countries. EVENT_OCCURRENCE questions generated for ALL countries (GDACS global coverage). Binary question wording references GDACS Orange/Red alerts. Epoch-specific question_ids with `_{epoch_label}` suffix.
- `forecaster/binary_prompts.py` — Binary event prompt builder for EVENT_OCCURRENCE questions. Contains: `build_binary_event_prompt()` (6-section prompt: role/task, base rate, current situation, GDACS event history, hazard reasoning, output instructions; accepts optional `gdacs_event_history` dict), `build_binary_base_rate()` (queries facts_resolved for seasonal event rates), `get_binary_hazard_reasoning_block()` (DR/FL/TC-specific reasoning), `parse_binary_response()` (JSON parser with code fence stripping, probability clamping to [0.01, 0.99]).
- `scripts/db/update_bucket_centroids.py` — Seeds bucket definitions and centroids into DuckDB from `pythia/buckets.py` BUCKET_SPECS. Iterates all metrics (PA, FATALITIES, PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED).
- `tools/analyze_fewsnet_distribution.py` — Diagnostic script to analyze FEWS NET Phase 3+ data distribution in facts_resolved. Computes distribution stats, histogram by DR_PHASE3_BUCKETS, country-level summary, and bucket balance assessment.
- `resolver/data/fewsnet_countries.json` — List of 48 FEWS NET-monitored country ISO3 codes. Used by question generator to restrict DR/PA questions to FEWS NET countries. Written by the FEWS NET IPC connector during backfill.

## Databases

Two DuckDB databases:

**Pythia DB** (`PYTHIA_DB_URL`): system of record
- `hs_runs`, `hs_triage` — Horizon Scanner outputs (triage scores, RC fields)
- `hs_hazard_tail_packs` — RC/triage grounding evidence packs (name is historical: the tail-pack subsystem was removed July 2026; the RC and triage grounding passes persist their packs here, query-prefixed `rc_grounding:` / `triage_grounding:`, and the forecaster injects them into SPD prompts as "HS GROUNDING EVIDENCE")
- `hs_adversarial_checks` — Counter-evidence for RC Level 1+ (devil's advocate)
- `seasonal_forecasts` — NMME country-level temp/precip anomalies (monthly from CPC)
- `conflict_forecasts` — VIEWS + conflictforecast.org + ACLED CAST conflict predictions (PK: source, iso3, hazard_code, metric, lead_months, forecast_issue_date)
- `questions`, `question_research` — Seeded questions + research briefs
- `forecasts_raw`, `forecasts_ensemble` — Per-model + aggregated SPDs. Both include `reasoning_trace_json TEXT` column storing structured reasoning traces (prior, updates with deltas, point estimate, RC assessment) as JSON. NULL for binary forecasts, Track 2, or models that don't emit traces.
- `sibyl_runs` — Sibyl run records (hs_run_id, K, aggregation, run cost split Opus/Brave, `budget_capped` flag, coverage counts)
- `sibyl_forecasts` — Per-question Sibyl provenance (status incl. `skipped`/`run budget cap`, asOf, per-trial belief traces + quantiles JSON, pooled quantiles, bucket probs, JS divergences vs standard + inter-trial, cost split, leakage stats). The scoreable SPD itself lives in `forecasts_raw`/`forecasts_ensemble` under `model_name='sibyl'`.
- `resolutions` — Ground truth values per (question_id, horizon_m). Not all 6 horizons may have rows — source-aware null handling skips unresolvable horizons.
- `scores` — Brier/log/CRPS per (question, horizon, model). Also carries the external reference rows (`run_id IS NULL`): `__ext_views`, and since Aug 2026 `__ext_climatology`/`__ext_uniform` from `score_baselines.py`
- `forecast_deviation` — Deviation-from-base-rate per (run, question, aggregate model): `js_vs_baserate`, `log_ev_ratio`, `eiv_nominal`/`eiv_per_100k`, `score_family`, `baserate_source`/`baserate_json` provenance (written by `compute_deviation.py`; the interpreter's attention index reads it)
- `baseline_scored_forecasts` — Audit trail for the `__ext_climatology`/`__ext_uniform` reference scores (the exact SPD scored per question/horizon/model, PK on all three)
- `interpretations` — Interpreter reports, versioned per (kind, run key): content_json (validated model output), content_md (rendered report), figures_json (resolved figure maps), status ok/failed_validation/failed_generation, prompt/pack hashes, cost/tokens (written by `interpreter/store.py`; in `mark_test_runs`' RUN_ID_TABLES)
- `calibration_weights`, `calibration_advice` — Per hazard/metric weights + LLM advice
- `bucket_centroids` — SPD bucket centroids per (hazard_code, metric, bucket_index); seeded from `pythia/buckets.py`, updated via EMA from resolution data. Carries `schema_version` (bucket-boundary digest from `bucket_schema_version(metric)`): rows stamped with a stale version are refused by `compute_scores._load_centroids`; NULL (pre-stamp) rows are grandfathered
- `source_coverage` — Per-(metric, iso3, ym) coverage map of the resolution source tables (PK: metric, iso3, ym); rebuilt at the start of every compute_resolutions run; gates zero-defaulting
- `bucket_definitions` — SPD bucket boundary definitions per (metric, bucket_index)
- `reliefweb_reports` — ReliefWeb humanitarian situation reports
- `acled_political_events` — ACLED event-level political data
- `ipc_phases` — IPC food security phase populations
- `acaps_inform_severity`, `acaps_inform_severity_trend` — ACAPS INFORM severity scores
- `acaps_risk_radar` — ACAPS forward-looking risk assessments
- `acaps_daily_monitoring` — ACAPS analyst-curated daily updates
- `acaps_humanitarian_access` — ACAPS humanitarian access scores
- `crisiswatch_entries` — ICG CrisisWatch monthly arrows + alerts (PK: iso3, year, month)
- `hdx_signals` — HDX Signals (OCHA automated crisis monitoring) persisted from CSV (PK: iso3, indicator, signal_date)
- `gdelt_conflict_indicators` — GDELT per-country daily conflict indicators (T1/T2/T3 CAMEO tier counts, Goldstein + tone averages, top codes) (PK: iso3, event_date)
- `enso_state` — ENSO state/forecast snapshots from IRI/CPC (PK: fetch_date)
- `seasonal_tc_outlooks` — Seasonal TC forecasts from TSR/NOAA CPC/BoM (PK: basin, source, fetched_at)
- `seasonal_tc_context_cache` — Pre-formatted per-country TC context text (PK: iso3)
- `llm_calls` — Full telemetry (cost, tokens, latency, errors)
- `llm_batches`, `llm_batch_requests` — Provider Batch-API state (batch ids, per-request bodies/results); the cross-stage state of the staged pipeline (see the Batch-API section)
- `hs_stage_state` — Staged-HS carry state: merged RC results per country (hs_rc_collect → hs_finalize), plus two run-scoped rows under the sentinel `iso3='__RUN__'` — `brave_breaker` (circuit-breaker stats carried across stages) and `pipeline_run_id` (keyed by **pipeline_id**, records the hs_run_id that pipeline minted; the fallback that lets a collect stage resolve its run id when hs_submit created no `llm_batches` row at all)

**Resolver DB** (`resolver/db/schema.sql`): fact ingestion
- `facts_resolved` — Precedence-resolved facts (unique on ym, iso3, hazard_code, metric, series_semantics). Includes `alertlevel` column (GDACS-specific, NULL for other sources).
- `facts_deltas` — Monthly flow changes
- `snapshots`, `manifests`, `meta_runs` — Pipeline metadata
- `haz_*` — Hazard resolution machine tables (created by `haz-migrate` / `python -m resolver.hazard_resolution.migrate`, idempotent — and self-created by every machine entry point via `ensure_haz_schema`; **populated since 2026-08-05 by two workflows**: `resolver_update.yml` Phase 2.5 runs `resolve-hazards` over the trailing 3 months for all three hazards each cycle, and the nightly `haz_backcast.yml` fills history in time-boxed chunks — Phase 1 `haz_raw_ibtracs` for TC, Phase 2 `haz_raw_{gdacs,emdat,ifrc_go,idmc_idu}` + `haz_impact_candidates` for TC and FL, Phase 3 `haz_raw_reliefweb_docs` + `haz_doc_extractions` for triggered cells): `haz_raw_{ibtracs,gdacs,emdat,idmc_idu,ifrc_go,reliefweb_docs,ipc,drought_indicators,dfo,population}` thin per-source caches with retrieval timestamps and content hashes (identical re-fetch is a no-op, upstream revision appends, readers take the newest), `haz_triggers` (Layer-1 detection verdicts + evidence of absence), `haz_impact_candidates` (Layer-2 ladder inputs; `value_type` ∈ affected / displaced_lower_bound / exposed_ceiling — the last is GDACS exposure, filtered out of the ladder's choices; `preference_rank` + `detail_json` carry the extracted rung's own precedence and its verbatim quote), `haz_doc_extractions` (the LLM extraction cache AND monthly cost ledger, keyed doc+model+prompt_version+**cell** (iso3, hazard, year, month) since 2026-08-05 — the prompt names the cell, so an answer for one cell is not an answer for another; only `status='ok'` rows are cache hits, and only rows that billed tokens count toward the monthly cap), `haz_resolutions` (RESOLVED_ZERO/RESOLVED_VALUE/NO_DATA + provenance_json + rule_fired + flagged + **provisional** + **run_type** + frozen_at), `haz_revisions` (post-freeze audit, never alters resolved values), `haz_backcast_progress` (the backcast's per-(hazard, month) resume ledger), `haz_base_rates_{occurrence,severity}`. **`run_type` (`live`/`backcast`) is on `haz_resolutions` and `haz_triggers`** — provenance on the row, never part of the key: a cell has one answer, and the freeze guard decides whether a later run may replace it. The migrated column cannot carry the DDL's CHECK, so `schema.validate_run_type` enforces it in Python at every writer

## Resolver architecture

The Resolver was refactored in PR #610 to a connector-based architecture. Defunct legacy connectors (DTM, EM-DAT ingestion, HDX, IPC, ODP, ReliefWeb, UNHCR, WFP, WHO, WorldPop) were removed. The GDACS connector was re-implemented as a new Connector protocol source.

**Connector protocol** (`resolver/connectors/protocol.py`): Every data source implements a `Connector` protocol with a `name` attribute and a `fetch_and_normalize()` method that returns a DataFrame with exactly 21 canonical columns (event_id, iso3, hazard_code, metric, value, as_of_date, publisher, etc.). Connectors may include supplementary columns beyond the canonical set (e.g. `alertlevel` for GDACS) by passing `extra_columns` to `validate_canonical`; `run_pipeline` auto-detects and passes these through.

**Active connectors** (`resolver/connectors/__init__.py` REGISTRY):
- `acled` — ACLED conflict/fatalities data (wraps `resolver/ingestion/acled_client`)
- `idmc` — IDMC internal displacement data (wraps `resolver/ingestion/idmc/`)
- `ifrc_montandon` — IFRC GO connector (active; pulls Field Reports, Events, Appeals, and Situation Reports via `resolver/ingestion/ifrc_go_client.py`, writes `publisher='IFRC'` rows to `facts_resolved`). Tier-0 source for natural-hazard PA (FL, DR, TC). Three sources of an `affected` row, in precedence order: (1) the source record's `num_affected` (and `gov_`/`other_` variants) — `method='api'`, `confidence='med'`; (2) for IFRC GO Appeals (DREF + Emergency Appeals), the `num_beneficiaries` headline "people targeted" figure, promoted to `affected` by `_maybe_use_beneficiaries_as_affected` — `method='api'`, `confidence='high'`, event_id suffix `-appeal`, source-field marker `appeal:num_beneficiaries`; (3) when neither is available but at least 2 of `{fatalities, injured, displaced, missing}` are present, the sum is synthesised as a floor estimate by `_maybe_derive_affected` — `method='derived'`, `confidence='low'`, event_id suffix `-derived`, source-field marker `derived_sum:...`.
