Since 2026-08-03 the gate is `python3 -m scripts.ci.check_pipeline_active --emit active`, with `actions/checkout@v4` running *before* it so the gate can be the shared script; it is stdlib + `gh` only, so idle ticks still skip setup-python and the pip install. **The weekly `ingest-structured-data.yml` is gated while a pipeline is in flight** (`scripts/ci/check_pipeline_active.py` gate job: newest batch-state artifact <`PYTHIA_PIPELINE_ACTIVE_WINDOW_H`=72h old with no newer successful fc_collect_finalize → skip with a `::notice`; `force_during_pipeline` dispatch input overrides; fails open) — the concurrency group only serializes single runs, and an ingest landing mid-pipeline would be silently discarded by the final canonical upload. **The monthly cron moved here from `run_horizon_scanner.yml` on 2026-07-27** (owner decision; the legacy workflow is manual-dispatch-only as the sync fallback — never re-add its cron while this one is live, or the pipeline double-runs); "Pythia Pipeline Stage" is in all three canonical-DB candidate lists.

**Prompt V3 order + caching** (Phase 1, flags `PYTHIA_PROMPT_V3_ORDER` / `PYTHIA_PROMPT_CACHE_ENABLED`, both default OFF): the SPD (`build_spd_prompt_v2`, `return_parts=True` → (prefix, suffix)), binary, RC and triage prompt builders have a static-first assembly (role/task/buckets/hazard guidance/method+schema first, per-question data last) that creates multi-KB shared prefixes for OpenAI automatic caching (+ `prompt_cache_key`), Gemini implicit caching, and Anthropic `cache_control` (via `call_chat_ms(cache_segments=...)`; Sibyl's step prompts use a 4-block template with breakpoints). Legacy assembly is byte-identical to the pre-refactor prompts (verified against HEAD; guarded by `forecaster/tests/test_prompt_v3_order.py`). **The forecaster call sites were only actually WIRED to the cache plumbing on 2026-07-28** (audit finding C1: before that, `cache_segments` had one caller — Sibyl — and `prompt_cache_key` none, so the flags were on in production while Opus 5 paid full input price on every SPD call): the Track-1/Track-2 SPD sites build via `return_parts=True` and thread `cache_prefix` + `prompt_cache_key` (`pythia:{family}:{hazard}:{metric}:t{track}`) down `_call_spd_members_v2(_compat)`/`_call_spd_bayesmc_v2` → `_call_spd_model_for_spec`, where segments are derived against the prompt ACTUALLY sent (tail evidence appends can't break the segments-join==prompt invariant); the binary path passes the OpenAI key only; HS RC/triage (Gemini) needs no body change. Verification: `scripts/post_run_diagnostics.py`'s "Prompt cache" section warns when the flag is on and cache reads stay zero. Guarded by `forecaster/tests/test_prompt_cache_wiring.py`. **Both flags are ON in the production workflows since 2026-07-27**: `pythia_pipeline_stage.yml`, `run_horizon_scanner.yml` (kept in lockstep so the sync fallback produces the same prompts), and `run_sibyl.yml` set `PYTHIA_PROMPT_V3_ORDER=1` + `PYTHIA_PROMPT_CACHE_ENABLED=1`; code defaults remain OFF for local/dev runs. `scripts/compare_prompt_order_runs.py` remains the regression harness — run it against the first production cycles (compare SPD JS-divergence, parse failures, RC/triage distributions vs the last pre-V3 run) and flip the env flags back to "0" in the three workflows to revert. Cost telemetry (Phase 0): `usage_to_dict` passes through `cache_read_input_tokens`/`cache_creation_input_tokens`/`cached_tokens`/`service_tier`/`batch_id`; `pythia/model_costs.json` supports object-form entries with cached rates; `compute_cost_split_usd` is the single cost formula (used by both `estimate_cost_usd` and `llm_logging`) applying cached rates and the 0.5× batch multiplier.

**The batched path had a 0% cache hit rate by construction until 2026-08-03.** Measured on the 2026-08-01 production DB: all 99 Anthropic, 198 OpenAI and 1,015 Gemini calls logged **zero** `cache_read_input_tokens` and zero `cache_creation_input_tokens` — because every one of them went through a provider Batch API, and `_batch_prompt_cache_enabled()` defaulted to off. Sibyl, the only synchronous Anthropic path, was the sole exception and works well (2026-07-31: 108,309 read tokens on 345,277 prompt tokens = 31%, against 5,519 write tokens — a 19.6:1 read:write ratio, ~11% off its Opus bill). Three changes: (1) `PYTHIA_BATCH_PROMPT_CACHE=1` in `pythia_pipeline_stage.yml` — the code default stays 0, since batch cache hits are best-effort (items fan out across nodes; an entry is only readable once the first response has begun) while the write premium is certain, but with the flag off the hypothesis could never be tested and the downside is ~$0.06/cycle; (2) batch bodies now request a **1h TTL** (`_batch_cache_ttl()`) — the 2026-08-01 SPD batch ran **41 minutes**, so a 5-minute entry written by the first item is long gone before the last item is scheduled; (3) the cacheable-minimum gate is now **per model** (`_anthropic_cache_min_chars`, keyed by literal id prefix like the sampling-param guards) instead of one 4096-char constant — the minimum is not monotonic across generations (512 tokens on claude-opus-5, still 4096 on Opus 4.6/4.5 and Haiku 4.5), and the old constant silently left every `binary_v2` prefix (~760 tokens) unmarked on the one model that would have cached it. **Expected ceiling is modest and should be checked against, not assumed:** the measured longest common prefix within each (model, phase, hazard, metric) group is 23% of Anthropic prompt tokens, and input is only 36% of Anthropic cost (output is 64%), so best case is ~$0.43/cycle of a $6.07 Anthropic bill. **Verify after the next cycle** via `batch_economics.py` / `post_run_diagnostics.py` (both already read `cache_read_input_tokens`); if reads stay at zero, set the workflow flag back to "0" — that is the whole revert. Guarded by `forecaster/tests/test_prompt_cache_wiring.py` (`test_batch_bodies_buy_an_hour_ttl_but_sync_bodies_do_not`, `test_cache_minimum_is_per_model_not_one_constant`). **Rule: a cache flag that is on in the workflow proves nothing — only a non-zero `cache_read_input_tokens` does.**

**Env flags**: `PYTHIA_BATCH_API_ENABLED` (0), `PYTHIA_BATCH_PROVIDERS` (openai,anthropic,google), `PYTHIA_BATCH_MAX_WAIT_H` (24), `PYTHIA_BATCH_MAX_REQUESTS`/`PYTHIA_BATCH_MAX_BYTES`/`PYTHIA_BATCH_HTTP_TIMEOUT_SEC` (chunking/HTTP), `PYTHIA_PROMPT_V3_ORDER` (0), `PYTHIA_PROMPT_CACHE_ENABLED` (0), `PYTHIA_BATCH_PROMPT_CACHE` (code default 0; **"1" in `pythia_pipeline_stage.yml` since 2026-08-03**), `PYTHIA_BATCH_CACHE_TTL` (`1h`; `none` reverts to Anthropic's 5-minute default), `PYTHIA_ANTHROPIC_CACHE_MIN_CHARS` (unset — overrides the per-model cacheable-minimum table), `PYTHIA_STAGE_MAX_DISPATCH_ATTEMPTS` (3) / `PYTHIA_STAGE_RETRY_COOLDOWN_MIN` (60) (poller stall cap), `PYTHIA_PIPELINE_ACTIVE_WINDOW_H` (72, ingest gate).

**Poller self-rescheduling (2026-07-29, IMPORTANT)**: `poll_llm_batches.yml`'s `*/15` cron **is not honoured by GitHub** — observed firing roughly hourly (07:21, 04:29, 01:08, 23:40), which parked staged pipelines between stages for hours and made the whole staged design look broken. Scheduled workflows are best-effort and frequent crons are throttled. The cron became **ignition only**: `poll_llm_batches.py` emits a `rearm` step output and the workflow, when it is true, sleeps ~5 min and `gh workflow run`s **itself** with an incremented `chain_depth`. **Since 2026-08-03 the cron is only the BACKSTOP** — each submit-side stage of `pythia_pipeline_stage.yml` dispatches the poller itself right after uploading its batch state (`pythia_pipeline_stage.yml:294, 502, 749, 1006`), because the cron proved unreliable at ignition (a 3h38m gap between scheduled ticks on 2026-08-01). Do not remove the cron: it is what recovers a pipeline whose ignition dispatch failed. Re-arm fires only for `REARM_ACTIONS = {waiting, dispatched, already running, cooling down}`; **`already completed` and `STALLED` are terminal and must stay that way** — the final stage (`fc_collect_finalize`) emits no batch state, so its predecessor's state lingers for the artifact's 14-day retention and would otherwise re-arm forever. That is why `_dispatch_decision` splits the old `already running/done` reason into `already running` vs `already completed` — **do not re-collapse them**. Three layered guards: live-pipeline check, `PYTHIA_BATCH_MAX_WAIT_H` age bound, and a `POLLER_MAX_CHAIN` cap that is **derived, never hardcoded** — `_max_chain()` returns `max(200, int(wait_h*60/POLLER_TICK_MINUTES * 1.5) + 1)` with `POLLER_TICK_MINUTES = 5.5`, i.e. **393 links (~36h) at the production 24h ceiling**. An explicit `POLLER_MAX_CHAIN` still wins as an operator hard-stop. (The cap was once hardcoded to 200 under a comment claiming "~7-minute cadence, a little over 24h"; both halves were wrong and it killed a live chain 6h early — see the 2026-07-31 entry in Known failure modes.) Self-dispatch is safe because the workflow already has `permissions: actions: write` and `concurrency: cancel-in-progress: false` (a self-dispatch queues rather than cancelling), and `workflow_dispatch` bypasses the activity gate so chained ticks always poll. Each tick also writes `diagnostics/poll_decision.json` (per-pipeline verdict, batch states, action) uploaded as **`pythia-poller-decisions`** — previously dispatch reasoning existed only in job logs, which expire, so a stall had no durable evidence trail. Env: `POLLER_CHAIN_DEPTH`, `POLLER_MAX_CHAIN`, `POLLER_DECISION_PATH`. Tests: `tests/test_poll_llm_batches_guard.py`. **The workflow's activity gate is `scripts/ci/check_pipeline_active.py --emit active` (2026-08-03)** — the SAME decision core the weekly-ingest gate uses, with the opposite sense (`active` = in flight; `proceed` = NOT in flight). It was previously an inline shell copy implementing only the artifact-age half of the test, so for ~72h after every pipeline finished each hourly cron tick paid a full checkout + pip install + provider poll only to print `already completed` for every pipeline (~70 wasted runs per cycle — this is what "the poller looks like it is still hunting for something" actually was). `actions/checkout@v4` now runs BEFORE the gate so the gate can be the shared script; the gate is stdlib + `gh` only, so idle ticks still skip setup-python and the pip install. **Both modes fail OPEN to `true`** — a broken gate must neither silence the weekly ingest nor stop the poller advancing a live pipeline — so the two senses cannot be collapsed into one negation. Note the cron itself still fires regardless: this shortens idle ticks, it does not remove them from the Actions run list.

**Tests**: `pythia/tests/test_llm_batch.py` (adapters mocked, state machine, custom-id charset + pipeline scoping, batch-tier pricing, body parity, salvage/counters), `forecaster/tests/test_batch_phase.py` (submit/collect/fallback via the `forecaster.cli.*` seams, cross-pipeline miss, sent-prompt fidelity), `horizon_scanner/tests/test_hs_stages.py` (mode matrix, enqueue body parity, replay + log-once, grounding/stage-state round-trips, hs_triage rerun idempotency, pack coexistence — this one runs in **horizon-scanner-ci**, not forecaster-ci), `forecaster/tests/test_prompt_v3_order.py`, `forecaster/tests/test_prompt_cache_wiring.py`, `tests/test_poll_llm_batches_guard.py`, `tests/test_check_pipeline_active.py` — **all wired into forecaster-ci since 2026-07-28** (they previously ran in no workflow).

## Testing

```bash
# Run all pythia tests (skip fastapi-dependent tests if not installed)
python3 -m pytest pythia/tests/ --ignore=pythia/tests/test_api_question_bundle.py -v

# Run cross-module tests (skip fastapi/openai-dependent tests)
python3 -m pytest tests/ --ignore-glob='tests/test_api_*' --ignore=tests/test_web_research.py -v

# Run RC calibration tests
python3 -m pytest resolver/tests/test_rc_calibration.py -v

# Run resolver tests
python3 -m pytest resolver/tests/ -v
```

**pytest config** (`pytest.ini`): 120s timeout, thread-based, log_cli enabled.
**Markers**: `@pytest.mark.db`, `@pytest.mark.allow_network`, `@pytest.mark.slow`, `@pytest.mark.nightly`, `@pytest.mark.legacy_freeze`.

Some test files require `fastapi` or `openai` which may not be installed locally — exclude them with `--ignore` as shown above.

## Common environment variables

| Variable | Purpose |
|----------|---------|
| `PYTHIA_DB_URL` | DuckDB connection URL |
| `PYTHIA_LLM_PROFILE` | LLM profile: `prod` |
| `PYTHIA_TEST_MODE` | Pipeline test mode flag (1/true/yes = stamp all output as test data) |
| `PYTHIA_LLM_CONCURRENCY` | Max concurrent LLM calls |
| `PYTHIA_API_TOKEN` | Admin API auth token |
| `PYTHIA_WEB_RESEARCH_ENABLED` | Enable shared retriever (0/1) |
| `HS_MAX_WORKERS` | HS concurrent country workers |
| `FORECASTER_RESEARCH_MAX_WORKERS` | Research phase concurrency |
| `FORECASTER_SPD_MAX_WORKERS` | SPD phase concurrency |
| `PYTHIA_HS_RC_LEVEL*_*` | RC threshold overrides |
| `PYTHIA_HS_RC_DIST_WARN_*` | RC distribution warning thresholds |
| `PYTHIA_HS_ADVERSARIAL_MAX_PER_COUNTRY` | Max RC-promoted hazards per country given adversarial checks (default 2) |
| `PYTHIA_HS_RC_PASSES` | RC LLM passes per hazard (default 1, max 2; 2 only adds signal with a distinct `PYTHIA_RC_MODEL_PASS2`) |
| `PYTHIA_HS_TRIAGE_PASSES` | Triage LLM passes per hazard (default 1, max 2; 2 only adds signal with a distinct `PYTHIA_TRIAGE_MODEL_PASS2`) |
| `PYTHIA_ADVERSARIAL_CHECK_ENABLED` | Enable adversarial checks for RC L1+ (0/1, default 1) |
| `PYTHIA_GROUNDING_PRIMARY_BACKEND` | Primary grounding backend: `brave` (default), `openai`, or `gemini` |
| `BRAVE_SEARCH_API_KEY` | Brave Search API subscription token (required for Brave grounding) |
| `PYTHIA_BRAVE_MAX_RPS` | Max Brave Search API requests per second (default: 15, paid limit is 20) |
| `PYTHIA_HS_RESEARCH_WEB_SEARCH_ENABLED` | Enable web search for the legacy country-level RC evidence packs (0/1, **default 0** at every read site). The HS caller (`_maybe_build_country_evidence_pack`) is marked DEPRECATED and no longer called; the live readers are `forecaster/cli.py` and the debug bundle |
| `PYTHIA_PREDICTION_MARKETS_ENABLED` | Enable the prediction market retriever (0/1). **Unset falls through to `pythia/config.yaml`'s `prediction_markets.enabled: true`, so the code default is ON** — it is off in production only because both workflows pin it to "0" (see known failure modes) |
| `PYTHIA_GOOGLE_SPD_TIMEOUT_FLASH_SEC` | Gemini Flash SPD timeout (default 300s) |
| `PYTHIA_GOOGLE_SPD_TIMEOUT_PRO_SEC` | Gemini Pro SPD timeout (default 300s) |
| `ANTHROPIC_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS` | Anthropic max output tokens for SPD calls (default 16384) |
| `PYTHIA_ANTHROPIC_SPD_MAX_TOKENS` | Anthropic SPD-specific max tokens override (default `max(ANTHROPIC_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS, 32768)` = **32768** — deliberately above the 16384 general ceiling because Opus 5 charges adaptive thinking against the same budget) |
| `ACAPS_USERNAME` / `ACAPS_PASSWORD` | ACAPS API credentials (the username var is `ACAPS_USERNAME`, not an email-named one) |
| `GDACS_MONTHS` | Number of months of GDACS history to fetch (default 3; 135 for full backfill to 2015) |
| `GDACS_REQUEST_DELAY` | Seconds between GDACS API requests per worker (default 1.0) |
| `GDACS_ENRICH_WORKERS` | Thread-pool size for GDACS per-event RSS enrichment (default 6; 1 = sequential) |
| `GDACS_FORCE_RSS` | Force static RSS feed path ("1" to enable; overrides auto-detection) |
| `GDACS_FORCE_JSON` | Force JSON search API path ("1" to enable; overrides auto-detection) |
| `FEWSNET_MONTHS` | Number of months of FEWS NET IPC history to fetch (default 12; 120 for backfill to 2016) |
| `FEWSNET_REQUEST_DELAY` | Seconds between FEWS NET API retries (default 1.0) |
| `IPC_API_KEY` | API key for api.ipcinfo.org (required for the IPC connector; OPTIONAL for the PA machine's drought path, which falls back to the `phase3plus_in_need` rows already in `facts_resolved`) |
| `EMDAT_API_KEY` | EM-DAT GraphQL API bearer token (PA resolution machine, ladder rung 1). Unset → the rung reports UNAVAILABLE, which is recorded on the row and is not the same as empty |
| `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` | Also backs the PA machine's `hazard_extraction` role (ladder rung 2, Haiku-class document transcription) |
| `IDMC_API_KEY` | IDMC IDU client id, sent as the `client_id` query param (PA resolution machine, lower-bound rung). **Falls back to `IDMC_HELIX_CLIENT_ID`** — the same IDMC credential, same `client_id` param, same `helix-tools-api.idmcdb.org/external-api/idus/` host (this rung uses `/all/`, the ingestion path `/last-180-days/`) — so one secret serves both. **Not** `IDMC_API_TOKEN`: that is a bearer token for `backend.idmcdb.org`, and its presence is a feature flag (`run_connectors.py` derives `RESOLVER_SKIP_IDMC` from it), so it must never be repurposed. Neither set → the rung reports UNAVAILABLE, recorded on the row |
| `RELIEFWEB_APPNAME` | ReliefWeb `appname` parameter for the silence sweep (falls back to `pythia-resolution-machine` with a warning) |
| `IPC_API_MONTHS` | Number of months of IPC API history to fetch (default 24; IPC analyses are infrequent) |
| `IPC_API_REQUEST_DELAY` | Seconds between IPC API retries (default 1.0) |
| `GDELT_EVENTS_DAYS` | Number of days of GDELT 1.0 daily exports to fetch (default 90) |
| `GDELT_EVENTS_REQUEST_DELAY` | Seconds between GDELT daily file downloads (default 1.0) |
| `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_MAX_PACK_TOKENS` | Current-run bundle hard token cap (default 250000; low-ranked question records truncated, recorded in the manifest) |
| `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_TOP_N` | Attention list length for the current-run bundle's deltas (default 8) |
| `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_PER_CAPITA_FLOOR` | Absolute `eiv_nominal` floor for the per-capita attention ranking (default 10000) |
| `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_ENABLED` | Interpreter kill switch (default 1) |
| `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_MODEL_ID` | Purpose-specific override, wins over the config `interpreter` role |
| `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_TEMPLATE_VERSION` | Interpreter template version (default v2) |
| `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_WORSENING_MULTIPLE` | How far above its base rate an extra worsening entry must sit (default 2.0) |
| `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_MIN_PER_CATEGORY` | Entries always shown per report section, however quiet the month (default 3) |
| `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_MAX_PER_CATEGORY` | Cap per report section (default 6) |
| `PYTHIA_PUBLIC_BASE_URL` | Base URL for absolute question links in the PDF (default https://fredforecaster.org) |
| `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_THINKING` | Anthropic `output_config.effort` level for the interpreter call (default high) |
| `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS` | Interpreter max_tokens — thinking shares this budget (default 32768) |
| `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_STRICT_VALIDATION` | When 1, a validation failure suppresses publication (Phase 4 consumer; default 0) |

| `PYTHIA_HS_ONLY_COUNTRIES` | Restrict HS to specific countries (comma-separated ISO3s) |
| `PYTHIA_HS_FALLBACK_MODEL_SPECS` | Fallback model for HS triage (default from profile `hs_fallback`) |
| `PYTHIA_HS_LLM_MAX_ATTEMPTS` | Max retry attempts for HS triage LLM calls (default 3) |
| `PYTHIA_HS_GEMINI_TIMEOUT_SEC` | Gemini timeout for HS triage (default 120s) |
| `PYTHIA_DUCKDB_MEMORY_LIMIT` | DuckDB memory limit for API (default "150MB"; applied to the read connection, the db_sync introspection connection, and ui_runs writes) |
| `PYTHIA_DUCKDB_THREADS` | DuckDB thread count for API (default "2") |
| `PYTHIA_MAX_CONCURRENT_HEAVY` | Max concurrent heavy API queries (default "2"; covers export *build* time only since the temp-file download refactor — set to 1 on Render) |
| `PYTHIA_ALLOW_INPROCESS_RUN` | Allow `/v1/run` to launch the HS+forecaster pipeline inside the API process (0/1, default 0 — must stay off on Render; local dev sets 1) |
| `PYTHIA_EXPORT_TMP_DIR` | Directory for spilled/cached download exports (default: system tmp + `/pythia-exports`; must NOT point at the Render persistent disk `/data`) |
| `PYTHIA_CORS_ALLOW_ORIGINS` | CORS allowed origins for API (default "*") |
| `PYTHIA_SYNC_FROM_ARTIFACTS` | Enable DB sync from GitHub artifacts on API startup |
| `GPT5_CALL_TIMEOUT_SEC` | GPT-5.x SPD call timeout (default 300s) |
| `GEMINI_CALL_TIMEOUT_SEC` | Gemini SPD call timeout (default 300s) |
| `PYTHIA_SPD_ENSEMBLE_SPECS` | Override SPD ensemble at runtime (comma-separated `provider:model_id`) |
| `PYTHIA_USE_CALIBRATION_WEIGHTS` | Apply calibration weights to SPD ensemble aggregation (0/1, default 1) |
| `PYTHIA_BLOCK_PROVIDERS` | Comma-separated provider names to exclude from ensemble |
| `PYTHIA_RC_MODEL_PASS1` / `PYTHIA_RC_MODEL_PASS2` | Override RC LLM model per pass |
| `PYTHIA_CREDIT_RETRY_PAUSE_{PROVIDER}` | Credit-retry pause in seconds per provider (default: OpenAI=900, Anthropic=300, Google=600) |
| `PYTHIA_CREDIT_RETRY_MAX_{PROVIDER}` | Credit-retry max attempts per provider (default: 3 for all) |
| `PYTHIA_BATCH_API_ENABLED` | Master switch for provider Batch-API submission (0/1, default 0) |
| `PYTHIA_BATCH_PROVIDERS` | Providers allowed to batch (default "openai,anthropic,google"; others stay sync) |
| `PYTHIA_BATCH_MAX_WAIT_H` | Max hours to wait on a provider batch before cancel + sync fallback (default 24) |
| `PYTHIA_PROMPT_V3_ORDER` | Static-first prompt assembly for cache-friendly prefixes (0/1, code default 0; set to 1 in the three production workflows since 2026-07-27) |
| `PYTHIA_PROMPT_CACHE_ENABLED` | Anthropic cache_control blocks + OpenAI prompt_cache_key (0/1, code default 0; set to 1 in the three production workflows since 2026-07-27) |
| `PYTHIA_SPD_V2_USE_BAYESMC` / `PYTHIA_SPD_V2_WRITE_BOTH` | Gate whether `ensemble_bayesmc_v2` is produced at all (both default "0" in `forecaster/cli.py`) — with both off, Track 1 writes only the mean aggregation |
| `PYTHIA_HS_PRIORITY_THRESHOLD` | Triage-score cutoff for the "priority" tier that routes RC-0 questions to Track 2 (default 0.50) |
| `PYTHIA_PROVIDER_FAILURE_THRESHOLD` / `PYTHIA_PROVIDER_COOLDOWN_SECONDS` | Provider-level circuit breaker in `forecaster/providers.py` (defaults 6 consecutive failures / 60s cooldown) — separate from, and in addition to, the Brave breaker |
| `PYTHIA_LLM_RETRIES` / `PYTHIA_GOOGLE_SPD_RETRIES` | Transient-retry depth for SPD calls (default 3) |
| `PYTHIA_TRIAGE_MODEL_PASS1` / `PYTHIA_TRIAGE_MODEL_PASS2` | Override the triage LLM model per pass |
| `PYTHIA_SCENARIO_MODEL_ID`, `PYTHIA_TRACK2_MODEL_ID`, `PYTHIA_GROUNDING_MODEL_ID`, `PYTHIA_WEB_RESEARCH_MODEL_ID`, `HS_MODEL_ID` | The purpose-specific overrides that win over the config `roles` block (see the model-registry section) |
| `PYTHIA_SPD_HARD_FAIL` | Make SPD failures fatal to the in-process pipeline runner |
| `CANONICAL_DB_ARTIFACT_NAME` / `CANONICAL_DB_RUN_ID` / `CANONICAL_DB_WORKFLOW` | Steer the API's canonical-DB artifact discovery (`pythia/api/db_sync.py`) |
| `PYTHIA_POPULATION_CSV_PATH` | Population table backing per-capita risk-index normalization |

Provider API keys: `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `GEMINI_API_KEY` (`GOOGLE_API_KEY` is accepted as a fallback), `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`. Connector auth: `ACLED_USERNAME`/`ACLED_PASSWORD` (or `ACLED_REFRESH_TOKEN`), `GO_API_TOKEN` (IFRC), `IDMC_API_TOKEN`, `ACAPS_USERNAME`/`ACAPS_PASSWORD`, `IPC_API_KEY`, `BRAVE_SEARCH_API_KEY`. API auth accepts `PYTHIA_API_TOKEN` or `PYTHIA_API_KEY`.

## Test mode

Test mode runs the identical pipeline as production. The only difference is that every row written during a test run carries `is_test = TRUE` in a dedicated boolean column. The website hides test data by default; users can opt in via the "Test ON/OFF" toggle in the navigation bar.

**Architecture:**
- `pythia/test_mode.py` — single source of truth; `is_test_mode()` reads `PYTHIA_TEST_MODE` env var
- `is_test BOOLEAN DEFAULT FALSE` column on all pipeline tables (hs_runs, hs_scenarios, hs_triage, hs_country_reports, hs_hazard_tail_packs, hs_adversarial_checks, questions, forecasts_ensemble, forecasts_raw, llm_calls, question_research, question_run_metrics, scenarios, question_context, run_provenance, resolutions, scores, eiv_scores)
- API endpoints accept `?include_test=true` query parameter (default: hidden)
- Calibration (`compute_calibration_pythia`, `generate_calibration_advice`) excludes test data automatically
- Downloads include an `is_test` column
- `scripts/mark_test_runs.py` retroactively marks existing runs as test data
- `.github/workflows/mark_test_runs.yml` workflow wraps the script for CI use
- `scripts/reconcile_llm_calls_is_test.py` self-heals `llm_calls.is_test` (flips FALSE→TRUE for calls whose run_id/hs_run_id belongs to a test run — repairs the historical forecaster stamping gap so test costs don't leak into non-test cost views); idempotent, wired into `compute_calibration_pythia.yml` every cycle. The forward fix is `log_forecaster_llm_call` inheriting `is_test_mode()` by default in `forecaster/llm_logging.py`

**Triggering test mode:**
- Set `PYTHIA_TEST_MODE=1` in environment (or `true`/`yes`)
- `run_horizon_scanner.yml` has a `test_mode` checkbox input
- `manual_test.yaml` always sets `PYTHIA_TEST_MODE=1`

## Build and run

```bash
# Install dependencies
pip install -r python_library_requirements.txt

# Initialize DB schema
python3 -c "from pythia.db.schema import ensure_schema; ensure_schema()"

# Run Resolver pipeline (fetch facts from ACLED + IDMC -> DuckDB)
python3 -m resolver.tools.run_pipeline
# Or specific connectors: python3 -m resolver.tools.run_pipeline --connectors acled idmc

# Ingest ALL structured data (conflict forecasts, ACAPS, IPC, ReliefWeb, NMME)
python3 -m pythia.tools.ingest_structured_data
# Filter by source: python3 -m pythia.tools.ingest_structured_data --sources conflict acaps ipc
# Valid sources: views, conflictforecast, acledcast, acaps_inform_severity,
#   acaps_risk_radar, acaps_daily_monitoring, acaps_humanitarian_access, ipc, reliefweb, acled_political, nmme, gdacs, fewsnet_ipc, ipc_api, gdelt
# Aliases: acaps (all 4 ACAPS sources), conflict (views+conflictforecast+acledcast)

# Standalone entry points (still work independently):
python3 -m resolver.tools.ingest_nmme                        # NMME only
python3 -m resolver.tools.fetch_conflict_forecasts            # conflict forecasts only
python3 -m resolver.tools.fetch_conflict_forecasts --sources views conflictforecast_org acled_cast

# Refresh CrisisWatch data — CI path (Wayback Machine, no browser needed)
python3 -m scripts.refresh_crisiswatch --source wayback --only-if-newer --max-age-days 60 --expect-edition-by-day 7
# Local live-site path (requires Playwright: pip install playwright && playwright install chromium)
python3 -m scripts.refresh_crisiswatch [--output PATH] [--verbose] [--channel chrome]

# Run Horizon Scanner
python3 -m horizon_scanner.horizon_scanner

# Run Forecaster
python3 -m forecaster.cli --mode pythia

# Post-forecast pipeline
python3 -m pythia.tools.compute_resolutions
python3 -m pythia.tools.compute_scores
python3 -m pythia.tools.compute_calibration_pythia
python3 -m pythia.tools.generate_calibration_advice

# API server
uvicorn pythia.api.app:app --reload --port 8000

# Dashboard
cd web && npm install && npm run dev
```

## Prompt editing

Before editing any prompt source files, always run `bash scripts/snapshot_prompts.sh` first. This archives the current prompts before changes so the About page can show historical versions. Commit the snapshot alongside the prompt edits.

Key prompt files:
- `forecaster/prompts.py` — Forecaster SPD prompt builder
- `horizon_scanner/rc_prompts.py` — Per-hazard RC prompt builders (ACE, DR, FL, TC; the HW builder is retained as dead code — HW is in `BLOCKED_HAZARDS`)
- `horizon_scanner/hs_triage_prompts.py` — Per-hazard triage prompt builders
- `horizon_scanner/rc_grounding_prompts.py` — RC-specific grounding queries (per-hazard)
- `horizon_scanner/hs_triage_grounding_prompts.py` — Triage-specific grounding queries (per-hazard)
- `pythia/web_research/backends/brave_search.py` — Brave Search API grounding backend (primary, deterministic, $5/1K queries). Wired to Brave circuit breaker: checks `is_tripped()` at entry, records success/failure per query.
- `pythia/web_research/brave_circuit_breaker.py` — Thread-safe circuit breaker for Brave Search API. Trips after 3 consecutive failures; short-circuits all subsequent Brave calls when tripped. Module-level singleton, reset per HS run.
- `pythia/web_research/backends/gemini_grounding.py` — Gemini grounding backend (second fallback)

## Overview editing

Before editing `docs/fred_overview.md`, always run `bash scripts/snapshot_overview.sh` first. This archives the current overview before changes so the About page can show historical versions. Commit the snapshot alongside the overview edits.

## Code conventions

- Copyright header on every file: `# Pythia / Copyright (c) 2025 Kevin Wyjad`
- License: Pythia Non-Commercial Public License v1.0
- DuckDB is the only database backend (no Postgres/SQLite)
- `pythia/db/schema.py` is authoritative for Pythia tables; `resolver/db/schema.sql` for Resolver tables
- Config loaded via `pythia.config.load()` which reads `pythia/config.yaml`
- LLM providers abstracted through `forecaster/providers.py` (OpenAI, Google, Anthropic — xAI/Kimi/DeepSeek support removed 2026-07-07)
- All LLM calls logged to `llm_calls` table with cost, tokens, latency, error tracking. This includes RC/triage LLM passes (logged by providers module), grounding calls (logged explicitly in `_run_grounding_for_hazard` via `log_hs_llm_call` with `hazard_code=grounding_{hazard}`), and adversarial checks (logged by `web_research` module for evidence fetches and by providers module for synthesis calls).
- Env vars override config defaults; threshold env vars use `_env_float()` pattern
- Structured data connectors follow a standard pattern: `fetch_*()` → `store_*()` → `load_*()` → `format_*_for_prompt()` / `format_*_for_spd()`
- **Reasoning traces** are required in SPD output JSON for Track 1 (full ensemble). The `reasoning_trace` object captures the prior SPD, sequential evidence updates with numeric deltas, point estimate, and RC assessment. Track 2 requires only `prior` and `rc_assessment`. Traces are stored in `reasoning_trace_json` columns on `forecasts_raw` and `forecasts_ensemble`, validated by `forecaster/trace_validation.py` (diagnostic only), and analyzed by `generate_calibration_advice.py` for prior anchoring diagnostics.
- **Question-level web research pipeline is deprecated**: The `fetch_evidence_pack` / `_build_question_evidence_queries` / `_merge_question_evidence_packs` flow is bypassed. SPD prompts now receive structured data directly via `_load_structured_data()`: conflict forecasts, ReliefWeb, HDX Signals, HS grounding evidence (RC/triage grounding packs from `hs_hazard_tail_packs`, rendered as "HS GROUNDING EVIDENCE"), ACAPS, IPC, ACLED political, NMME, ENSO, seasonal TC, GDACS event history (FL/DR/TC), GDELT (ACE), CrisisWatch (ACE), adversarial checks (RC L1+). The `question_research` table is no longer populated by the pipeline (only placeholder rows). Env vars `PYTHIA_RETRIEVER_ENABLED`, `PYTHIA_WEB_RESEARCH_ENABLED`, `PYTHIA_SPD_WEB_SEARCH_ENABLED`, `PYTHIA_FORECASTER_SELF_SEARCH` are set to "0" in the workflow.
- HS pipeline is per-hazard: each hazard gets its own RC grounding, RC call, triage grounding, and triage call (single pass each by default on gemini-3.5-flash; `PYTHIA_HS_RC_PASSES`/`PYTHIA_HS_TRIAGE_PASSES=2` restore the 2-pass merge — only useful with a distinct pass-2 model)
- **Grounding backend order**: Brave Search API is the primary grounding backend (direct, deterministic, $5/1K queries); OpenAI GPT-4.1-mini web search is the first fallback; Gemini Google Search grounding is the second fallback. Override with `PYTHIA_GROUNDING_PRIMARY_BACKEND=openai` or `=gemini` to change the primary. All three backends are attempted in order until one returns sources. RC, triage, and adversarial grounding all use this three-tier chain. Triage grounding calls are logged to `llm_calls` with `hazard_code=TRIAGE_GROUNDING_{hazard}` (RC grounding logged as `grounding_{hazard}`).
- RC and triage grounding use different signal categories and recency windows (RC: TRIGGER/DAMPENER/BASELINE; triage: SITUATION/RESPONSE/FORECAST/VULNERABILITY)
- **Grounding recency windows**: RC grounding uses moderate windows (ACE=30d, DR=60d, FL=30d, HW=30d, TC=60d) balancing change signal detection with sufficient search coverage for a monthly forecast cycle. Triage grounding uses wider windows (ACE=60d, DR=90d, FL=60d, HW=60d, TC=60d) for the operational picture. Both are defined in their respective `RECENCY_DAYS` dicts.
- **Grounding source steering**: All 10 grounding prompts (5 RC + 5 triage) include a `PRIORITIZE THESE SOURCES` section with hazard-specific source priority lists and a `RECENCY FILTER` instruction. RC prompts prioritize wire services and specialist sources for novelty; triage prompts elevate OCHA/humanitarian sources for the operational picture.

## LLM credit-retry safety net

When LLM providers return billing/quota-exhaustion errors (distinct from transient rate limits), a credit-retry wrapper pauses and retries, waiting for auto-recharge. Implemented in `forecaster/providers.py` inside `call_chat_ms`, wrapping the existing transient-retry inner loop.

**Provider-specific config** (`_CREDIT_RETRY_CONFIG`):
- OpenAI: 900s pause, 3 retries (worst-case 45min)
- Anthropic: 300s pause, 3 retries (worst-case 15min)
- Google: 600s pause, 3 retries (worst-case 30min)
- Providers outside the table (none currently) get no credit retry

**Detection** (`_is_billing_error`): Conservative per-provider rules that distinguish billing errors from rate limits. OpenAI: 429 + quota/billing keywords but NOT "rate limit". Anthropic: 400/403 + insufficient/billing/blocked. Google: 429 + RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED + quota/billing. Returns False when uncertain.

**Env-var overrides**: `PYTHIA_CREDIT_RETRY_PAUSE_{PROVIDER}` (seconds), `PYTHIA_CREDIT_RETRY_MAX_{PROVIDER}` (count). E.g. `PYTHIA_CREDIT_RETRY_PAUSE_OPENAI=600` reduces OpenAI pause to 10 minutes.

**Usage tracking**: `credit_retries_used`, `credit_retry_pauses_sec`, `billing_error_detected` fields added to the usage dict when billing errors are detected.

## Brave Search circuit breaker

Thread-safe circuit breaker (`pythia/web_research/brave_circuit_breaker.py`) that tracks consecutive Brave API failures across all call sites. When 3 consecutive raw API calls return errors, the breaker trips and all subsequent Brave calls are short-circuited (return immediately with `circuit_breaker_tripped` error). The fallback chain (OpenAI → Gemini) takes over grounding.

**Safety gate**: When the breaker is tripped, `_write_hs_triage` checks whether each hazard received grounding evidence (via `_hazard_has_grounding()` querying `llm_calls`). Hazards without grounding are blocked from forecasting (`need_full_spd = False`, `data_quality.brave_budget_gate = "blocked_no_grounding"`). Hazards that completed grounding before the breaker tripped proceed normally.

**Lifecycle**: Breaker resets at the start of each HS run (`main()` → `reset_brave_breaker()`). Stats logged at run end. `HS_BRAVE_BREAKER_TRIPPED` emitted as workflow output.

## Known failure modes

Older post-mortems — bugs fixed in code that has since been rewritten, or whose current
behaviour is described in the reference sections above — live in
[`docs/archive/claude_md_failure_modes.md`](docs/archive/claude_md_failure_modes.md). **Every rule
they taught is in [`## Invariants`](#invariants) at the top of this file**; check both before
re-reporting an issue, and check `docs/audit_2026-07.md` too.

- **The canonical DB outgrew GitHub's release-asset limit, and the publish path deleted the working asset before finding out** (fixed, 2026-08-06): a routine publish of the interpreter backfill failed with `HTTP 422 ... size must be less than 2147483648` — the canonical DB had reached **3.0 GB**. Because `gh release upload --clobber` DELETES the existing asset before uploading the replacement, the `pythia-data-latest` release was left holding `manifest.json` and **no database at all**, with that manifest advertising a `db_sha256` for a file that no longer existed (so the API would sync a 404). Recovery was a re-publish from the 2026-08-01 Sibyl artifact, which restored the identical `db_sha256` (`905c1cbb…`). Two independent defects. **(1) Capacity**: the release carried the PA machine's raw source caches — `haz_raw_*` (ReliefWeb document bodies, IBTrACS tracks, GDACS payloads), which grew the canonical artifact from 42 MB to 336 MB *compressed* over the five nights after the machine was wired in on 2026-08-05. **Nothing in `pythia/api/**` or `web/src/**` reads a single `haz_*` table** — they are the machine's own working state, needed in the canonical ARTIFACT (which it resumes from) and pointless in the serving copy. **(2) Safety**: the publish validated nothing before the destructive step, so the first thing to discover the asset was unpublishable was the deletion itself. Fixed with `scripts/ci/build_release_db.py`, which runs before the sha, the regression guard and every upload: it copies the canonical DB into a fresh file minus `haz_raw_*` (two `COPY FROM DATABASE` passes — DROP alone leaves the freed blocks in the file), and **exits non-zero when the result still exceeds the limit, stopping the job with the existing asset untouched**. Measured on the real 2026-08-01 DB: 452.5 MB -> 280.3 MB in 9s with every served table intact. Note compaction ALONE was not a sufficient fix — 38% off 3.0 GB is ~1.9 GB, under today's ceiling but failing again within a month or two of nightly backcast growth. **Rules:** see the three new "Release publishing" lines in `## Invariants`. Tests: `scripts/ci/tests/test_build_release_db.py` (named in forecaster-ci, which now also triggers on `publish_latest_data.yml`).
- **The PA resolution machine shipped complete, tested, and entirely unrun — and its extraction path had five bugs that would have surfaced on the first real run** (fixed, 2026-08-05 audit of PRs #843–#849): the six build phases wired the machine into nothing — no workflow invoked any of its CLIs, the `haz_*` tables never existed in the canonical DB, `_load_haz_base_rate_block` silently rendered `""` on every production SPD prompt (its seam was a bare `except Exception: return ""` with no log line), extraction spend wrote no `llm_calls` row (the `log_call=False` docstring claimed a rich row existed; none did), and no diagnostic script knew a `haz_` table existed. Inside the extraction path: (1) `load_cached_extraction` served `status='error'` rows as cache hits — a transient outage or missing API key permanently blinded every document it touched, while the budget was charged for calls that never reached the provider; (2) the cache was keyed (doc, model, prompt_version) while the PROMPT varies by hazard/country — a typhoon-plus-flooding sitrep returned by both hazards' queries had one cell's answer silently served as the other's; (3) the prompt told the model to ignore "other time periods" without ever naming the period, and addressed countries by ISO3 code; (4) quote verification checked the quote against the body but never the VALUE against the quote — a fabricated number paired with any real sentence passed; (5) `resolve_triggered_cells` had no per-country guard and `cli.main`'s month loop caught return codes but not exceptions, so one country's error killed the month and every later month, directly contradicting the comment above the loop. Also: `haz_backcast_progress`'s `extraction_calls`/`extraction_cost_usd` columns were written from keys `month_counts` never produced (always 0 — the machine's only durable cost record), and `compute_severity` admitted provisional (pre-freeze, still-revisable) values into the severity quantiles. All fixed in the audit PR along with the wiring (resolver_update Phase 2.5 + nightly `haz_backcast.yml` + cost phase + diagnostics — see the machine section's "Production wiring" block). **Rules:** see the seven new PA-machine lines in `## Invariants`. Broader lesson, already an invariant but proven again: a feature that ships with no caller passes every test and does nothing — integration is part of the feature.

- **The canonical adapter contract dropped every connector's publication date, provenance and confidence** (partly fixed, 2026-08-04): `CANONICAL_COLUMNS` in `resolver/transform/adapters/base.py` had 12 entries, `normalize._write_canonical` writes exactly `frame.loc[:, CANONICAL_COLUMNS]`, and `load_and_derive._read_canonical_dir` **dropped every column outside its own copy of that list** — so anything an adapter knew beyond those 12 fields died at the normalize seam. For IFRC that meant `publication_date`, `publisher`, `method`, `confidence`, `source_url` and `doc_title` were all discarded, and `load_and_derive` then set `publication_date = as_of_date` and `confidence = None`. Because `IFRCAdapter` snaps `as_of_date` to month-end, **every IFRC row in a month ended up with an identical publication_date**, which is what left the revision tiebreak (entry below) with nothing to order by — the two bugs compounded, and fixing the sort alone would have been inert. `publication_date` is now part of the canonical contract (adapters with no distinct publication date emit `""`; `load_and_derive` falls back to `as_of_date` when it is blank) and is carried through as an optional passthrough rather than dropped. Verified end-to-end: two August IFRC records for PHL/TC collapse to the 80,000 figure published 2025-08-19, not the 5,000 first reported on 2025-08-04. **Still open: `method`/`confidence` are lost at the same seam**, which is why the three `affected` provenance branches (real `num_affected`, appeal beneficiaries, derived casualty sum) are indistinguishable at resolution time — see `docs/montandon_assessment.md` §4. **Rule: an adapter can only carry what `CANONICAL_COLUMNS` lists — check that list before assuming connector metadata survives to `facts_resolved`.** Tests: `resolver/tests/transform/test_normalize_ifrc.py::test_publication_date_survives_the_month_end_snap`.
- **`resolve_sources` kept the FIRST field report of a country-month and discarded every later revision** (fixed, 2026-08-04): `resolver/transform/resolve_sources.py` sorted ascending on every column and then took `drop_duplicates(..., keep="first")`, so within a dedup group the winner was the row with the *earliest* `publication_date`. Because `IFRCAdapter` snaps `as_of_date` to month-end and `as_of_date` is in `_REQUIRED_GROUP_COLS`, **every IFRC GO record for a country-hazard-metric-month lands in one group** — so the initial field report, filed when least was known, beat every later revision of the same disaster. Field reports are progressive: a flood first reported at 5,000 affected is routinely revised to 80,000 a fortnight later, and this silently took the 5,000 as PA ground truth. It also directly contradicted `resolver/tools/precedence_config.yml`, which has declared `tiebreak: [as_of_desc, value_nonnull, source_alpha]` all along — the repo's two precedence implementations disagreed, and the one that actually runs on IFRC was the wrong one. Fixed by sorting `provenance_rank ASC, value-is-null ASC, publication_date DESC, source ASC` so the tiebreak reads: best source tier, then a real value, then the most recent publication. **Deliberately NOT changed: the collapse still yields one row per group, so a country-month holding genuinely distinct events keeps only one of them rather than summing.** Choosing between sum and max needs evidence on how often multi-event months occur versus revision chains — do not guess it. **Rule: when collapsing revisions of one fact, the latest informed report wins — `drop_duplicates(keep="first")` after an all-ascending sort keeps the earliest.** Tests: `resolver/tests/test_resolve_sources_revision_tiebreak.py` (two of its four cases fail against the old sort; the other two pin the behaviour that was already correct — source priority outranking recency, and a null value never winning on recency alone).
- **GDACS modelled exposure was served to models as IFRC reported-affected history** (fixed, 2026-08-04): `_build_natural_hazard_seasonal_profile` (`forecaster/cli.py`) — the live base-rate builder for natural-hazard PA, reached from `_build_history_summary` for FL/DR/TC — queried `lower(metric) IN ('affected', 'in_need', 'pa')` with **no publisher filter** and hardcoded `"source": "IFRC"` into the result the prompt renders. GDACS writes `in_need` for exactly FL/DR/TC, and it is **modelled population exposure** (hazard footprint × population), not reported impact: on the test fixture an IFRC figure of 42,000 sits beside a GDACS figure of 8,400,000 for the same country-hazard-month. Meanwhile `compute_resolutions._try_facts_resolved` filters PA on `('affected','people_affected','pa','displaced')` — **`in_need` is excluded** — so the model was anchored on a series the resolver would never score it against, and the mismatch was worst on the hazards where IFRC coverage is thinnest. Not noise: because a larger event is likelier to attract an IFRC report, blending the two makes the scale of the target change *as a function of severity*, which corrupts the upper buckets systematically. Fixed by single-sourcing the metric list as `compute_resolutions.PA_FACTS_RESOLVED_METRICS` (consumed via `history_loaders._pa_metric_in_clause`, defensively imported with a literal fallback), reporting the publishers actually behind the rows instead of asserting IFRC, and guarding both facts_resolved and facts_deltas filters with `_metric_in_clause`. The same leak was fixed in `_load_ifrc_pa_history`, which is currently unreached (its only caller `_load_pa_history_block` is never invoked) and additionally used the forbidden raw read-only `duckdb.connect` and a `LIMIT` that counted rows rather than months. **Rule: a question's base rate must be drawn from the same source class and quantity definition that will resolve it — precedence may operate within a definitional class, never across classes.** Tests: `forecaster/tests/test_base_rate_matches_resolution_source.py` (wired into forecaster-ci; fails on both the subset check and the end-to-end profile check when reverted). Background: `docs/montandon_assessment.md`.
- **The poller's cron is unreliable at ignition, and stage 4 threw away the one post-run diagnostic that mattered** (fixed, 2026-08-03): two workflow gaps found reviewing the 2026-08-01 run. (1) **Ignition latency.** `poll_llm_batches.yml` asks for `*/15` but GitHub throttles it hard — hs_submit emitted its batch state at 02:59:17 and the next SCHEDULED tick was 03:50:27, a **3h38m gap between ticks** (previous tick 00:12:32), 51 minutes of which was dead pipeline time. Once ignited the 2026-07-29 self-re-arm chain performed exactly as designed (dispatches at 03:55, 04:01, 04:06 … 04:56, every ~5.5 min, terminating correctly), and the S3→S4 40-minute gap was genuine SPD batch latency polled 8 times — so **the chain is reliable and only ignition is fragile**. Every submit-side stage now dispatches `poll_llm_batches.yml` itself right after uploading its batch state (`gh workflow run … -f chain_depth=0`, signature-gated, `continue-on-error`), which also bypasses the poller's activity gate. The cron stays as the backstop and a duplicate tick is harmless because collects are idempotent. **Do not remove the cron** — it is what recovers a pipeline whose ignition dispatch failed. (2) **`post_run__fc_collect_finalize.txt` did not exist.** S1–S3 tee `post_run_diagnostics.py` to a file and upload it; S4 ran it bare, with `set -e` and no `continue-on-error`, and its upload `path:` omitted `post_run__*.txt` — so for the ONLY stage that makes SPD/binary calls, the prompt-cache and forecast-coverage check lived solely in job logs, which expire. Now teed, uploaded and non-fatal like its siblings. S4's `stage_health` also now receives the `--fc-run-id` the stage already resolves from `llm_batches` (it was passed to `batch_economics` but not `stage_health`, which therefore reported `fc_run_id: ""`). **Rule: any diagnostic that exists only in a job log does not exist — logs expire, artifacts are the record.**
- **49 of 122 countries were reported "degraded" because ACE was deliberately skipped** (fixed, 2026-08-03): the 2026-08-01 run's `hs_triage_coverage__*.csv` showed `final_status=degraded` for 49 countries and `HS_TRIAGE_DEGRADED_ISO3S` named every one for rerun — while all 261 triage LLM calls had succeeded and the DB held zero `error` rows (statuses were exactly `ok` 261 / `seasonal_skip` 125 / `rc_promoted` 53 / `acled_low_activity` 49 — and 49 is the match). `run_triage_for_country` built its hazard list TWICE: `hazards_to_ground` excluded `(hz == "ACE" and ace_low_activity)`, the `active_statuses` roll-up did not. So the deliberate ACE low-activity skip contributed status `"acled_low_activity"` — neither `ok` nor `error` — and `combine` fell through to `degraded`. Rather than re-sync the duplicate filter, the selection is now a single named helper `_scored_hazards(expected_hazards, active, rc_promoted, ace_low_activity)` used by **both** call sites, with the roll-up itself extracted as `combine_hazard_statuses` — the two can no longer drift. **Rule: a skip is not a failure; any status roll-up must run over the same list that decided what to actually do.** Two reporting fixes rode along: the coverage CSV / failures JSON columns `pass1_status`/`pass2_status` now emit as `rc_status`/`triage_status` (they have not meant "two passes of one model" since RC and triage were split — the internal dict keys are left alone as historical), and `stage_health`'s `rc_levels` now reports `n_assessed` / `n_not_assessed` / `by_level_assessed` alongside the raw `by_level`, because folding the 125 seasonal screen-outs (level 0 by default, never assessed) into level 0 made it report 435 where the debug bundle correctly said 310. Verified against the real run DB: 488 rows = 363 assessed (310/38/14/1) + 125 screen-outs. Tests: `horizon_scanner/tests/test_hs_triage_status.py`, plus two `rc_levels` cases in `scripts/ci/tests/test_stage_health.py`.
- **The LLM prompt artifact rendered only the FIRST hazard; DR/FL/TC reported "no triage data" against 122 rows each** (fixed, 2026-08-03): on the 2026-08-01 run, `diagnostics/prompt_artifact.md` contained a complete ACE section and then `_No HS triage data found for DR; skipping._` for DR, FL and TC — while `hs_triage` held 122 rows for every one of them. Same class as the 2026-07-13 `questions.created_at` fix and the shared-connection footgun documented with it, but a different mechanism: the artifact reopened its connection **exactly once, inside the SPD block**, and then carried that assumption into the next hazard. The forecaster/pythia sub-loaders reached from `_load_structured_data_for_artifact` close AND evict the shared `duckdb_io` handle (and leave their own pooled handles open on the same path), so a connection that was healthy through the ACE SPD render was dead by the DR lookup — where `_load_sample_country_for_hazard`'s bare `except Exception: return None` turned "Connection already closed" into "this hazard has no data". Net effect: **no visibility into the DR, FL or TC prompts at all.** Production injection was unaffected — verified from `llm_calls`, where 262/262 ACE SPD prompts carried HS grounding + conflict forecasts, 252 GDELT, 240 CrisisWatch. Fixed with `_ensure_live(con, db_url)` (a `SELECT 1` probe that reopens on failure) called at the **top of every hazard iteration** as well as after the structured-data load, plus a `LOG.warning` in the sample-country loader so a dead connection can never again masquerade as missing data. `HW` was also dropped from `ACTIVE_HAZARDS` — it is in `BLOCKED_HAZARDS`, so it could only ever emit a skip line. **Rule: never assume a `duckdb_io` handle survives a call into forecaster/pythia loader code — re-acquire it, don't reopen once and hope.** Note the pre-existing `test_connect_reopens_after_shared_connection_closed` passed throughout: it proved the reopen MECHANISM, not the loop invariant, which is exactly why the regression survived it. Tests: `scripts/ci/tests/test_snapshot_prompt_artifact.py` (the new loop test fails with "Connection already closed" for DR/FL/TC when the top-of-loop guard is removed).
- **A single `+0.25` in a delta array silently destroyed a whole SPD forecast** (fixed, 2026-08-03): on the 2026-08-01 production run, 8 of 345 SPD member calls returned complete, well-formed 6-month forecasts, were billed in full, and were then discarded whole — because the model wrote an explicit unary plus in `reasoning_trace.updates[].delta` (`[-0.07, -0.13, +0.25]`). JSON has no unary plus, so `json.loads` rejected the ENTIRE object including a perfectly good `spds` block, and `_safe_json_loads` (`forecaster/cli.py`) had no numeric repair. The failure was invisible from every angle: `llm_calls` recorded `status='ok'` with tokens and cost for all 8, the v2 member call site swallowed the exception with a bare `except Exception: continue`, and the only trace was 8 `no_forecast` rows in `forecasts_raw` — so 8 Track-1 questions quietly ran on 4 ensemble members instead of 5. `_safe_json_loads` now has a **last-resort** repair pass (`strip_unary_plus_outside_strings`): a string-aware scan that drops a `+` only where it sits in value position (directly after `[`, `,` or `:`) and prefixes a digit, and rewrites `+.5` to `0.5` (dropping the `+` alone would leave `.5`, which JSON also rejects). It runs only after the normal whole-string and first-`{...}`-block attempts both fail, so well-formed responses parse byte-identically to before, and a `+` inside a string value is never touched. Verified by replaying all 596 real SPD/binary responses from the 2026-08-01 run: 596/596 parse, including all 8 previously-lost members, each recovering a full 6-month SPD. The member call site now logs a `[warn]` naming the question, model and response length instead of discarding silently, and the SPD prompt tells models to write positive numbers plainly. **Rule: a parse failure downstream of a billed LLM call must be logged — `llm_calls.status='ok'` means the HTTP call succeeded, not that the output was used.** A bare `.5`/`-.5` (no plus) is a different malformation and is deliberately out of scope. Tests: `forecaster/tests/test_spd_json_repair.py` (wired into forecaster-ci's SPD step).
- **A transient archive.org refusal silently cost a whole CrisisWatch edition** (fixed, 2026-08-03): the first CI execution of the Wayback path (scheduled run 10:05 UTC, the workflow's cron fires hours late like every scheduled workflow in this repo) proved the chain works end-to-end — SPN capture, CDX re-poll, gzip download, validation, parse, 85 country entries. But it still produced JUNE data. `_wait_for_new_snapshot` indexed the fresh 3-Aug capture, then `_fetch_snapshot_html` got `Connection refused` three times, and the run fell back to walking existing snapshots — landing on 20 July, which necessarily predates the July edition (`outlook=July 2026, report=June 2026`). It then went green. **A freshly-created SPN capture is routinely INDEXED before it is SERVABLE, and falling back is not equivalent to one more retry: older snapshots carry older monthly EDITIONS.** (Note the same run's walk-back also failed twice before succeeding on its third attempt — the refusals are plainly transient.) `_fetch_snapshot_html` is now split into `_download_snapshot_html` + `_accept_snapshot_html` so the two failure modes are distinguishable, and the fresh-capture path is far more patient than the walk (`_FRESH_CAPTURE_ATTEMPTS=6`, `_FRESH_CAPTURE_BACKOFF_SEC=20`) — but ONLY on download failure. A capture that downloads and then fails validation is Cloudflare-challenged and will never improve, so it falls back immediately rather than burning six attempts. Latency is bounded by `_FRESH_CAPTURE_TIMEOUT_SEC=45` (worst case 6x45s + 5x20s = 370s) and the job timeout was raised 20 -> 30 min for headroom. **Rule: when a fallback silently changes WHICH edition of a monthly publication you get, it is not a retry — treat it as data loss and be patient first.** Tests: `tests/test_refresh_crisiswatch.py` (the patient-retry test fails with "walked back to the older snapshot ... this is the edition-losing regression" when the fix is reverted).
- **Three diagnostics that were technically true and practically misleading** (fixed, 2026-08-03, from the 2026-08-01 run review): (1) **"31.0% of spend at full price"** in the debug bundle's Batch Economics row read as lost discount, while `batch_economics.py` — which owns the real figure — correctly reported `lost_discount_cost_usd: 0.00` for the same run. The bundle's `(total − batch_tier) / total` counts search-API spend (Brave, $9.79) and the never-batchable synchronous phases (scenarios, adversarial synthesis) as "full price". Renamed `full_price_pct_of_spend` → `non_batch_tier_pct_of_spend` and reworded to "not batch-eligible: search APIs + synchronous phases". **The bundle reports batch-tier SHARE; batch_economics reports LOST DISCOUNT — do not conflate them.** (2) **The latency table read `p50 = p95 = 0.00` for every SPD and binary model.** A batch replay records `elapsed_ms = 0` (the provider worked asynchronously; there is no client-side duration), so on a 100%-batched run the table described nothing. `llm_latency_summary` now computes percentiles over SYNCHRONOUS calls only, adds an `n_batched` column, and renders `—` where a group was entirely batched — leaving `scenario_v2` (the one genuinely sync phase) as the only row with numbers. Guarded on `usage_json` existing. (3) **CrisisWatch's `--max-age-days 60` cannot see a single missed monthly edition.** It measures the age of the edition's END date: on 2026-08-03 the on-disk June edition was 34 days old and sailed past the threshold while the July edition was simply absent. New `--expect-edition-by-day` (set to 7 in `refresh-crisiswatch.yml`) asks the question that matters — is the previous complete month's edition present? — warns inside the grace period (ICG publishes in the first days of the month; the cron fires on the 3rd/5th/7th/10th) and turns the run red after it. **Rule: on a monthly publication, staleness must be measured in EDITIONS, not days.** Separately noted while investigating: `refresh-crisiswatch.yml` reports `state: active` with a valid cron but has produced **no run since 2026-04-03** (its last 6 runs all failed), so the July 2026 Wayback path has never actually executed in CI — the checked-in JSON was produced manually during #791. Dispatch it manually to confirm the CI path. Also documented (no behaviour change): the binary writer's 5-bucket padding is a deliberate constant, not an SPD bucket count — `EVENT_OCCURRENCE` is absent from `BUCKET_SPECS`, `compute_scores` reads `bucket_index = 1` only, and the SPD `{1..K}` bucket-set guard must never be applied to those rows.
- **A degraded API blanked the whole homepage, and that blank was misread as data loss** (fixed, 2026-08-03): `web/src/app/page.tsx` fetched `/v1/version` and `/v1/diagnostics/kpi_scopes` inside a bare `await Promise.all([...])` while the sibling `/risk_index` and `/countries` calls were already try/caught. `apiGet` throws on any non-2xx, and **`/v1/version` returns 503 whenever DB sync is misconfigured or wedged** (`_ensure_read_connection` raises `DB not available yet` / the release-tag guard) — so one degraded dependency rejected the entire server component and the dashboard rendered as an error page. That is indistinguishable from "there is no data", which is exactly how the 2026-08-01 publish/sync lag was read as the pipeline having produced nothing. Now `Promise.allSettled` with null-safe fallbacks (`{}` for version, an empty `available_months`/`scopes` shape for kpi_scopes — every field is optional downstream, so the selectors render empty and `RiskIndexPanel` falls back to its month-window branch), plus an amber **"Live data unavailable"** banner naming what failed and pointing at `/v1/health` (which already reports `{"status":"degraded","reason":"db_sync_failing"}` with sync detail). **Rule: on the dashboard, a soft-fail must never look identical to "no data" — degrade visibly.** Verified end-to-end against the real published DB with `/v1/version` 503ing: page returns HTTP 200 with the banner instead of a 500.
- **run_summary's Sibyl block had no test filter and fell back to the newest row in the table** (fixed, 2026-08-03): `diagnostics.py`'s Sibyl query appended `WHERE hs_run_id = ?` **only when `hs_run_id` was truthy** and never applied `is_test`. So a Test-OFF view with no production run returned `has_run=false` and an all-zero KPI row *next to a fully populated Sibyl cost block belonging to some other, possibly test, run* — the same leak class the cost block was fixed for in July. Now the block requires `hs_run_id` (no run id → no Sibyl block) and both `sibyl_runs` and `sibyl_forecasts` are test-filtered. Tests: `tests/test_api_run_summary.py::test_run_summary_test_{off,on}_*_sibyl_run`.
- **`core._test_filter` had no column guard, so a missing `is_test` zeroed the dashboard ONLY with Test OFF** (hardened, 2026-08-03): the helper emits `AND COALESCE(is_test, FALSE) = FALSE` whether or not the table has the column, its two siblings (`app._probe_test_filter`, `core._run_filter_cte`) both guard, and it was spliced into `diagnostics.py`'s `available_months` plus the `tf_q`/`tf_ht`/`tf_lc` clauses feeding ~25 queries whose exceptions are swallowed by `_q`/`_q1`. Against a schema missing the column those queries RAISE, get swallowed, and return 0/[] — but only when Test is OFF, because `include_test=true` makes the clause empty and the identical query binds fine. New `core._test_filter_for(con, table, include_test, alias)` returns "" when the column is absent; every call site in `routes/diagnostics.py` now uses it, and `_scope_from_questions` (which had ignored `include_test` entirely, so "Total active"/"Total active + inactive" always counted test rows) now threads it through `questions`/`resolutions`/`forecasts_ensemble`/`llm_calls`. **The other route modules (`risk_index.py`, `performance.py`, `costs.py`, `questions.py`, `forecasts.py`, `sibyl.py`) still use the unguarded helper — deliberately: they let the exception surface as a visible 500 rather than silently zeroing. Use `_test_filter_for` for anything that swallows query errors.** Tests: `tests/test_api_test_toggle.py::test_test_filter_for_is_empty_when_the_column_is_absent`.
- **The dashboard reported "6 / 5" ensemble models — Sibyl counted as a member** (fixed, 2026-08-03): the Track-1 distinct-model count in `diagnostics.py` excluded `ensemble_%` and `track2_%` but not `sibyl`, which writes its pooled SPD into `forecasts_raw` under `model_name='sibyl'` **on the same Track 1 questions**. Now excluded via `AGGREGATE_MODEL_NAMES` imported (defensively, with a literal fallback) from `pythia/tools/compute_calibration_pythia.py`, so the exclusion list stays single-sourced with the calibration weight softmax; the query also gained the `fr`/`q` test filters it was missing. Confirmed against the 2026-08-01 DB: 5 real members + `sibyl` → now reports 5/5.

- **A test run's grounding packs were written as production data** (fixed, 2026-07-29): `horizon_scanner/db_writer.py::log_hs_hazard_tail_packs_to_db` took `is_test: bool = False` and **neither production caller passed it** (`triage.py` triage grounding, `regime_change_llm.py` RC grounding), so every `hs_hazard_tail_packs` row was stamped `is_test=FALSE` regardless of `PYTHIA_TEST_MODE`. Caught by the new `stage_health` is_test check on its first production run (10 packs FALSE while hs_runs/hs_triage/llm_calls/hs_adversarial_checks were all TRUE). Same defect class as the `log_forecaster_llm_call` gap fixed 2026-07-13, and the same remedy: **all five HS writers now take `is_test: bool | None = None` and inherit `is_test_mode()` when the caller omits it** — an explicit argument still wins. **Rule: an `is_test` parameter must default to None-and-inherit, never to `False`; a `= False` default converts a forgetful caller into silent production data.** Tests: `resolver/tests/test_hs_hazard_tail_packs_schema_and_writer.py` — note that file lives under `resolver/tests` but guards `horizon_scanner/`, and `resolver-ci-fast.yml` does NOT trigger on `horizon_scanner/**`, so it is named explicitly in `horizon-scanner-ci.yml`.
- **An OpenAI batch returned zero results and the reason was unrecoverable** (instrumented, 2026-07-29): on pipeline `hs_20260729T071137` both OpenAI batches collected as `{'succeeded': 0, 'errored': 0, 'expired': 12/4}` while anthropic and google collected cleanly. Every OpenAI request fell back to synchronous full-price calls — and because the OpenAI members are the expensive ones (`gpt-5.6-sol` alone $1.43), that was **67% of forecaster spend paying full price**, not the 32%-of-requests the fallback-rate warning implied. `_OpenAIBatch.fetch` yields nothing when the batch payload carries neither `output_file_id` nor `error_file_id`, `collect_batch` then sweeps every still-`submitted` row to `expired`, and the batch was recorded as `status='collected'` — the provider's terminal status and error payload were never persisted, so the cause could not be recovered afterwards. `collect_batch` now calls `_record_empty_batch` whenever a batch ends with zero successes and ≥1 expired item: it re-polls the provider, writes the state/counts/detail into `llm_batches.error_text`, logs at ERROR and emits a `::warning::`; `batch_economics.py` renders those rows and warns per empty batch. **The helper is fully guarded and must stay that way — it runs mid-collect, and losing the explanation is far better than losing the forecasts.** **Rule: a batch that yields nothing must record the provider's terminal state; "collected" with zero results is indistinguishable from success in every downstream view.** **Root cause found and fixed 2026-07-30** (the instrumentation did its job on its first reproduction): **OpenAI rejects a batch containing more than one model, wholesale, at validation** — `code=mismatched_model`, "Each batch must contain requests for a single model". The ensemble has TWO OpenAI members (`gpt-5.6-sol`, `gpt-5.6-luna`), and `submit_pending` grouped batches by `(provider, model_id if provider == "google" else None)` — per-model for Gemini only, on the reasoning that "OpenAI/Anthropic batches carry the model per item". Carrying the model per item is true but does not license MIXING models, so every OpenAI batch since the staged pipeline began contained both members and was refused entirely, producing `request_counts={total: 0, completed: 0, failed: 0}` with **no `error_file_id`** (there are no per-request results when nothing is ingested). Fixed by grouping **always** per `(provider, model_id)`; Anthropic does allow mixed models but is grouped per model too, which is a no-op today and removes the trap for whoever adds a second Anthropic member. Two companion fixes made the diagnosis possible and repeatable: `_OpenAIBatch.poll` now records the batch object's **`errors`** list (code/message/line) in its detail — that field is the ONLY place a validation failure is reported, and dropping it is why two separate occurrences were unrecoverable — and `scripts/ci/inspect_openai_batch.py` dumps a batch object in full (errors, counts, input-file status/bytes/first lines), wired into `poll_llm_batches.yml` behind the `inspect_batches` / `inspect_recent` dispatch inputs because reading a retained batch needs the API key that only CI holds. That diagnostic path skips polling and the re-arm step keys off the poll step's output, so it cannot dispatch a pipeline stage as a side effect. **Rule: any provider batch must contain exactly one model — group by `(provider, model_id)`, never by provider alone.** Tests: `TestEmptyBatchDiagnosis` and `test_submit_never_mixes_models_in_one_openai_batch` in `pythia/tests/test_llm_batch.py` (the latter asserts single-model-ness from the request bodies actually handed to the adapter, which is what the provider validates, not from the grouping key).
- **The poller's self-chain died 6 hours before the ceiling it exists to outlive** (fixed, 2026-07-31): `POLLER_MAX_CHAIN` was hardcoded to 200 under a comment asserting "at the workflow's ~7-minute cadence, 200 links is a little over 24h, which is already past `PYTHIA_BATCH_MAX_WAIT_H` — so a healthy pipeline can never reach it". **Both halves were wrong.** 200 x 7min = 23.3h, already UNDER the 24h ceiling; and the real cadence is `sleep 300` + ~25s of job = **~5.4 min**, giving **18.0h**. The chain exists precisely so the poller survives until `PYTHIA_BATCH_MAX_WAIT_H` cancels stragglers and dispatches the collect stage — so a cap shorter than the ceiling kills the chain while batches are still legitimately in flight. Observed on run `hs_20260730T072840`, whose two `gpt-5.6-sol` batches ran >18h (the other 8 finished in minutes): the poller emitted `rearm=false, "chain cap reached (200/200)"` at **exactly `age_hours 18.16`**, with both batches still `in_progress` and ~6h of ceiling left, and the pipeline parked. It is recoverable — the hourly `schedule` tick re-ignites a fresh chain at depth 0, and the activity gate passes because the batch-state artifact is well inside 72h — but a 5-minute hand-off silently becomes an up-to-1-hour stall. **Aggravating detail: while the chain IS running, scheduled cron ticks are cancelled** — self-dispatches land in the `poll-llm-batches` concurrency group every ~5.4 min and a queued cron run gets cancelled by the next one (observed: the 01:11:58Z `schedule` run, `conclusion=cancelled`). So the cron backstop only functions once the chain has already stopped. Fixed by **deriving** the cap from the ceiling instead of asserting a relationship to it: `_max_chain()` returns `max(200, int(wait_h*60/POLLER_TICK_MINUTES * 1.5) + 1)` (393 links = 36h at a 24h ceiling; 786 = 72h at 48h), with an explicit `POLLER_MAX_CHAIN` still winning as an operator hard-stop. **Rule: `POLLER_MAX_CHAIN x POLLER_TICK_MINUTES` must always exceed `PYTHIA_BATCH_MAX_WAIT_H` — never hardcode the cap, and never raise the wait ceiling without re-deriving it.** Guarded by `tests/test_poll_llm_batches_guard.py::test_chain_cap_always_outlives_the_batch_wait_ceiling`, parameterised over 12/24/36/48h (the old hardcoded 200 fails at 24h and above). **Do not "fix" slow batches by lowering `PYTHIA_BATCH_MAX_WAIT_H`** — batching `gpt-5.6-sol` is the single largest saving in the pipeline (59% of forecaster spend, ~$0.0985/question of the ~$0.1665/question total), and at monthly cadence a multi-hour wait is the correct trade.
- **The batch-economics reporter blamed providers for spend that was never batchable** (fixed, 2026-07-30): `batch_economics.py` classified spend by PROVIDER — every dollar a provider spent outside a batch counted as lost discount, as long as that provider had created at least one batch in the pipeline. But a provider's spend spans families the staged pipeline never batches at all: grounding, scenario writing, adversarial synthesis. On the 2026-07-30 SOM run it therefore reported **google losing 41.6% of batchable spend when none of that spend was batchable in the first place** — a headline that looks like a serious regression and is not actionable, in the one report whose entire job is to say whether the discount is being collected. Fixed by classifying on the **call family** instead (`_batchable_sql()`): `spd_v2` / `binary_v2` matched by `phase`, `hs_rc` / `hs_triage` by `call_type LIKE 'rc_pass_%'` / `'triage_pass_%'`, with a fallback for rows written before the `call_type` column existed. Everything else is reported under a new `non_batchable_cost_usd` key rather than folded into the loss. **Rule: "lost discount" must be measured against what this pipeline actually batches — a provider is not a unit of batchability, a call family is.**
- **The workflow sanity checks were never triggered by workflow changes** (fixed, 2026-07-30 audit): `resolver/tests/test_ci_shell_sanity.py` guards `.github/workflows/**` (shell `A && B || C` if/else, `[[ ]]` spacing, `echo -e`, upload-artifact `path:` list form) but lives in `resolver/tests/`, and `resolver-ci-fast.yml` has no `.github/workflows/**` path trigger — so a workflow-only PR never ran them, and #814's prose comment landed a (false-positive) violation on main that stayed invisible until an unrelated PR touched a resolver path. `ci-lint.yml` (which runs on every PR) now runs that file directly; the check itself was also taught to skip comment lines. **Rule: a lint that guards directory A must run when directory A changes — check the path filters of the workflow that owns the test, not just where the test file sits.**
- **A test guards the directory it IMPORTS from, not the directory it lives in — and ten of them were wired to nothing** (fixed + made self-enforcing, 2026-07-29): three separate incidents share one cause. `test_ci_shell_sanity.py` guarded `.github/workflows/**` from `resolver/tests/` (entry above); `test_idmc_history_conflict_pa.py` guarded forecaster's ACE/PA base rate and **sat red on `main` for days** after #816 re-routed it; `test_hs_hazard_tail_packs_schema_and_writer.py` guarded `horizon_scanner/db_writer.py`. The structural gap: **`resolver-ci-fast.yml` runs all of `resolver/tests/` but triggers only on `resolver/**`**, so a PR touching only `forecaster/`, `horizon_scanner/` or `pythia/` ran **none** of it, while `ci-lint.yml` runs on every PR but executes exactly one file. Twelve files under `resolver/tests/` import a non-resolver package; two were wired, ten were not — all passing at the time, i.e. a pure blind spot rather than a live failure. Fixed by naming each in **both** the `paths:` trigger and the pytest call of the workflow owning the code it guards (`horizon_scanner` → `horizon-scanner-ci.yml`; `forecaster`/`pythia` → `forecaster-ci.yml`; dual-owner files in both — duplicate execution is cheap, a gap is not). For the three `pythia`-guarding files the added triggers are deliberately **narrow** (`pythia/buckets.py`, `pythia/api/app.py`, `pythia/api/core.py`, `pythia/api/routes/risk_index.py`) — **do not blanket-add `pythia/**`**, which would fire forecaster-ci on nearly every PR. **Because hand-wiring is what failed three times, this is now self-enforcing**: `test_ci_shell_sanity.py::test_cross_package_resolver_tests_are_wired_into_a_workflow` scans every `resolver/tests/test_*.py` for top-level imports of `forecaster`/`horizon_scanner`/`pythia`/`sibyl` and asserts the path appears in some workflow's `paths:` **and** its pytest invocation (quoted `- "path"` = trigger, bare `path` = invocation), failing with the file, the packages, and the workflow to add it to. It is stdlib-only (no `yaml`) because `ci-lint.yml` installs just pytest and runs it with `--noconftest`. **Rule: when adding a test under `resolver/tests/` that imports another package, wire it into that package's workflow in the same PR — the guard will fail the PR otherwise. Known blind spot: nothing runs `pythia/tests/` broadly — forecaster-ci names an explicit file list, so 8 of the 9 `pythia/tests/test_api_*.py` files (all except `test_api_llm_costs.py`) still run in no workflow.**
- **workflow_dispatch BOOLEAN inputs are not reliably typed when a stage is dispatched over the REST API** (hardened, 2026-07-30 audit): `pythia_pipeline_stage.yml`'s stages 2..N are launched by `poll_llm_batches.py` via `gh workflow run -f`, and a boolean input can arrive as the STRING `"false"`. `${{ inputs.test_mode && '1' || '0' }}` treats any non-empty string as truthy, so a production month would be stamped `is_test` end-to-end (invisible on the dashboard, excluded from calibration); `${{ !inputs.disable_brave && secrets.X || '' }}` has the mirror-image failure — `!"false"` is false, withholding the Brave key from every poller-dispatched stage. Both expressions now test the boolean AND the string form explicitly. **Rule: in `pythia_pipeline_stage.yml`, never write a bare truthiness test on a boolean input — compare against `true` and `'true'` (or `!= true && != 'true'`), because the poller, not the UI, launches most stages.**
- **A staged pipeline whose submit stage created zero provider batches died at the next stage** (fixed, 2026-07-30 audit, CRITICAL): `hs_rc_collect`/`hs_finalize` resolved their `hs_run_id` **only** from `llm_batches`, which gets a row only when a provider batch is actually CREATED. If every submit fails — a provider outage, a rejected batch payload (the Gemini `batchGenerateContent` adapter is explicitly documented as not yet exercised against the live service), or `PYTHIA_BATCH_PROVIDERS` excluding the RC/triage provider — `hs_submit` still goes green (its rows stay `pending`, which is the *designed* degradation to the sync path) and emits a batch state with `pending: []`, so the poller immediately dispatches `hs_rc_collect`, which then `SystemExit`s with "No hs_run_id found in llm_batches". Three poller attempts later the pipeline is STALLED and the whole month is lost. Fixed by writing a durable pipeline→hs_run_id row to `hs_stage_state` at hs_submit (`hs_batch.save_pipeline_run_id`, before any model work) and falling back to it in `resolve_run_id_for_pipeline` — the collect stages then run every model call synchronously (full price, loud warning) instead of not running at all. **Rule: a batch-pipeline stage must never depend on batch-submission SUCCESS to find its own identity — the sync fallback is only a fallback if the stage can still start.** Tests: `test_pipeline_run_id_resolves_without_any_llm_batches_row`, `test_llm_batches_wins_over_stage_state_for_run_id`.
- **The poller dropped every run-shaping dispatch input, so a smoke run became a full production run at stage 2** (fixed, 2026-07-30 audit): `pythia_pipeline_stage.yml` gained `batch_providers` / `only_countries` / `grounding_primary` / `disable_brave` inputs for smoke runs, but `scripts/ci/poll_llm_batches.py` dispatched the next stage with only `stage`/`pipeline_id`/`db_run_id`/`test_mode` — so a 2-country Gemini-only Brave-disabled smoke run silently reverted to 122 countries, all three providers and a live Brave key from `hs_rc_collect` onwards (and a 402ing Brave key re-armed the breaker, which blocks ungrounded hazards from forecasting). `emit_batch_state` now records the stage's EFFECTIVE settings as `dispatch_inputs` (read back out of the env the workflow set; `disable_brave` is inferred from an empty `BRAVE_SEARCH_API_KEY`, which is how the workflow implements it) and the poller re-supplies them. Absent-field states dispatch exactly as before. **Rule: every workflow input that shapes a stage's cost or scope must be carried in the batch-state artifact — the poller, not a human, launches stages 2..N.** Tests: `tests/test_poll_llm_batches_guard.py`.
- **`_table_exists` answered about the CONNECTION, not the table — calibration could still silently no-op on DuckDB ≥ 1.5** (fixed, 2026-07-30 audit): the shared helpers in `pythia/tools/_db_utils.py` caught the aborted-transaction error, issued `ROLLBACK`, and then returned the FALLBACK for that call — so the scrub only helped the NEXT caller. `compute_calibration_pythia` reads `_table_exists(conn, "scores")` exactly once and skips the entire run on `False`, which is precisely the documented DuckDB 1.5.2 no-op this fix was supposed to close; `row_count` returned 0 and `column_exists` returned False the same way. The probes now **retry once after the rollback**, so they answer about the object.
