The pinned regression test (`pythia/tests/test_calibration_safe_ddl.py::test_table_exists_recovers_after_failed_alter`) had been failing on DuckDB 1.5.x and no workflow ran it. **Rule: `TransactionContext Error: Current transaction is aborted` is a statement about the previous statement — never convert it into an answer about the object being probed.**
- **Every publish went red AFTER successfully uploading the release — inline DB inspect had no checkout** (fixed, 2026-07-28): `publish_latest_data.yml` never checks out the repo (every Python step is an inline heredoc with its own `pip install duckdb`), but the "Inspect published DB" step added in #803 runs `python -m scripts.ci.inspect_resolver_db`, which needs the repo on disk — `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'scripts'` after the `gh release upload` succeeded, so the release WAS updated while the job showed failure and the best-effort API sync poke never ran (the API still catches up via its 60s poll). Latent since 2026-07-16 (#803 landed one day after the previous publish); first observed on the 2026-07-28 smoke-run publish, the first run to reach the step. Fixed with `actions/checkout@v4` as the job's first step + a defensive duckdb install in the inspect step. **Rule: a red publish run does not mean the release wasn't updated — check the release asset timestamp before re-dispatching; and any workflow step that `python -m`-imports repo modules needs a checkout, unlike the inline-heredoc steps around it.**
- **Batch state was not pipeline-scoped — month 2 would replay month 1's LLM outputs verbatim** (fixed, 2026-07-28 audit, CRITICAL): custom ids (`hsrc-{iso3}-{hz}-p{n}`, `spd-{sha1(qid)}-{model}`) carried no pipeline scope while `llm_batch_requests.custom_id` is a global PK in a DB artifact that travels between runs — the second staged pipeline's `enqueue_request` found the first's `succeeded` rows, `submit_pending` submitted ZERO provider batches, and the collect stages served last month's RC/triage/SPD text into this month's forecasts (only symptom: ~$0 HS cost; same-epoch forecaster re-runs collided identically, and `submit_pending` swept stale foreign pending rows into new batches). Fixed with a `sha1(pipeline_id)[:8]` id segment + `pipeline_id` column + scoped queries + `expire_and_purge_stale` (see the Batch layer section). **Rule: any key used to replay stored LLM output across process boundaries must be scoped to the execution that created it.** Companion fixes in the same audit: poller stall cap (a failed stage was re-dispatched every 15 min forever), signature-gated + reordered stage uploads with red-on-regression, hs_batch full-lock DB access, delete-first `_write_hs_triage`, Brave-breaker state carried across stages, cancelled-batch salvage, and the weekly-ingest gate. Full detail: `docs/audit_2026-07.md` §2026-07-28. Tests: `TestPipelineScoping`/`TestSalvageAndCounters` in `pythia/tests/test_llm_batch.py`, `test_collect_under_different_pipeline_misses`, `tests/test_poll_llm_batches_guard.py`, `tests/test_check_pipeline_active.py`.
- **Prompt caching was enabled in production but wired to no forecaster call site** (fixed, 2026-07-28 audit, HIGH): `PYTHIA_PROMPT_CACHE_ENABLED=1` in all three workflows while `cache_segments` had exactly one caller (Sibyl) and `prompt_cache_key` none — `build_spd_prompt_v2(return_parts=True)` was referenced only by tests, so claude-opus-5 paid full input price on every SPD call and the failure was invisible (0 cached tokens reads as "cache not warm"; nothing consumed the telemetry). Now wired at the Track-1/Track-2/binary call sites (see the Prompt V3 section) with a zero-reads `::warning` in post_run_diagnostics. **Rule: a feature flag that changes REQUESTS must be verified by response telemetry (cache_read_input_tokens > 0), not by the flag being set.** Related cost-accounting fixes in the same audit: Gemini `thoughtsTokenCount` folded into billed output, errored-batch-item + retried-attempt usage costed, `combine_usage` cache/tier handling, gpt-4.1 cached rates, refusal/truncation excluded from the provider breaker, sibyl_step raised max_tokens.
- **Per-model `thinking`/`temperature` never reached the SPD provider call** (fixed, 2026-07-27 lineup swap): `_call_spd_model_for_spec` in `forecaster/cli.py` stamps `purpose="spd_v2"` on each ensemble member before calling the provider, and did so by rebuilding the `ModelSpec` field-by-field — omitting `temperature` and `thinking`. Config-derived specs always carry `purpose=None` (`_make_model_spec` never sets it), so the branch fired for **every** member and every per-model param in `config.yaml`'s ensemble block was discarded. Effects: the OpenAI members ran at the provider's default reasoning effort (config said `high`/`medium`); `gemini_flash` was accidentally correct because the `PYTHIA_GOOGLE_SPD_THINKING_LEVEL_FLASH` env fallback defaults to `low`; **`gemini_pro` ran with no `thinkingConfig` at all** because the workflow sets `PYTHIA_GOOGLE_SPD_THINKING_LEVEL_PRO=""` — directly contradicting the "config is authoritative … `ModelSpec.thinking` from config wins" claim in the model-registry section. Fixed with `dataclasses.replace(ms, purpose="spd_v2")`. **Rule: never rebuild a `ModelSpec` field-by-field — use `replace()`, or new params will be silently dropped at that seam.** Two sibling rebuilds still drop params by design and are documented as such: the `PYTHIA_SPD_GOOGLE_MODEL_ID` override in `providers.py` and `parse_ensemble_specs` (which is why `PYTHIA_SPD_ENSEMBLE_SPECS` runs need the `PYTHIA_GOOGLE_SPD_THINKING_LEVEL_*` env fallbacks). Tests: `forecaster/tests/test_spd_model_params.py`.
- **A model swap that misses the sampling-param guard 400s every call** (guarded, 2026-07-27): `_ANTHROPIC_NO_TEMPERATURE_PREFIXES` holds **literal id prefixes, not families** — `claude-opus-4-8` does not cover `claude-opus-5`, so swapping the `claude` alias without extending the tuple sends `temperature` to a model that rejects it (HTTP 400), taking out the SPD Claude member **and every Sibyl step** at once. `test_anthropic_temperature_guard_covers_every_reachable_anthropic_model` now derives the reachable set from the live config plus `sibyl/config.MODEL` and fails if any of them is unguarded. The OpenAI side is already prefix-based (`("gpt-5",)`) and covers `gpt-5.6-*`. Related: **`sibyl/config.py`'s `SIBYL_MODEL` default is independent of the `claude` registry alias** — swapping the ensemble member does not move Sibyl; edit both.
- **Opus 5 thinking shares the `max_tokens` budget** (handled, 2026-07-27): from Claude Opus 5 adaptive thinking is **on by default** (Opus 4.8 ran without it unless asked), and thinking tokens are charged against the same `max_tokens` ceiling as the answer — so a budget that comfortably held an SPD JSON on 4.8 can truncate mid-object, surfacing only as a parse failure. `_ANTHROPIC_SPD_MAX_OUTPUT` now defaults to 32,768 (still overridable by `PYTHIA_ANTHROPIC_SPD_MAX_TOKENS`), and `call_anthropic` surfaces `stop_reason` — `refusal` (safety classifiers, with the `stop_details.category`) and `max_tokens` (truncation) previously both returned HTTP 200 with empty text and **no error**, so the member just vanished from the ensemble. Usage is preserved on the error result: a truncated call still burned tokens and must still be costed. The response parser already filtered `type == "text"`, so thinking blocks never leaked into the answer.
- **Failed grounding logged as a model named "unknown"** (fixed, 2026-07-27 costs-page review): both grounding log sites (`horizon_scanner/regime_change_llm.py`, `horizon_scanner/triage.py`) defaulted `model_id` to the bare string `"unknown"` via `pack.get("debug", {}).get("model_id", "unknown")`. The RC/triage chains log only the FINAL pack after `_try_fallbacks`, and three pack shapes carry no backend model id: the synthetic `{"grounding_backend": "all_failed"}` pack (brave AND openai AND gemini all returned nothing), the Brave circuit-breaker pack (`brave_search.py` → `grounding_backend: "brave_circuit_breaker_tripped"`), and the missing-key pack (`debug: {"error": "missing_api_key"}`, which carries no `grounding_backend` at all). Those rows then surfaced on the Costs page's by-model table as a row literally named `unknown` with $0 cost and N countries — i.e. **N country-hazard pairs that got zero grounding evidence, disguised as a mystery model**. A shared helper `resolve_grounding_model_id(pack)` in `horizon_scanner/llm_logging.py` now returns the backend's real model id when one exists (including for a backend that ran and found 0 sources — that call still cost money) and otherwise a naming sentinel: `grounding-failed` / `grounding-breaker-tripped` / `grounding-unavailable`. The debug bundle's `rc_grounding_health` / `triage_grounding_health` gained `n_no_backend` + `no_backend_by_reason`, so a non-zero `brave_circuit_breaker_tripped` count (the Brave budget gate firing) is countable instead of being inferred from a costs page. **The sentinels are deliberately absent from `pythia/model_costs.json` — they are not models, and `resolve_price_per_1m` returning None ($0) is correct; do not add cost entries for them.** `resolver/query/costs.py`'s `fillna("unknown")` (both `model_id` and `model_name` NULL) is unchanged and remains the correct label for a genuinely model-less row — note those writers pass no `iso3`, so a real "unknown" row always shows Countries=0. Tests: `tests/test_horizon_scanner_llm_logging.py::TestResolveGroundingModelId`, `web/src/app/costs/__tests__/model_names.test.ts`. **2026-07-28 follow-up**: the third grounding log site (`pythia/web_research/web_research.py`) had the same defect — its no-backend outcomes now log `grounding-disabled` / `grounding-budget-exceeded` / `grounding-unavailable` sentinels (same not-a-model rule).
- **Test toggle OFF still showed test-run data on Countries/Forecasts pages; "Last updated" advanced on test publishes** (fixed, 2026-07-15 dashboard review): with only test-mode runs published (the pre-production window), the dashboard leaked them everywhere despite Test OFF. Same class as the #796 run-selection sweep, four more gaps: (1) `core._latest_questions_view` — the latest_only CTE behind `/v1/questions` (Forecasts page) and `/v1/forecasts/ensemble` — had **no is_test filter**; it now takes `include_test` and filters `COALESCE(q.is_test,FALSE)=FALSE` inside the CTE. (2) `resolver/query/countries_index.py` filtered is_test only in `_base_rows`; `_latest_hs_run_id`, `_hs_run_id_for_month`, `_add_hs_country_list` (this is what surfaced Iran/Somalia country rows from a test run), `_update_last_triaged`, `_update_forecasts`, and `_update_highest_rc` all queried hs_runs/hs_triage/questions/forecasts_ensemble unfiltered — all now take `include_test` and use the shared `_test_clause()` helper (column-existence-guarded; test-forecast exclusion sits in the LEFT JOIN condition so a question with only test forecasts still shows with n_forecasted=0). (3) `compute_questions_forecast_summary` had no test filter on forecasts_ensemble, so a production question (same-epoch reuse) could get its forecast_date/EIV from a test run's rows. (4) `/v1/version` had NO test filter by design (see the superseded note in the 2026-07-13 entry) — but that meant a test publish advanced the homepage "Last updated" while the Test-OFF dashboard showed nothing new (the 15-July symptom: "Last updated 14 July" was yesterday's TEST run, while the 15-July production run was still publishing). `/v1/version` now accepts `include_test` (homepage passes the toggle); the staleness probes are test-aware and additionally probe hs_runs so the manifest's unfiltered `latest_hs_run_id`/`latest_hs_created_at` are overridden with the latest PRODUCTION run when the filter is active (probe cache is keyed `(mtime, include_test)`; `_hs_probe_ok` distinguishes "no production runs" from "probe unavailable" so minimal DBs keep manifest values). Also: the `/v1/countries` exception-fallback SQL in app.py gained the same filters, and `publish_latest_data.yml` gained a best-effort post-upload **force_sync poke** (secrets `PYTHIA_API_BASE_URL` + `FRED_DEBUG_TOKEN`; skips silently when unset) so the API picks up a publish immediately instead of within its 60s poll — publish-to-dashboard lag was repeatedly misread as "dashboard broken". **Rule: any query that picks a "latest" run or joins a test-stampable table (questions, hs_runs, hs_triage, forecasts_ensemble, scores) for a default dashboard view must thread `include_test` and filter each table it touches.** Tests: `tests/test_api_test_toggle.py`.
- **fewsnet_countries.json / ipc_countries.json overwritten with only the current fetch window's countries** (fixed, 2026-07-15 run review): both connectors' `_write_country_list` opened the file in `"w"` mode with only the countries seen in the current fetch — a `fewsnet_months=3` dispatch wrote 27 countries over the curated 48-entry git file (runner-workspace only; nothing commits it), which corrupted `ipc_api`'s FEWS NET exclusion set *within the same run*. Both writers now **merge-union with the existing file (never shrink)**, and `summarize_all_phases` carries a shrink detector (on-disk vs git HEAD count). Related gap fixed at the consumer: `ipc_countries.json` never exists at question-generation time (fresh checkout), so `_is_ipc_country` was always False and IPC-only countries could never get DR PHASE3PLUS questions — the question generator now falls back to a DB query (distinct iso3 with `phase3plus_in_need` rows, last 36 months). Tests: `test_merges_with_existing_never_shrinks` (both connector test files), `test_food_security_db_fallback_covers_ipc_only_country`.
- **acled_political stored NOTHING for ~14 countries — ACLED name forms missing from the alias table** (fixed, 2026-07-15 run review): the server-side numeric `iso` filter worked, but client-side re-attribution failed because ACLED's country names don't match `countries.csv` labels ("United Kingdom" vs "Great Britain", "Guinea" vs "Guinea Conakry", "Republic of Congo" vs "Congo", "Bosnia and Herzegovina" vs "Bosnia-Herzegovina", "South Korea" vs "South Korea (Republic of)", "East Timor" vs "Timor-Leste", "North Macedonia" vs "Macedonia", …) and none were in `DEFAULT_ALIAS_SOURCE` — so `_filter_events_to_country` correctly discarded 100% and the run stored zero events for GIN/COG/BIH/KOR/TLS + others (6 of them in the 122-country target list). Fixed by adding the ACLED name forms to `DEFAULT_ALIAS_SOURCE` in `resolver/ingestion/utils/iso_normalize.py` (benefits every `resolve_iso3` consumer). Also: countries with no pycountry numeric now SKIP the fetch instead of pulling ~50 global events to discard (`_ISO_NUMERIC_OVERRIDES = {"RKS": "0"}` keeps Kosovo — a wrong override is harmless because the attribution guard drops non-matching events), and whole-country attribution drops are counted (`get_attribution_drop_stats`) and surfaced in the ingest diagnostics extras + the all-phases run summary. **Rule: when a "0/N events resolve" warning appears for a country, check the ACLED name form against countries.csv/aliases first.** Tests: `test_filter_resolves_acled_name_forms`, `resolver/tests/test_iso_normalize_aliases.py`.
- **VIEWS conflict forecasts 75 days stale with no warning** (instrumented, 2026-07-15): the 2026-07-15 run carried a 2026-05-01 VIEWS vintage (the API's latest served run) and only ACLED CAST had a staleness check. `views.py` and `conflictforecast.py` now warn (>45d, warn-only) like `acled_cast.py`; the all-phases summary renders a per-source vintage table and flags stale vintages in Problems & Warnings. If VIEWS staleness persists across cycles it is upstream — escalate to VIEWS, don't chase the connector. (ACLED CAST remains pinned to its 2025-12-01 vintage — confirmed upstream, escalate to ACLED.)
- **Same-epoch question reuse kept the test run's `is_test=TRUE`** (fixed, 2026-07-14 pre-production review): question_ids are epoch-keyed (`SOM_ACE_PA_2026-08`), so a production run in the same month as a test run ADOPTS the test run's question rows via `_upsert_question`'s UPDATE path — which refreshed `hs_run_id`/`track`/`pythia_metadata_json` but NOT `is_test`. Because `compute_resolutions` and `compute_scores` stamp their rows from `questions.is_test` and calibration/dashboard exclude test questions, the production forecasts for those questions would never display, resolve into calibration, or score as production data. Fixed: the UPDATE now sets `is_test = COALESCE(is_test, FALSE) AND ?` (current `is_test_mode()`) — a one-way TRUE→FALSE transition (production adoption clears the flag; a later test re-run can never taint a production question). Two more latest-run-selection is_test gaps fixed in the same sweep (same class as #796): `/v1/diagnostics/summary`'s hs_runs fallback and `core._run_filter_cte`'s `MAX(run_id)`-per-question CTE (an explicit `forecaster_run_id` param remains an operator override, not test-filtered). Tests: `tests/test_create_questions_from_triage.py::test_same_epoch_*`, `tests/test_api_is_test_run_selection.py`.
- **llm_calls double-write: every generation call logged twice; adversarial synthesis invisible** (fixed, 2026-07-14): `call_chat_ms` unconditionally wrote a generic row via `providers._log_llm_call` → `write_llm_call` (NO phase/iso3/run linkage/is_test/prompt) in ADDITION to the rich row from `log_hs_llm_call`/`log_forecaster_llm_call` — so phase-less cost aggregations (Costs page "other" bucket via `phase_group(None)`) double-counted generation spend, and the generic rows leaked test-run cost into non-test views. Meanwhile the adversarial-check synthesis call was logged ONLY generically, making it invisible in the debug bundle/health report/run cost (the July 2026 two-country bundle showed 69 calls with zero synthesis rows despite completed checks). Fixed: `call_chat_ms(log_call=False)` opt-out passed from every verified rich-logging call site (RC/triage pass loops incl. fallbacks, SPD member/retry/self-search, all scenario_writer calls, the ensemble low-level wrapper, adversarial synthesis); synthesis is now rich-logged as `hazard_code=ADVERSARIAL_SYNTH_{hz}` (the debug bundle reports these separately as "Adversarial Synthesis" — the `ADVERSARIAL_%` SEARCH stats exclude `ADVERSARIAL_SYNTH_%`, otherwise the 0-source generation rows read as failed grounding and produced a false WARN); `write_llm_call` stamps `is_test` (default `is_test_mode()`). Paths WITHOUT rich logging (HS JSON repair via `_utils.repair_json_response`, legacy ensembles, research, prediction markets) intentionally keep the generic row so their cost is still recorded — when adding a new rich-logging call site, pass `log_call=False`; when adding a bare `call_chat_ms` call, don't. Tests: `forecaster/tests/test_llm_call_logging.py`.
- **Costs page: Sibyl spend absorbed into "other", `sibyl_ms` always 0, and phase-less generic rows unattributable** (fixed, 2026-07-16 costs-page review): three attribution gaps in `resolver/query/costs.py`, all fixed query-side (recovering historical rows, no call-site changes). (1) `phase_group()` had no `sibyl` branch → `phase_group("sibyl")` fell to `"other"`, and because `_load_llm_calls` maps `phase` through `phase_group` **before** both cost and runtime aggregation, Sibyl's Opus + Brave dollars landed in the `other` by_phase bucket AND the `sibyl_ms` runtime column was always 0 (its pivot/frontend plumbing existed but the value was pre-collapsed to `other`; `sibyl_ms` was additionally dropped from the final `RUN_RUNTIME_COLUMNS` projection). (2) The live binary-forecast call sites write `phase='binary_v2'`, which matched none of `phase_group`'s branches → `other` (SPD used `spd_v2`→forecast, but binary was missed). (3) Generic `write_llm_call`/`_log_llm_call` rows carry a meaningful `component` (e.g. `HorizonScanner`, `prediction_markets`, `Researcher`) but the cost query read only `phase` — so those rows became un-attributable `other`; they also write run linkage to `forecaster_run_id`, but the query read only `run_id`/`hs_run_id`. (NB: the generic `component`/`forecaster_run_id` columns are absent from the current fresh-restart `llm_calls` schema — `write_llm_call` actually errors against it — so the `component` coalesce is a safe no-op there and a recovery path for legacy DBs that still carry the columns; the live wins on the current DB are the `sibyl` and `binary_v2` branches.) Fixes: added `sibyl`, `binary_v2`→forecast, `prediction_markets`, and `HorizonScanner`/`horizon`/`triage` recognition to `phase_group` with an explicit `CANONICAL_PHASES` list; `_load_llm_calls` now coalesces `phase = COALESCE(phase, component)` before mapping and adds `forecaster_run_id` as a third run-linkage fallback; `sibyl_ms`/`prediction_markets_ms` added to `RUN_RUNTIME_COLUMNS`; a diagnostic logs the top `component`s still in `other` so that bucket means "genuinely unmapped". Frontend (`web/src/app/costs/CostsClient.tsx`): friendly `formatModelName`/phase labels, a spend-by-phase bar and a monthly stacked spend-over-time SVG chart (validated dataviz palette, light-only to match the app), a phase legend. **Rule: never map `sibyl` to `other`, and any new phase must be added to both `phase_group`/`CANONICAL_PHASES` and the frontend `PHASE_ORDER`/`PHASE_LABELS`/`PHASE_COLORS` (guarded by `web/src/app/costs/__tests__/phases.test.ts`).** Tests: `resolver/tests/test_costs_aggregations.py` (`test_sibyl_and_component_attribution`, `test_run_runtimes_itemizes_sibyl_and_pm`, `test_phase_group_canonical_mapping`).
- **Binary EVENT_OCCURRENCE prompt: phantom "?" event names, Green rows as "Active alerts", hardcoded "2015–present", headerless ReliefWeb** (fixed, 2026-07-14): (a) the recent-GDACS-alerts loader (`forecaster/cli.py` binary path) selected only `ym,value,alertlevel` while the renderer read `event_name`/`event_id` → every alert rendered as `[Green] ? (ym)`; facts_resolved has no event name for these monthly rows, so the section now renders `[level] ym` only. (b) The query had no level/value filter, so Green and value=0 months were presented as "Active GDACS alerts" while the prompt says Green does NOT count — now filtered to `value>=1 AND alertlevel IN (ORANGE,RED)` and relabeled "Recent significant GDACS event months". (c) `_section_base_rate`'s "(GDACS, 2015–present)" label was hardcoded; `_query_base_rate` now returns `coverage_start/coverage_end` and the header renders the real window. (d) ReliefWeb list-form titles rendered with no section header, appearing visually nested under "ENSO STATE:" — header added. Also: `ENSOForecast.to_prompt_context` rendered "published )" when the scraped publication date was empty (now conditional). **Rule: never hardcode a coverage-era label in a prompt — derive it from the rows.** Tests: `forecaster/tests/test_binary_prompts.py`, `horizon_scanner/tests/test_enso_prompt_header.py`.
- **NOAA CPC scraper had the same tercile None-crash fixed for TSR in #794** (fixed, 2026-07-14): `noaa_cpc_scraper.to_prompt_context`/`_log_forecast` guarded only `prob_above_normal` before formatting all three terciles with `:.0%`; the three fields come from independent regexes, so a partial parse raised TypeError inside `noaa_process` and `collect_noaa` silently dropped the whole NOAA batch. Latent until NOAA's late-May/early-Aug publications. Both sites now use the `if None not in (above, near, below)` guard. **Rule: when copying a fix for a parse-then-format crash, sweep ALL sibling scrapers for the same pattern.** Tests: `horizon_scanner/seasonal_tc/tests/test_noaa_tercile_safety.py`.
- **ACLED CAST year filter could hide the freshest issue at the year boundary** (fixed, 2026-07-14): the connector preferred `year=current_year` (a TARGET-year filter) and fell back to unfiltered only when the response was EMPTY. CAST issues 6-month-ahead forecasts, so in Nov/Dec the fresh issue's targets are mostly next-year: the current-year pull returned a non-empty stale subset, the fallback never ran, and `_transform`'s lead 1..6 filter discarded the rest. Now both target years (current, current+1) are fetched and merged, each pull verified by the records' OWN `year` field (per the "never trust an ACLED request-side filter" rule) so an ignored filter can't double-count. Tests: `TestYearFilterAndStaleness` in `resolver/tests/test_acled_cast_connector.py`.
- **ACLED political events globally cross-contaminated** (fixed, July 2026 run review): `pythia/acled_political.py` passed `iso3` as an ACLED API filter param — but `iso3` is a RESPONSE field, not a filter, and the ACLED API **silently ignores unknown filter params**. Every per-country fetch therefore returned the same ~50 GLOBAL events, and `store_acled_political_events` stamped them with the requested iso3 — Somalia's ACE SPD prompt contained Iranian/Ecuadorian/Mozambican "KEY POLITICAL EVENTS" (11,592 rows = the identical 46 events under 252 countries). Fixed by: requesting `iso3|country` in `fields`, filtering server-side with the documented numeric `iso` param (via pycountry), **attributing client-side by each event's own returned iso3** (`_filter_events_to_country`), a store-side misattribution guard, and a contamination self-heal (`purge_contaminated_events`, run at the start of `_bulk_fetch_acled_political`; manual: `python -m scripts.purge_acled_political_events`). **Rule: never trust an ACLED request-side filter — verify by the returned rows' own country fields.** Tests: `pythia/tests/test_acled_political_attribution.py`.
- **ACLED political events stored NOTHING (contamination guard over-rejected)** (fixed, 2026-07-12): the contamination fix above attributed each event by a single hardcoded `ev.get("iso3")` key, but the live ACLED API does NOT reliably return the code under the literal key `iso3` — it uses the HXL tag `#country+code`, the underscore variant `country_iso3`, or only the `country` name. So `_event_iso3` resolved to "" for every event and `_filter_events_to_country` discarded 100% (a July run showed 5,257 events across 182 countries → 0 stored, `acled_political_events` table empty). The unit tests passed anyway because their fixtures mock a literal `iso3` key — a mock-shape mismatch. The `iso` numeric **server filter is working** (per-country event counts vary 1→50, not a constant global page), so the data was correct; only the client-side key read was wrong. Fixed by adding `_event_iso3(ev)`: try `["iso3","country_iso3","#country+code"]`, then fall back to `resolve_iso3(ev, name_keys=("country",))` — mirroring the fatalities connector's robust extraction (`resolver/ingestion/acled_client.py`). **Rule: attribute ACLED events with the multi-key + name-fallback resolver, never a single `.get("iso3")`; and test connectors against the REAL response shape (`#country+code`/name-only), not a mocked `iso3` key.** Tests: the two `test_filter_resolves_iso3_from_*` cases in `test_acled_political_attribution.py`.
- **ACLED CAST conflict forecasts stuck at a Dec-2025 vintage** (mitigated + instrumented, 2026-07-12): `conflict_forecasts` showed `ACLED_CAST` last `forecast_issue_date` = 2025-12-01 (>200 days old) even though the connector reported success. **This is NOT an auth/fetch/pagination failure** — the log confirms auth OK and "fetched 76,935 total records, wrote 1752 rows" (a complete, naturally-terminated paginated pull, well under the 20-page cap). `_derive_issue_date` takes `max(timestamp)` across all records, and the newest record `/api/cast/read` served (called with no `year`/`month` filter) is December 2025. Most likely **upstream staleness** (ACLED not serving 2026 CAST via the API). Changes: `_fetch_all_records(year=...)` now prefers the current calendar year with a fallback to an unfiltered pull (can't regress to zero data), and `fetch_forecasts` logs a loud `[acled_cast] latest forecast issue date … is N days old` **staleness warning** past `_STALENESS_WARN_DAYS=45`. **Caveat: ACLED's `year` filters the forecast TARGET year, not the issue year — so if CAST stays pinned to Dec-2025 after this, it is upstream and should be escalated to ACLED, not chased in the connector.** Tests: `TestYearFilterAndStaleness` in `resolver/tests/test_acled_cast_connector.py`. **Confirmed upstream on the 2026-07-12 re-run**: with the year filter active it fetched 9,879 records (vs 76,935 unfiltered) but the newest issue is still 2025-12-01, and the staleness warning fired — so ACLED genuinely isn't serving 2026 CAST. Escalate, don't chase.
- **Run Results dashboard showed "0 forecasts but $X cost" with Test OFF, and leaked test costs into production cost views** (fixed, 2026-07-13 dashboard review): two compounding bugs surfaced when the only published runs were test-mode. (1) **Run selection ignored the test filter** — `diagnostics_run_summary` (`pythia/api/routes/diagnostics.py`) picked the latest `run_id` from `forecasts_ensemble`/`hs_triage` with **no `is_test` filter**, so with `include_test=false` it selected a test run, zeroed its coverage (sub-queries ARE test-filtered) but still surfaced its cost/ensemble/llm_health. Fixed: both run-selection queries now append `COALESCE(is_test,FALSE)=FALSE` when `include_test` is false; when nothing matches, `run_id`/`hs_run_id` stay None and every block returns its zero default. The response gained a **`has_run`** boolean; the dashboard (`web/src/components/RunSummaryView.tsx`) renders an explicit "No production runs to show — toggle Test ON" empty state when `has_run === false` instead of a misleading all-zero KPI row. (2) **Forecaster llm_calls weren't stamped `is_test`** — `forecaster/llm_logging.py::log_forecaster_llm_call` defaulted `is_test=False`, so `spd_v2`/`binary_v2`/`scenario_v2` calls in a test run were `is_test=False` even though the run's forecast outputs and its `hs_triage`/`sibyl` calls were `is_test=True`. That leaked test SPD costs into non-test cost views (Run Results + the Costs page). Fixed: `is_test` now defaults to `None` and inherits `is_test_mode()` when the caller doesn't set it. **Backfill**: `scripts/reconcile_llm_calls_is_test.py` flips `llm_calls.is_test` FALSE→TRUE for any call whose `run_id`/`hs_run_id` belongs to a test run (idempotent, FALSE→TRUE only), wired into `compute_calibration_pythia.yml` (self-heals every cycle, like `retire_blocked_hazard_questions`). **Note on "Last updated"** (superseded 2026-07-15): the homepage's "Last updated"/"Latest forecast scan" come from `/v1/version`, which was originally a global staleness probe with NO test filter — that turned out to be its own confusion source (a test-mode publish advanced "Last updated" while the Test-OFF dashboard showed nothing new), so `/v1/version` now accepts `include_test` and the probes are test-aware by default (see the 2026-07-15 test-toggle entry below). The 09-July-vs-12-July symptom in the original report was API sync lag (the 12 July release published at 13:45 and the API synced it hours later), not a code bug; `/v1/version` was current once synced. Tests: `tests/test_api_run_summary.py::test_run_summary_test_{off,on}_*`, `scripts/tests/test_reconcile_llm_calls_is_test.py`.
- **LLM prompt artifact never captured any SPD or Scenario prompt** (fixed, 2026-07-13 run review): `scripts/ci/snapshot_prompt_artifact.py::_load_question_for_hazard` ran `… FROM questions … ORDER BY created_at DESC`, but the `questions` table has **no `created_at` column**. The resulting DuckDB `BinderException` was swallowed by a bare `except Exception: return None`, so the loader returned None for every hazard and the `pythia-llm-prompts` artifact showed "_No active question found … SPD prompt not rendered_" for all hazards — hiding the single most important prompt (the SPD forecast) from prompt review. Isolated to this diagnostic script (the pipeline never reads `questions.created_at`), so forecasts were unaffected. Fixed by ordering on `window_start_date DESC, question_id` (existing columns; epoch recency + deterministic tiebreak) and logging the swallowed exception. Regression test: `scripts/ci/tests/test_snapshot_prompt_artifact.py` (run by `horizon-scanner-ci.yml`). **Follow-up (fixed, 2026-07-13): the SPD render now wires in the full `_load_structured_data` injects** (conflict forecasts, adversarial checks, HS grounding evidence, GDACS, CrisisWatch, food security) so the artifact shows exactly what the LLM sees. This was previously blocked by a **shared-connection footgun**: `_load_structured_data`'s sub-loaders (`pythia/ipc_phases.py`, `pythia/acaps.py`) call `resolver.db.duckdb_io.get_db(path)` — a cache HIT returns the artifact's OWN shared connection — and then `close_db(con)`, which closes AND evicts that shared handle, so the artifact's `con` died mid-loop (dropping every hazard after the first to "No HS triage data found"). Fixed in the artifact by reopening `con = _connect(db_url)` immediately after each `_load_structured_data_for_artifact()` call (`get_db` returns a fresh connection once the cached one is closed/evicted, guarded by its `_closed`/healthcheck logic). The load MUST run after all con-based reads for the hazard (sample/features/evidence/question/calibration) and before the scenario block / next iteration. **Rule: any code that holds a `duckdb_io.get_db()` connection across a call into `_load_structured_data` (or the ipc_phases/acaps loaders) must reopen it afterwards — those loaders `close_db()` the shared cached handle.** Test: `test_connect_reopens_after_shared_connection_closed`.
- **Seasonal TC (Tropical Cyclone) forecasts wrote 0 rows — TSR None-tercile crash + BoM 403** (fixed, 2026-07-12): a Resolver Update Phase 4 wrote 0 `seasonal_tc_outlooks`. Three separate scraper problems in `horizon_scanner/seasonal_tc/`, all surfacing as `collect_all` → 0 → `fetch_and_store_seasonal_tc` returns False: (1) **TSR crash** — `tsr_seasonal_extractor.extract_forecast` (and `SeasonalForecast.to_prompt_context`) formatted `tercile_near`/`tercile_below` with `:.0%` but guarded only on `tercile_above`; a 2026 PDF reported a partial tercile set (above present, near/below None) → `TypeError: unsupported format string passed to NoneType.__format__`. Worse, `discover_and_extract` caught only `requests.RequestException`, so that one PDF's error propagated and discarded ALL ~6 successfully-parsed TSR forecasts. (2) **BoM 403** — `bom_scraper`'s `PythiaBot/1.0` User-Agent is blocked by bom.gov.au (403); a browser UA returns 200. (3) **NOAA CPC 0** — seasonal (its Atlantic outlook publishes late-May/early-Aug; mid-July there's no new page), and TSR already covers the Atlantic, so not a bug. Fixes: guard all three terciles before `:.0%` formatting (both sites), broaden the `discover_and_extract` per-URL `except` to skip a bad PDF instead of aborting the batch, and give BoM a desktop-browser UA. Verified live: TSR 6 forecasts (N. Atlantic + NW Pacific incl. July updates) + BoM 2 (AUS + S. Pacific) = 8 stored, 63/82 countries cached, returns True. Tests: `horizon_scanner/seasonal_tc/tests/test_tsr_tercile_safety.py` (network-free tercile None-safety), now run by `horizon-scanner-ci.yml`. Minor pre-existing BoM quirk left as-is: its season parser can emit `season=1980-81` (the Australian outlook is issued yearly in October, so mid-year data is the prior Oct outlook).
- **Seasonal TC basin coverage: SWI + NIO sourced July 2026; ENP/CP remain sourceless** (was: only 4 of 8 mapped basins had a source): `mfr_swio_scraper.py` scrapes the annual Météo-France La Réunion / RSMC seasonal outlook for **SWI** (French HTML article on meteofrance.re; slug drifts year-over-year → candidate-slug list + listing-page discovery + meteofrance.yt mirror; browser UA required — the site 403s generic fetchers like BoM does; issued Oct/Nov for Nov–Apr, so mid-year the prior edition is served and labeled). **NIO** gets a climatology-first context block from `imd_nio_scraper.py` (IMD/RSMC New Delhi publishes NO seasonal TC count outlook — verified; the block always renders with zero network, optionally enriched by RSMC New Delhi's weekly Extended Range Outlook for Cyclogenesis PDF when <21 days old, plus the DB ENSO state; `build_nio_context` never returns empty; since the run-2 follow-up, `get_seasonal_tc_context_for_country` also serves this climatology block in-process as a FINAL fallback for NIO countries when both the DB cache and the JSON file are empty — NIO countries never lose TC context to ingest timing). IRN was added to the NIO country list (Makran coast). Remaining gaps: **ENP(7 countries)/CP(1)** have no source (NOAA CPC covers them in its May/Aug press releases when parsed). Live-source reachability from GitHub runners should be spot-checked on the first CI run — the July 2026 dev sandbox could not reach either host (egress-proxy 403), and a total SWI fetch failure degrades to no-SWI-context rather than a crash.
- **FEWS NET Phase 3+ history always rendered "0% coverage" in production** (fixed, July 2026 run review): `_load_fewsnet_phase3_history` opened a raw `duckdb.connect(path, read_only=True)`. `pythia.db.schema.connect` deliberately forces read-write pooled connections to avoid DuckDB's same-process "different configuration" error — so the raw read-only connect raised exactly that error whenever the pipeline held a pooled handle (i.e. always), was swallowed by `except Exception`, and every DR/PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED SPD prompt claimed "Phase 3+ population reported in 0 of the last 36 months" for countries with years of data. Fixed by using `schema.connect(read_only=True)` and rendering loader errors explicitly ("History unavailable this run (data loader error)") instead of a false 0%-coverage history. **Rule: inside the pipeline, never open a raw read-only duckdb.connect on the Pythia DB path — use `pythia.db.schema.connect` (or `duckdb_io.get_db`).** Test seam changed: `forecaster/tests/test_fewsnet_history.py` now patches `forecaster.cli.connect`.
- **CrisisWatch loaded but never rendered in SPD prompts** (fixed, July 2026 run review): `_load_structured_data` put `format_crisiswatch_for_prompt(iso3)` into `sd["crisiswatch"]` for ACE, but `forecaster/prompts.py` had no render block for that key — no ACE SPD prompt ever contained CrisisWatch data. A render block now appends it to structured_sections. Same class of bug hit the grounding packs: the loader returns `report_markdown` but the prompt read `_hg.get("markdown")`, so "HS GROUNDING EVIDENCE" never rendered either (now reads `report_markdown` with `markdown` fallback). **Rule: when adding an sd[...] inject, grep prompts.py for a consumer — loading without rendering fails silently.** Tests: `forecaster/tests/test_spd_prompt_structured_sections.py`.
- **Conflict base rate treated the current partial month as complete** (fixed, July 2026 run review): `_build_conflict_base_rate` took the 6 most recent `acled_monthly_fatalities` months with no cutoff, so a run on July 9 presented 8 days of July as "Last month (2026-07): 36" and computed a spurious "de-escalating (-52.2%)" trend that biased the ACE prior anchor down. Both the ACLED and IDMC queries now exclude months >= the current calendar month.
- **Prompt offered a NEED_WEB_EVIDENCE escape the executor refused** (fixed, July 2026 run review): the prompt's WEB SEARCH/escape text was gated on `PYTHIA_SPD_WEB_SEARCH_ENABLED` alone, while execution (`forecaster/self_search.py:self_search_enabled`) requires THREE vars (`PYTHIA_FORECASTER_SELF_SEARCH`, `PYTHIA_SPD_WEB_SEARCH_ENABLED`, `PYTHIA_MODEL_SELF_SEARCH_ENABLED`) all "1". The HS workflow set only the SPD var on the forecaster step, so models were invited to request evidence, then dropped with error `self_search_disabled` (gemini-3.5-flash lost SOM_ACE_PA this way). Prompt text is now gated on the SAME `self_search_enabled()` check; when disabled the prompt explicitly forbids the escape; and `_call_spd_model` retries once with a no-evidence instruction if a model emits it anyway. The step-level `PYTHIA_SPD_WEB_SEARCH_ENABLED/GOOGLE` "1" overrides were removed from `run_horizon_scanner.yml` — to enable self-search, set all three vars.
- **Sibyl found 0 eligible questions on test-mode HS runs** (fixed, July 2026 run review): a test-mode HS run stamps every question `is_test=TRUE`, but Sibyl chains off HS via `workflow_run` (which cannot carry the upstream `test_mode` input) and never set `PYTHIA_TEST_MODE`, so `select_top_questions`' env-based test filter excluded ALL questions and the gate skipped the run. There is NO volatility threshold in the gate — it is purely `eligible count >= 1`. Fixed by deriving test mode from the DB: `sibyl/run.py` reads `hs_runs.is_test` for the resolved run and sets `PYTHIA_TEST_MODE=1` (so cost/SPD writers stamp outputs `is_test`), and `select_top_questions` includes a test run's questions via `hs_run_is_test()`. The gate step also logs an `eligibility_breakdown` JSON (per-pair counts, test status) so 0-eligible outcomes are self-explanatory. Tests: `tests/test_sibyl_select_questions.py`.
- **`inspect_resolver_duckdb.yml` crashed on a fresh/reset DB (`NoneType.__format__`)** (fixed, July 2026): after a full-reset Resolver Update backfill (which `ensure_schema()`s a fresh DB with populated `facts_resolved` but empty post-pipeline/forecast tables), the Inspect Resolver DuckDB workflow's embedded Python diagnostic died with `TypeError: unsupported format string passed to NoneType.__format__`. The `question_run_metrics` section runs an aggregate with **no `GROUP BY`**, so on an empty table it returns one all-NULL row `(0, None, None, None)`; the guard `if rows and rows[0]:` passes (truthy tuple) and `f"{avg_wall:.0f}"` then formats `None` → crash (the step runs under `bash -eo pipefail`, so it fails the job). Fixed by adding a None-safe `fmt_float(n, nd)` helper next to `fmt_num` and routing every numeric-format-spec interpolation of a DB-derived value through it (the `question_run_metrics` avg-wall/total-cost/avg-cost lines plus the lower-risk `bucket_centroids` centroid and two `llm_calls` cost/latency rows), so empty aggregates render `—` instead of crashing and a NULL in a non-empty result is also handled. **When adding a `f"{x:...}"`/`.format()` numeric spec on a query result in this diagnostic, wrap the value in `fmt_num` (ints) or `fmt_float` (floats) — an ungrouped aggregate always yields a row even when the table is empty.**
- **`publish_latest_data.yml` regression guard hard-fails on a fresh reset DB — by design** (not a bug; message clarified July 2026): after a reset backfill the release workflow correctly refuses to publish, because the fresh DB has 0 rows in the five post-pipeline tables (`resolutions`/`scores`/`bucket_centroids`/`calibration_weights`/`calibration_advice`) vs. the current release's data, and the freshness override can't rescue it (it keys on `max(hs_runs.generated_at)`/`max(forecasts_ensemble.created_at)`, both `None` in a reset DB → `if new_ts is None: continue` skips the override). A manual dispatch with an explicit `run_id` hard-fails (red); an auto-trigger or blank-`run_id` dispatch soft-skips (green). **The correct reset recovery is to run the full pipeline (Horizon Scanner Triage → forecaster → resolutions → scores → calibration), which republishes the DB at the end so the guard passes on its own — do NOT `force_publish=true` a bare reset DB, which rolls the dashboard back to a forecast-less state.** The guard's hard-fail message was reworded to name this fresh-reset case and steer to the pipeline path (no behavioral change).
- **DuckDB per-path instance caching defeated every API connection reopen** (fixed, July 2026 follow-up audit): DuckDB caches database instances per path within a process — opening a "new" connection while the old one is still open returns a handle to the SAME cached instance, which still reads the pre-`os.replace()` inode. Both reopen sites (`core._maybe_refresh_db` and `/v1/admin/force_sync`) used open-new-then-close-old, so the "reopened" connection never actually saw a swapped DB file (verified on DuckDB 1.5.4) — the likely residual root cause of the recurring dashboard-frozen-on-stale-DB incidents above. Fixed with `core._swap_read_connection()`: close FIRST, then open (the `_execute` retry path already did this correctly). **Any future connection-reopen code must close before reopening the same path.** On reopen failure the connection is left None and recreated by the next `_ensure_read_connection()`.
- **`/v1/version` staleness probes and download-export cache pinned pre-refresh data** (fixed, July 2026 follow-up audit): both caches were keyed on the raw on-disk file mtime while computing through the throttled (possibly not-yet-reopened) connection — the first request after a publish cached pre-swap data under the NEW DB version until the next publish. Both are now keyed on `core._READ_CON_MTIME` (the mtime the read connection was opened with); the export-cache filename tag also moved from `int(mtime)` to full precision (two swaps within the same second used to collide). **Cache keys for DB-derived data must reflect what the serving connection actually sees, not what is on disk.**
- **JSON-repair fallback dead — gpt-5.4 sent `temperature`** (fixed, July 2026 follow-up audit): `_OPENAI_NO_CUSTOM_TEMPERATURE` listed only the mini ids, so any gpt-5.4 call without `reasoning_effort` (the `hs_fallback` repair spec has no thinking value) sent `temperature` → HTTP 400 → all triage/RC JSON repair failed. Now prefix-based: `_OPENAI_NO_TEMPERATURE_PREFIXES = ("gpt-5",)` in `forecaster/providers.py` (mirrors `_ANTHROPIC_NO_TEMPERATURE_PREFIXES`). When adding an OpenAI reasoning family, extend the prefix tuple.
- **Brave breaker gate bypassed for priority hazards** (fixed, July 2026 follow-up audit): `create_questions_from_triage.py` recomputed `need = need_full_spd OR tier=='priority'`, so a priority-tier hazard whose `need_full_spd` the HS circuit-breaker gate had cleared (budget exhausted, zero grounding evidence) still got questions and a full SPD ensemble. The stored `need_full_spd` column is now the single source of truth — do not re-derive forecastability from `tier` downstream of `_write_hs_triage`.
- **Manual dispatch of Compute Resolutions / Compute SPD Scores always failed** (fixed, July 2026 follow-up audit): both hardcoded `RUN_ID=${{ github.event.workflow_run.id }}` (empty on `workflow_dispatch`) with no discovery fallback; compute_resolutions also lacked the job-level success guard so it ran on FAILED Resolver Update completions. Fixed by the `download-canonical-db` composite action (see Canonical DB artifact discovery) + the `if:` guard.
- **API OOM-killed on the 2GB Render instance** (fixed, July 2026): the FastAPI service randomly exceeded memory and crashed. Four compounding causes, all fixed in the `fix/api-memory-oom` PR: (1) **Download endpoints materialized full pandas DataFrames** — every `/v1/downloads/*` endpoint built its export via `build_*_export().fetchdf()` and then `_stream_csv` held the ENTIRE DataFrame (plus the heavy semaphore) for the whole client download; the XLSX endpoint additionally held DF + openpyxl workbook + BytesIO simultaneously, and `PYTHIA_MAX_CONCURRENT_HEAVY=2` allowed two such spikes at once. Now all downloads go through `core._serve_export()`: build under the semaphore, spill to a file under `PYTHIA_EXPORT_TMP_DIR` (default system tmp — **never `/data`**, which must keep ≥2× DB size free for sync staging), free the DF, release the semaphore at build end, serve via `FileResponse`. Files are cached keyed by `(slug, params-hash, int(DB mtime))` — invalidation is automatic because `download_db_atomic`'s `os.replace` advances the mtime (same signal `_maybe_refresh_db` trusts); stale entries are swept per request with a 60s grace. CSV bytes unchanged; the semaphore no longer covers client download time, so `PYTHIA_MAX_CONCURRENT_HEAVY=1` is now safe and recommended on Render. (2) **The API imported the entire pipeline at startup** — `app.py` imported `enqueue_run` from `pythia.pipeline.run`, whose module-scope imports pull the full horizon_scanner + calibration tree (~100–300MB RSS) into the web process; `/v1/run` could even execute the whole HS+forecaster pipeline in-process (guaranteed OOM). The import is now deferred into the handler and the endpoint is gated by `PYTHIA_ALLOW_INPROCESS_RUN` (default off → HTTP 503; guarded by `pythia/tests/test_api_lazy_pipeline_import.py`). (3) **Uncapped introspection connection** — `db_sync._refresh_latest_runs()` opened a second DuckDB connection with no `memory_limit`/`threads` pragmas (DuckDB default ≈ 80% of system RAM) right after each ~1GB DB download; now capped with the same env-driven limits (read directly from env — importing `pythia.api.core` there would be circular). `_update_ui_run` in `pythia/pipeline/run.py` got the same caps. (4) **`/v1/version` ran 6 full-column MAX scans per request** — and the dashboard homepage is force-dynamic, calling it on every page view; the staleness probes are now cached per DB mtime (`_staleness_probes()` in app.py; all-None results are not cached so a transient connection failure can't stick). Also: `/v1/forecasts/ensemble` and `/v1/forecasts/history` (unused by the dashboard) gained a default 200k-row cap with `?limit=` up to 500k and an additive `truncated: true` flag. **Render settings that pair with these fixes**: `PYTHIA_MAX_CONCURRENT_HEAVY=1`, `MALLOC_ARENA_MAX=2`, single-worker start command (`uvicorn pythia.api.app:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port $PORT --workers 1` — multiple workers multiply every per-process cost), `PYTHIA_ALLOW_INPROCESS_RUN` unset. Verify with `GET /v1/diagnostics/memory` (rss_mb + duckdb_memory()) before/after deploys.
- **SQL injection via `iso3`/`forecaster_run_id` API query params** (fixed, July 2026 audit): the resolver accordion endpoints (`/v1/resolver/source_inventory`, `/v1/resolver/source_data`) interpolated the user-supplied `iso3` param directly into SQL (`f"iso3 = '{iso3.upper()}'"`), and `_run_filter_cte` did the same with `forecaster_run_id` (used by 8+ endpoints). Fixed with `_validate_iso3_param()` (`^[A-Za-z]{3}$`, HTTP 400 otherwise) + bound `?` parameters, and a strict pattern check on `forecaster_run_id` at the CTE chokepoint. **Any new endpoint param that reaches SQL must be validated or parameterized** — tests in `pythia/tests/test_api_input_validation.py`.
- **Precedence policy silently non-functional in the pipeline path** (fixed, July 2026 audit): the production `precedence_config.yml` never actually drove `resolve_facts_frame` (the `run_pipeline` path). (1) The tier-0 entry `ifrc_montandon` didn't match the publisher string the IFRC connector writes (`IFRC`), so IFRC fell to the default (worst/unlisted) tier; (2) the engine reads config keys `tiebreak`/`defaults`/`metrics`, none of which existed in the YAML, so within-tier winners were picked alphabetically by `(source, run_id)` — no recency preference, and a NULL-value row could beat a non-null one; (3) the YAML's `metric_preference`/`source_precedence`/`conflict_rule` keys are read only by the legacy CLI path. Fixed by adding `ifrc` to tier 0 plus `tiebreak`/`defaults` blocks to the YAML, and defaulting the engine tiebreak to `[as_of_desc, value_nonnull]` when the key is missing. Tests exercise the real production config (`resolver/tests/test_precedence_pipeline_config.py`). Facts written before this fix may have wrong winners; the next full pipeline run rewrites them. **The legacy CLI path was subsequently removed in the S4 consolidation — `resolve_facts_frame` is now the single engine and `tiers`/`tiebreak`/`defaults`/`metrics` the only config keys.**
- **FATALITIES zero-defaults for countries outside ACLED's universe** (fixed, July 2026 audit): the month-coverage gate was global, so a country ACLED has never reported still zero-defaulted to FATALITIES=0 for globally-covered months. `_countries_with_source_data()` now also requires the country to appear at least once in the metric's source tables. EVENT_OCCURRENCE is deliberately exempt (GDACS is satellite-global; no rows genuinely means no qualifying events) — do not extend the universe gate to it.
- **Binary BayesMC aggregation always crashed with >1 model** (fixed, July 2026 S4): the Track-1 binary BayesMC block in `_run_binary_forecast_for_question` constructed `MemberOutput(..., raw="")` but the dataclass field is `raw_text` — so whenever more than one model returned a valid binary forecast, the block raised TypeError, the outer handler swallowed it, and NO `ensemble_bayesmc_v2` binary rows were ever written (plus a stray "binary: exception during forecast" no-forecast record after the successful mean write). Found by the S4 test-gap suite (`forecaster/tests/test_aggregation_gaps.py`), which now pins both aggregation writes. Note: binary questions are still SCORED under the mean ensemble only (see the scoring notes above) — but bayesmc rows now exist in `forecasts_ensemble` as intended.
- **Binary forecasts could be written with partial month coverage** (fixed, July 2026 audit): `parse_binary_response` passed through responses missing expected months, and the aggregation wrote whatever months survived — leaving silent month gaps in `forecasts_ensemble`. The parser now rejects incomplete responses at model level, and the binary writer path has an `insufficient_month_coverage` gate mirroring the SPD convention (never write a partial window).
- **SPD forecasts anchored +5 months late (runs 2026-03-21 → 2026-07-01)** (fixed): the v2 SPD prompt's six required calendar keys (`_forecast_month_keys_from_question` in `forecaster/prompts.py`) started at the question's `target_month` and counted forward. That was correct under the pre-March convention where `target_month` was the *opening* window month, but the question-provenance fix redefined `target_month` as the **6th (last)** window month — so every SPD run from 2026-03-21 onward asked models to forecast months `[target..target+5]`, i.e. the six months *after* the question window, while `compute_resolutions` scored horizons `[window_start..target]`. Verified from stored `forecasts_raw.spd_json` month keys (e.g. `SOM_DR_PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED_2026-04`: wording says Apr–Sep 2026, keys were Sep 2026–Feb 2027). Binary EVENT_OCCURRENCE questions were unaffected (`binary_prompts.py` derives months from `window_start_date`). Fixed by anchoring everything to the first window month: `_forecast_month_keys_from_question` prefers `window_start_date` (falls back to `target_month − 5`); a new `_anchor_month_for_question()` helper in `cli.py` computes the anchor, and the internal SPD chain (`_call_spd_model*`, `_call_spd_members_v2*`, `_call_spd_bayesmc_v2`, `_build_bayesmc_spd_obj`, `_expected_months`) now takes `anchor_month` (renamed from `target_month` to prevent recurrence). The BayesMC month-coverage check doubles as a regression guard: misanchored calendar keys now fail with `insufficient_month_coverage` instead of being silently accepted. **Forecasts and scores produced by runs in that window are misaligned and were archived; do not re-anchor any writer/prompt to `target_month`.**
- **Binary probability floor lowered 0.01 → 0.001**: `parse_binary_response` clamped model probabilities to [0.01, 0.99], putting a hard floor under the Brier score of well-calibrated rare-event forecasts (monthly base rates are often <1%). Clamp is now [0.001, 0.999], the binary prompt text tells models the new bounds, and clamping logs at debug level.
- **RC degradation without data sources**: RC assessments degrade severely when both `PYTHIA_HS_RESEARCH_WEB_SEARCH_ENABLED=0` and structured data connectors (ACLED, facts_deltas) are unavailable simultaneously. All country-hazard pairs may return RC Level 0 (baseline), even for active crisis countries. Always ensure at least one of web search or structured data is available for RC grounding.
- **Prediction market retriever**: Disabled in production via `PYTHIA_PREDICTION_MARKETS_ENABLED=0` in `run_horizon_scanner.yml` and `pythia_pipeline_stage.yml`. **The code default is ON** (`pythia/config.yaml`'s `prediction_markets.enabled: true` is the fallback when the env var is unset), so a new workflow or a local run picks it up unless it pins the var. Metaculus returns 403, Polymarket returns HTTP 422, and each call times out at 30s — adding ~6 minutes of wasted wall time per run. Do not re-enable until upstream APIs are fixed.
- **ACLED OAuth password grant missing `scope=authenticated` → HTTP 415** (fixed, 2026-07-15): the scheduled Resolver Update failed in Phase 1 when both the refresh grant (expired token — refresh tokens have a ~14-day TTL, shorter than the monthly cadence, so it correctly falls through) AND the password grant returned HTTP 415. Root cause: `_password_grant()` in `resolver/ingestion/acled_auth.py` omitted the `scope=authenticated` body parameter that ACLED's documented password-grant request requires (see acleddata.com/api-documentation/getting-started). ACLED's OAuth gateway conflates body/param-level problems into a non-standard 415 (the same code the whitespace bug above produced), so the missing scope surfaced as 415 rather than a cleaner 400. Fixed by adding `"scope": "authenticated"` to the password-grant `data` dict so the request matches ACLED's canonical example exactly (refresh grant left as documented — no scope). Also enriched both grant failures' `RuntimeError` messages with a `_body_snippet(resp)` of the response body so a future non-200 shows the server's reason instead of just the status code. **Rule: keep `_password_grant`'s params in lockstep with ACLED's documented curl example — the gateway reports missing/malformed params as 415, not 400.** Tests: `resolver/tests/test_acled_auth_tokens.py::test_password_grant_fetches_when_no_refresh` asserts `scope=authenticated`.
- **ACLED connector (`fetch_events`) path SIGKILLed at the 900s wrapper** (fixed, July 2026): the April 2026 timeout-retry fix above only hardened `ACLEDClient._fetch_page` (the `acled_to_duckdb` monthly-fatalities path). The *connector* path — module-level `fetch_events()` in `resolver/ingestion/acled_client.py`, run via `scripts.ci.run_connectors` in `resolver_update.yml` Phase 1 — still used a bare `session.get(..., timeout=60)` with no `Timeout`/`ConnectionError` retry and no total-time budget, so a slow-but-progressing all-country pull just ground on until the 900s per-connector wrapper (`scripts/ci/run_connectors.py`'s `CONNECTOR_TIMEOUT`, default 900) killed it (rc=124 → Phase 1 fatal). Fixed by wrapping the per-page `session.get` in the same retry loop (catch `Timeout`/`ConnectionError`, backoff `min(60, 2^attempt)`, up to `max_retries`), making the per-page timeout configurable (`ACLED_PAGE_TIMEOUT` env → config `timeout` → 120s, was hardcoded 60s), and raising headroom in the workflow: `CONNECTOR_TIMEOUT: "1800"` on the connectors step and job `timeout-minutes` 90→120. New env knobs: `ACLED_PAGE_TIMEOUT`, `ACLED_MAX_RETRIES`. Tests: `resolver/tests/test_acled_client_http.py::test_fetch_events_retries_on_timeout` / `::test_fetch_events_reraises_timeout_after_max_retries`. Note the two ACLED code paths remain separate (`fetch_events` for the connector CSV, `ACLEDClient._fetch_page` for monthly fatalities) — harden both when touching timeout behavior. **The retry+timeout bump alone did NOT fix it** — the connector still SIGKILLed at the raised 1800s limit. Real root cause (fixed, July 2026): the connector `query` block in `resolver/ingestion/config/acled.yml` sent **no `fields` param**, so ACLED returned all ~30 columns (actors, admin1-3, geo, tags, long `notes`) for every event globally, making the all-country pull huge and slow to transfer/parse — whereas the fast `ACLEDClient` path limits to 4 fields via the API's `fields=a|b|c` (pipe-separated) convention. Fixed by adding `fields: "event_date|iso3|country|event_type|sub_event_type|fatalities|notes"` to the `query` block (exactly the 7 fields `_prepare_dataframe` consumes; `notes` retained for the participants regex). Belt-and-suspenders: `fetch_events` now also honors a wall-clock deadline `ACLED_MAX_RUNTIME_SEC` (set to `1500` on the connectors step, 300s under `CONNECTOR_TIMEOUT=1800`) that stops pagination and returns partial rows (rc=0, `diagnostics_meta["truncated_by_deadline"]=True`, loud log) instead of being SIGKILLed, plus per-page stdout progress logging (previously nothing printed between auth success and the timeout). New env knobs: `ACLED_MAX_RUNTIME_SEC`. Tests: `::test_fetch_events_forwards_fields_param` / `::test_fetch_events_respects_runtime_deadline`.
- **Resolver Update reset run canceled at the 2-hour job timeout** (fixed, July 2026): the 2026-07-08 reset/backfill run (reset_db=true, months_back=12, GDACS_MONTHS=135) was canceled at exactly 2h00m during Phase 3 ACAPS — Phases 3–5 never ran, no `pythia-resolver-db` artifact was uploaded, and the entire run was wasted. The 2h limit was the **self-imposed** `timeout-minutes: 120` (GitHub's hard cap is 360 min/job on hosted runners). Three compounding time sinks, all fixed: (1) **GDACS per-event RSS enrichment was strictly sequential** — `_enrich_with_population` in `resolver/connectors/gdacs.py` slept `GDACS_REQUEST_DELAY` (1s) after *every* event, so the 135-month backfill's 3,272 events took 72 minutes (~55 min pure sleeping). Now a `ThreadPoolExecutor` with `GDACS_ENRICH_WORKERS` (default 6) thread-local sessions; per-worker delay retained (effective rate ≈ workers/(delay+RTT) ≈ 5 rps); `GDACS_ENRICH_WORKERS=1` restores exact sequential behavior. (2) **ACLED was pulled twice per run** — the connector `fetch_events` (27 min, truncated) and `acled_to_duckdb` monthly fatalities (16.5 min) each downloaded the same ~380k-event window. `collect_rows()` now stages the raw events (`resolver/staging/acled_events_raw.csv` + `.meta.json` sidecar with window/truncation/rows) and `acled_to_duckdb --events-file` aggregates from that file via `monthly_fatalities(events_frame=...)` (same `_normalize_events_frame` normalization + window filter), falling back to the API when the file is missing, truncated, doesn't cover the window, or has zero rows. (3) **The 12-month reset window blew the 1500s ACLED deadline** — `fetch_events` hit `ACLED_MAX_RUNTIME_SEC=1500` at page 77 of ~96 and **silently wrote truncated monthly aggregates to facts_resolved** (tier-0 data corruption; only discarded because the run died before export). The workflow now scales budgets on reset (`CONNECTOR_TIMEOUT` 1800→4200, `ACLED_MAX_RUNTIME_SEC` 1500→3900), `_write_run_summary` persists `truncated_by_deadline` into `acled_client_run.json`, and `run_connectors.py` emits a GitHub `::warning::` annotation whenever a connector's run summary has that flag — truncation is never silent again. Workflow-level containment: job `timeout-minutes: ${{ inputs.reset_db && 300 || 150 }}`, and every Phase 2/3/4 step has its own `timeout-minutes` (GDACS 60 reset / 20 normal; Phase 3 sources 30; Phase 4 sources 15) so one hung source is killed by its step timeout (they are `continue-on-error: true`) and the run still reaches Phase 5 export instead of losing everything. Tests: `resolver/tests/test_gdacs_connector.py::TestParallelEnrichment`, `resolver/tests/test_acled_events_file_reuse.py`.
- **`resolver_update.yml` reset input silently ignored** (fixed, July 2026): the `reset_db` workflow input ("Delete existing resolver.duckdb and rebuild from scratch?") was a free-text **string** (default `"false"`) gated by `inputs.reset_db == 'true'`. A manual run left the "Remove existing resolver.duckdb (reset mode only)" step skipped whenever the value wasn't the exact literal `'true'` (default untouched / mistyped), so the old DB was never deleted and canonical discovery re-downloaded it. Fixed by converting the input to `type: boolean` (GitHub renders an unambiguous checkbox) and updating all 7 comparison sites to boolean semantics (`inputs.reset_db` / `!inputs.reset_db`; the month-window ternaries at lines ~112-114 use `inputs.reset_db && '120'`). When editing this workflow, treat `inputs.reset_db` as a boolean, not the string `'true'`.
- **Post-calibration DB never published — workflow_run chain too deep** (fixed): On the monthly cycle the dashboard froze on stale resolutions (e.g. visible through March 13 even after the June 15 scoring/calibration loop ran green). Root cause was a workflow-orchestration race plus a GitHub `workflow_run` chain-depth limit. `Resolver Update` (cron) completing triggered TWO things in parallel: (a) the post-pipeline chain `Compute Resolutions → Compute SPD Scores → Compute Calibration` (each a `workflow_run` hop), and (b) `Publish Latest Data (Release)` directly (Publish used to subscribe to `Resolver Update`). Publish raced ahead and shipped Resolver Update's *pre-resolution* artifact (stale `resolutions`/`scores`, empty `calibration_weights`/`calibration_advice`, `max hs_runs.generated_at` = the prior HS run) — its regression guard saw new==old counts and published it. Meanwhile `Compute Calibration` (3 `workflow_run` hops below the root cron) completed *after* that publish, and GitHub did **not** propagate the `workflow_run` event one level further to fire Publish again — so the calibration **terminus DB** (which carries every upstream table forward plus fresh calibration outputs) was never published. Confirmed via the Actions API: on 2026-06-15 Publish #105 logged `RESOLVE_SOURCE=workflow_run`, `GITHUB_EVENT_WORKFLOW_RUN_CREATED_AT=2026-06-15T09:25:35Z` (= Resolver Update), published `resolutions=170, scores=7076, calibration_weights=0, calibration_advice=0`; Calibration #83 finished ~3 min later and no Publish #106 ever ran. Same pattern on 2026-05-15. Fixed by (1) adding an explicit `Publish post-calibration DB to release` step at the end of `compute_calibration_pythia.yml` that runs `gh workflow run publish_latest_data.yml --ref main -f run_id=${{ github.run_id }}` (workflow_dispatch is a GITHUB_TOKEN-allowed trigger, so no PAT needed; the step runs after the artifact upload and targets the calibration run's own `pythia-resolver-db`), and bumping that workflow's `permissions` from `actions: read` to `actions: write`; and (2) removing `"Resolver Update"` from `publish_latest_data.yml`'s `on.workflow_run.workflows` list so the racing pre-resolution publish can no longer fire. **Superseded 2026-07-16**: `"Horizon Scanner Triage"` was also removed from `publish_latest_data.yml`'s `on.workflow_run.workflows` (it used to fire a release in parallel with Sibyl); the forecast pipeline now publishes only via Sibyl's end-of-run dispatch. Publish entry points are now: **Sibyl end-of-run dispatch** (forecast pipeline), Calibration completion + its explicit dispatch (post-scoring chain), and manual `workflow_dispatch`. To recover an already-frozen release, manually dispatch `publish_latest_data.yml` with `run_id` = the most recent successful Compute Calibration run (post-scoring chain) or Sibyl run (forecast chain).
- **DI, CU, HW hazards fully silenced**: DI (Displacement Influx), CU (Civil Unrest), and HW (Heatwave) are now blocked at the hazard catalog level (`BLOCKED_HAZARDS` in `db_writer.py` — note it is a **union** of the literals `{CU, ACO, DI, HW}` with every hazard carrying `blocked: true` in `pythia/config.yaml`, so it is data-driven and can grow without a code change). They never enter RC assessment, triage, grounding, or question generation. This eliminates all wasted LLM/grounding calls on these unforecastable hazards. Historical data for these hazards is preserved in the database. HW/DI remain in `resolver/data/shocks.csv` and `UNRESOLVABLE_HAZARDS` in `compute_resolutions.py`.
- **Legacy blocked-hazard questions retired from diagnostics endpoints**: Question generation for `BLOCKED_HAZARDS` (DI/CU/HW/ACO + any `blocked: true` codes in `pythia/config.yaml`) was stopped, but ~138 legacy rows (97 DI/PA + 41 HW/PA at last count) remained `status='active'` in the `questions` table and cluttered `/v1/diagnostics/resolution_rates` with permanent 0% tiles that could never resolve. `scripts/retire_blocked_hazard_questions.py` (idempotent UPDATE) marks any matching active row as `status='retired'` and is wired into `compute_calibration_pythia.yml` so it runs on every pipeline cycle. `/v1/diagnostics/resolution_rates` and `/v1/diagnostics/summary` (the `questions_with_forecasts` / `questions_with_resolutions` / `questions_with_scores` counts via the new `_count_distinct_active_questions` helper) now exclude `status='retired'`. The `questions_by_status` block in `/v1/diagnostics/summary` still surfaces the retired count for auditing.
- **FEWS NET "zero PA-eligible matches" in `diagnose_pa_resolution_coverage.py` is NOT a connector bug** (investigated, no fix needed): the 2026-05 PA-coverage diagnostic showed DR/PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED with `events_in_facts=36` but `pa_eligible_in_facts=0`, and zero publisher rows attributed to FEWS NET. Looks like a missing source, but is actually structural-timing: (1) `resolver/connectors/fewsnet_ipc.py` correctly writes monthly `phase3plus_in_need` rows from 2019-01 through `2026-04` for ~34 countries (current as of mid-May); (2) all 42 active DR/PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED questions are from the 2026-05 and 2026-06 epochs (`window_start_date >= 2026-05-01`), so their earliest horizon (`window_start_date` month) is already past the calendar cutoff (`previous complete month` = 2026-04); (3) the 36 `events_in_facts` come from FEWS NET writing the *forecast* metric `phase3plus_projection` (31), GDACS DR rows (8), and IPC `phase3plus_projection` (3) — none of which the resolution filter `LOWER(metric) = 'phase3plus_in_need'` matches by design. By design, only the Current Situation scenario (`phase3plus_in_need`) resolves; Most Likely (`phase3plus_projection`) is prompt context only. The May/June epoch PHASE3 questions will start resolving naturally once FEWS NET publishes 2026-05 CS data (typically by mid-June) and the calendar cutoff advances. **If a future diagnostic run shows the same pattern for PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED, do not change the metric filter — it's the "questions too new" condition already surfaced as the "Awaiting first horizon" tile state in PR #765.**
- **IPC API now integrated as supplementary source** (fixed): The legacy IPC connector (`pythia/ipc_phases.py`) was dead code. A new Resolver connector (`resolver/connectors/ipc_api.py`) fetches Phase 3+ data from `api.ipcinfo.org` for non-FEWS NET countries (Afghanistan, Pakistan, Guatemala, etc.). The unified module `pythia/food_security.py` tries FEWS NET first, falls back to IPC. The old `fewsnet_food_security.py` is now a backward-compat shim. The `ipc_phases.py` module is retained as dead code.
- **OpenAI web search nondeterministic — Brave Search primary**: OpenAI web search (LLM-mediated) is nondeterministic and returns empty results ~40% of the time. Replaced with Brave Search API ($5/1K queries) as the primary grounding backend. Brave provides deterministic direct web search with freshness filtering. Three-tier fallback: Brave → OpenAI → Gemini. Override with `PYTHIA_GROUNDING_PRIMARY_BACKEND=openai` or `=gemini`. `BRAVE_SEARCH_API_KEY` env var required; if missing, falls through to OpenAI/Gemini gracefully. Brave is also wired as primary for adversarial check evidence searches. Backend module: `pythia/web_research/backends/brave_search.py`. Cost tracked via `cost_usd` in usage dict ($0.005 per query), logged to `llm_calls` as `model_id=brave-web-search`, `provider=brave`.

## Canonical DB artifact discovery

The `pythia-resolver-db` DuckDB artifact is shared across multiple workflows. **Discovery is centralized in the composite action `.github/actions/download-canonical-db`** (July 2026) — it is the single home of the candidate list:

1. **Horizon Scanner Triage** (`run_horizon_scanner.yml`)
2. **Pythia Pipeline Stage** (`pythia_pipeline_stage.yml`) — only its final stage uploads the canonical artifact; other stage runs upload `pythia-resolver-db-staged` and are skipped naturally when the canonical download fails
3. **Sibyl Deep Research** (`run_sibyl.yml`)
4. **Resolver Update** (primary ingestion: single-job workflow with 5 phases)
5. **Ingest Structured Data** (`ingest-structured-data.yml`) (mid-cycle refresh for fast-changing sources)
6. **Pythia — Compute Resolutions** (`compute_resolutions.yml`)
7. **Pythia — Compute SPD Scores** (`compute_scores.yml`)
8. **Pythia — Compute Calibration Weights & Advice** (`compute_calibration_pythia.yml`)

Candidates are sorted by `createdAt` descending; the first one that downloads successfully (and passes the optional per-candidate `validate-cmd`, used for the db_signature check) is used. The action supports: **forced mode** (`force-run-id` from an operator input — exact run or fail, no fallback), **Path A** (`run-id` = the triggering `workflow_run.id` — tried first, falls back to discovery when empty/expired; this is why manual `workflow_dispatch` of Compute Resolutions/Scores/Calibration works), **Path B** (canonical discovery), and `extra-workflows` (self-inclusion for maintenance workflows like Purge HS Run). Migrated consumers: compute_resolutions, compute_scores, compute_calibration_pythia, run_sibyl, resolver_update, ingest-structured-data, purge_hs_run. **Two consumers are intentionally bespoke** (marked with comments): `run_horizon_scanner.yml` (entangled with db_run_id/db_artifact_name overrides, DB_SOURCE mapping, bootstrap-reset ensure_schema) and `publish_latest_data.yml` (resolve outputs feed the regression guard). **When a new workflow starts producing `pythia-resolver-db`, add it to the composite action's list AND the two bespoke blocks.**

## Consolidated backfill workflow

The `resolver_update.yml` workflow is the primary data ingestion point (runs **28th monthly**, deliberately just ahead of the forecast pipeline's 1st-of-month cron — see the cadence note below). It is a single-job workflow with 5 sequential phases operating on one DuckDB file:

```
Phase 1 (fatal):    Resolver connectors (IDMC, ACLED, IFRC) → facts_resolved + acled_monthly_fatalities
Phase 2 (non-fatal): Resolution sources (FEWS NET IPC, IPC API, GDACS) → facts_resolved
Phase 3 (non-fatal): Structured data (conflict forecasts, ACAPS, ReliefWeb, ACLED political, NMME) → Pythia tables
Phase 4 (non-fatal): Context sources (ENSO, Seasonal TC, HDX Signals, CrisisWatch) → Pythia tables
Phase 5:            Verify + export pythia-resolver-db artifact
```

Phase 1 failures are fatal (core resolution data). Phases 2-4 use `continue-on-error: true` so individual source failures don't block the entire backfill. Backfill depth is controlled by `FEWSNET_MONTHS` (120 on reset, 12 otherwise), `IPC_API_MONTHS` (120 on reset, 24 otherwise), and `GDACS_MONTHS` (135 on reset, 3 otherwise).

**Time budgets (July 2026)**: the job timeout is `${{ inputs.reset_db && 300 || 150 }}` minutes (self-imposed; GitHub's cap is 360). The ACLED connectors step scales its budgets on reset (`CONNECTOR_TIMEOUT` 4200 vs 1800, `ACLED_MAX_RUNTIME_SEC` 3900 vs 1500) because reset runs use a ~12-month window. Every Phase 2/3/4 step carries its own `timeout-minutes` (GDACS 60 reset / 20 normal; Phase 3 sources 30; Phase 4 sources 15) so a hung source is contained by its step timeout and the run still reaches Phase 5 export. ACLED is fetched **once** per run: the connector stages raw events to `resolver/staging/acled_events_raw.csv` (+ `.meta.json` sidecar) and the monthly-fatalities step consumes it via `--events-file`, falling back to a fresh API fetch when the staged file is missing/truncated/doesn't cover the window. `run_connectors.py` emits a `::warning::` annotation when a connector's run summary has `truncated_by_deadline: true`.

The `ingest-structured-data.yml` workflow is a mid-cycle refresh for fast-changing sources (weekly Sunday 03:00 UTC). Scheduled runs only refresh `conflict`, `gdacs`, `reliefweb`, `acled_political`, and `gdelt`. Manual dispatch supports all sources for debugging.

### Monthly cadence: ingest on the 28th, forecast on the 1st

**The ingestion cron must stay ahead of the forecast cron.** `resolver_update.yml` is the ONLY refresh for the tier-0 resolution sources (ACLED monthly fatalities, IFRC, IDMC, FEWS NET / IPC); the weekly `ingest-structured-data.yml` run covers only the supplementary fast-changing sources (conflict forecasts, GDACS, ReliefWeb, ACLED political, GDELT). The intended order each month is:

```
28th 03:00 UTC  resolver_update  →  compute_resolutions  →  compute_scores
                                 →  compute_calibration  →  publish
 1st 00:00 UTC  pythia_pipeline_stage (hs_submit → … → fc_collect_finalize)
                                 →  Sibyl  →  publish
```

so the forecast runs on core data <4 days old **and** on calibration weights computed three days earlier. **Until 2026-08-03 this workflow ran on the 15th**, i.e. two weeks *after* the forecast it was meant to feed: the 2026-08-01 production run carried a `facts_resolved` ingest vintage of 2026-07-15 for IFRC/ACLED/IDMC/FEWS NET/IPC (17 days stale), and that week's supplementary ingest landed 2026-08-02 — one day too late to matter. Nothing was broken; the two monthly crons were simply out of phase.

Accepted cost of the move: each month's ground truth is resolved ~13 days later than under the 15th cadence (the resolution cutoff is the previous *complete* month either way). The ordering is pinned by `resolver/tests/test_ci_yaml_contains_idmc.py::test_backfill_ingests_before_the_monthly_forecast`, which reads both workflows — **do not move either cron without re-reading that test.**

## DB-first context data pattern

Context sources (ENSO, Seasonal TC, HDX Signals, CrisisWatch) follow a DB-first loading pattern:

1. During backfill (Phase 4): data is fetched from upstream sources and stored in DB tables (`enso_state`, `seasonal_tc_context_cache`, `hdx_signals`, `crisiswatch_entries`)
2. At HS/forecaster runtime: prompt loaders try DB first, fall back to live fetch/file cache if DB is empty
3. The HS workflow no longer downloads the seasonal TC artifact — TC data is in the DB artifact

This ensures the DB artifact is fully self-contained and reproducible.

## Run health diagnostics

- **`scripts/ci/batch_economics.py` — did the staged pipeline actually get the batch discount?** `llm_batches` carries per-batch outcome counters (`n_requests`/`n_succeeded`/`n_errored`/`n_expired`/`n_fallback_sync`) and `llm_batch_requests.status` carries the per-request terminal state, but until 2026-07-29 **nothing read either into any artifact or step summary** — so a pipeline in which every request silently took the synchronous full-price fallback produced artifacts byte-indistinguishable from a fully-batched one, leaving the economic premise of the staged pipeline unverified (same shape as the C1 prompt-cache finding). The reporter runs at **every** stage of `pythia_pipeline_stage.yml`, writes `diagnostics/batch_economics__<stage>.json` into the `pythia-stage-diagnostics` artifact, renders a Markdown rollup into the step summary, and emits a `::warning::` when the fallback-sync rate exceeds `--fallback-warn-pct` (default 20). The fallback rate is computed from the **per-request rows**, not the batch counters, because batch counters go stale when a stage dies mid-collect. Queries are scoped by `pipeline_id` — the DB travels between runs, so an unscoped rollup would mix pipelines. **Cost weighting (2026-07-29)**: a request count understates a cost problem whenever the batched and unbatched members differ in price, and they always do — on the reference run the fallback rate was *32% of requests* but **66% of batchable spend**, because the OpenAI members are the expensive ones.
