The reporter now takes `--hs-run-id` / `--fc-run-id` (both already exported in every stage's env, and passed at all five call sites) and adds a cost table with `batched_cost_usd` / `batchable_cost_usd` / `non_batchable_cost_usd` / `lost_discount_cost_usd`, warning on the **cost** share (`--cost-warn-pct`, default 20) as well as the request share. Two deliberate properties: (1) **spend is classified by CALL FAMILY, not by provider** (`_batchable_sql()`, since 2026-07-30): only the families the staged pipeline actually batches count as batchable — `spd_v2` / `binary_v2` by `phase`, and `hs_rc` / `hs_triage` by `call_type LIKE 'rc_pass_%'` / `'triage_pass_%'` (with a fallback for rows predating the `call_type` column). Grounding, scenarios and adversarial synthesis are never batchable and are reported as `non_batchable_cost_usd`. Provider-level attribution was tried first and **was itself the bug** — it reported google losing 41.6% of batchable spend on the 2026-07-30 SOM run when none of that spend was batchable at all, inflating the loss and making the headline unactionable; (2) **without a run id the cost and ledger sections report nothing rather than something wrong** — `llm_calls` accumulates in the travelling DB, which is how the ledger line once read "$1.37 of $65.26" for a run that had spent $3.29. **The step is `continue-on-error: true` and `main()` always returns 0 by design: stages 2..N of a live pipeline run whatever is on `main` (the poller dispatches with `--ref main`), so a diagnostics bug would otherwise take down real forecasting work — keep every new per-stage diagnostic non-fatal.** `post_run_diagnostics.py` likewise now runs at every stage (was final-stage only, so its prompt-cache zero-reads warning never evaluated the HS stages), writing `diagnostics/post_run__<stage>.txt`.
- **`scripts/ci/stage_health.py` — per-stage health snapshot + cost attribution.** Also runs at every stage of `pythia_pipeline_stage.yml`, writing `diagnostics/stage_health__<stage>.json` into `pythia-stage-diagnostics` plus a step-summary rollup. Four sections: (1) **grounding health** for RC and triage, counting the no-backend sentinels (`grounding-failed` / `grounding-breaker-tripped` / `grounding-unavailable`) so a **Brave breaker trip is countable at S1** instead of surfacing hours later in the S4 debug bundle — note the RC query must exclude `TRIAGE_GROUNDING_%` explicitly, since the `GROUNDING_%` LIKE pattern would otherwise swallow it; (2) RC level + track distribution from `hs_triage` — `rc_levels` reports `n_assessed` / `n_not_assessed` / `by_level_assessed` alongside the raw `by_level`, because folding the seasonal screen-outs (level 0 by default, never assessed) into level 0 overcounted it; (3) **`is_test` consistency** across the stamped tables (`hs_runs`/`hs_triage`/`llm_calls`/`hs_hazard_tail_packs`/`hs_adversarial_checks`), warning both on mixed values and on disagreement with `PYTHIA_TEST_MODE` — the boolean-input coercion hazard means a production month can be silently stamped as test data; (4) **cost by phase/call_type/model**, both run-scoped and windowed to the stage. The window comes from a `Record stage start` step (a bare `date` echo into `STAGE_STARTED_AT`) added at the top of each job — **without it the rollup can only report the travelling DB's cumulative spend, since `llm_calls` accumulates across stages** (note `llm_calls` timestamps live in a column named `timestamp`, not `created_at`). It deliberately does NOT import `scripts.dump_pythia_debug_bundle` (that module pulls pandas + the whole forecaster tree — far more import surface than an early stage should take); the grounding sentinels are imported from `horizon_scanner.llm_logging` behind a try/except with literal fallbacks. (5) **Sibyl** — coverage, budget-cap state and the opus/brave cost split from `sibyl_runs`, plus `sibyl_forecasts` status counts. Same never-fail-a-stage contract as batch_economics. **`run_sibyl.yml` now runs `stage_health` too (2026-07-29)**, with its own `Record stage start` step: Sibyl runs 40+ minutes on Claude Opus under a `$40` hard cap and was the ONLY stage in the pipeline with no telemetry of any kind — on the reference DB its spend turned out to be **$5.83** (opus $5.48 + brave $0.35), i.e. larger than the entire $3.29 forecast pipeline it accompanies. Its `llm_calls` carry `phase='sibyl'` and the run's `hs_run_id`, so the generic cost rollup itemises the spend for free. **The Sibyl section pins on the `sibyl_run_id` the run printed to stdout (`--sibyl-run-id`), never "newest `sibyl_runs` row for this hs_run_id"** — the latter was tried first and, verified against the real reference DB, resolved the PREVIOUS day's Sibyl run, which is exactly the stale-telemetry failure these diagnostics exist to catch; when Sibyl is gated out or dies before persisting, the section must report `available: false`, not someone else's run. Artifact: `pythia-stage-diagnostics-sibyl` (a distinct name — the pipeline-stage artifact is uploaded per stage run and must not be collided with). Tests: `scripts/ci/tests/test_stage_health.py`. Tests: `scripts/ci/tests/test_batch_economics.py`, wired into `forecaster-ci.yml` alongside the other Batch-API suites — **CI runs an explicit test-file list, so a new test file added here runs in NO workflow until it is named in one.**

- **`pythia/tools/summarize_all_phases.py` — the Resolver Update run summary** (rendered into the GitHub Step Summary at Phase 5). Reads `connectors_report.jsonl` for status/reason/duration and queries the just-written resolver DB (`--db`) for **authoritative** row/country counts, because the JSONL `counts` are inconsistent across connector types (per-country sources put *countries* in `written`; self-storing globals put *rows* in `fetched` and a nominal 1-2 in `written`; gdelt/nmme report neither). Output: a one-line verdict + per-phase roll-up + an effective-ingest-windows line (`FEWSNET_MONTHS`/`IPC_API_MONTHS`/`GDACS_MONTHS`, so non-default dispatch inputs are visible), a **Problems & Warnings** section listing every error/skip, every "OK but target table empty" silent failure, **stale conflict-forecast vintages** (per-source `MAX(forecast_issue_date)` age >45d, also rendered as a "Conflict Forecast Vintages" table), a **fewsnet_countries.json shrink detector** (on-disk entry count vs git HEAD — backstop for the merge-union connector writes), and **acled_political whole-country attribution drops** (read from the JSONL entry's `extras.attribution_dropped_countries`, stamped by `ingest_structured_data`), then uniform per-phase tables (`Source | Status | Rows in DB | Δ this run | Countries | Duration`). `_CONNECTORS` maps each connector_id to `(phase, table, where, iso_col)` with verified `facts_resolved.publisher` / `conflict_forecasts.source` filter strings. Degrades gracefully when the DB is unavailable (counts show `—`, no false empty-flags). Do NOT trust the raw JSONL `counts`/`http`/`normalized`/`coverage` fields for row counts — they are unreliable/unpopulated; the DB is the source of truth. Tests: `pythia/tests/test_summarize_all_phases.py` (temp-DuckDB, no network; note **no CI runs `pythia/tests` broadly** — forecaster-ci names an explicit file list, currently `test_model_registry.py`, `test_llm_batch.py` and `test_api_llm_costs.py`, so this file runs in no workflow).
- `hs_country_evidence` and `question_evidence` CSVs are the primary artifacts for diagnosing structured data connector health post-run.
- If a connector shows "unavailable" across all countries, it indicates an upstream data gap, a connector bug, or a missing environment secret.
- After applying fixes, a clean re-run must produce a queryable DuckDB artifact before connector health can be verified.
- The Resolver DuckDB inspection report includes 7 data quality checks (the logic lives in `scripts/ci/inspect_resolver_db.py`; `inspect_resolver_duckdb.yml` is a thin wrapper around it): conflict forecast value range validation (warns if probability values > 10), seasonal forecast country count, empty connector table warnings, IDMC/IDU hazard code consistency, conflict forecast staleness (> 45 days), HDX Signals note (cached as CSV, not in DB), and per-country conflict forecast sampling (IRN, SOM, ETH, SDN, UKR).
- **Batch Economics row in the debug bundle** (added 2026-07-29): `scripts/dump_pythia_debug_bundle.py` previously contained **zero** references to `llm_batches` / `service_tier` / batches, so for the reference run it reported *"LLM Calls: OK, 80 calls, 0 errors"* — true, and useless: that run had lost the batch discount on two thirds of its spend and had two OpenAI batches return nothing. **The bundle is the artifact a human reads first, and an error-free run can still be an expensive one.** `_load_batch_health` (mirroring the queries in `scripts/ci/batch_economics.py`, scoped by `hs_run_id`/`run_id` so the travelling DB's other runs are excluded) adds a **Batch Economics** row to the executive summary's Pipeline Status table: batches, fallback-sync share of requests, the `service_tier=batch` share of spend, and any batch that returned no results with its recorded cause. **FAIL** on an empty batch, **WARN** above a 20% fallback rate. Fully guarded — a pre-batch DB returns `{"available": False}` and the row is omitted rather than raising. Tests: `forecaster/tests/test_debug_bundle_batch_economics.py` — note the **four debug-bundle test files ran in NO workflow** until this change (forecaster-ci executes an explicit file list), so they are now all named in forecaster-ci's "Run debug-bundle tests" step.
- **CrisisWatch diagnostics** in the debug bundle (`scripts/dump_pythia_debug_bundle.py`): (1) Traffic-light health check in `_evaluate_pipeline_health` — reports OK/WARN/FAIL with country counts, arrow breakdown, and alerts; WARN if 0 entries, <10 countries, or no arrow data; (2) Per-country `crisiswatch_arrow` column in the data inject inventory CSV showing arrow direction and alert type; (3) `crisiswatch_health` section in health report JSON with arrow counts, alert counts, notable (deteriorated/alert) entries, and countries missing CrisisWatch data.
- **Food security diagnostics** in the debug bundle: Food security data comes from two separate connectors: `fewsnet_ipc` (48 FEWS NET countries, no auth) and `ipc_api` (non-FEWS NET countries, requires `IPC_API_KEY`). The data inject inventory shows per-country row counts (`fewsnet_ipc_rows`, `ipc_api_rows`) and source attribution (`food_security_source`) for each connector. The health report flags countries with DR questions but no food security data. The `ipc_phases` table (from the legacy `pythia/ipc_phases.py` module) is dead code and is not used for diagnostics.

## Scored-forecast analysis bundle (AI analysis, July 2026)

`scripts/ai_bundle/build_scored_forecast_bundle.py` builds **one self-describing zip per scoring round** — the artifact the owner hands to an AI analyst to find patterns that improve forecast performance. It runs at the **calibration terminus** (`compute_calibration_pythia.yml`, after advice generation, `continue-on-error` — a bundle bug must never block calibration or the publish chain) and is uploaded as the **`pythia-ai-analysis-bundle`** artifact (`ai_bundle/scored_forecast_analysis__{YYYY-MM}.zip`). Rationale: the canonical DB carries forecast-time state forward, so at calibration time it contains BOTH the reasoning (llm_calls prompts/responses, forecasts_raw traces, hs_triage rationale, hs_hazard_tail_packs grounding, adversarial checks, sibyl trials) AND the outcomes (resolutions, scores, calibration outputs) — one workflow, one bundle, covering **scored questions only** (analysis runs when there are results to learn from; owner decision 2026-07-28).

**Bundle contents** (zip root): `ANALYST_GUIDE.md` (generated FOR the consuming AI — bucket tables rendered live from `pythia.buckets`, score semantics incl. the never-blend-score-families rule, llm_calls vocabulary, known blind spots), `manifest.json`, `digest.md` (<200KB run summary), `briefing/` (2 size-capped markdown files for chat upload), `questions_index.csv`, `questions/{question_id}.json` (the per-question reasoning→outcome record: exact SPD prompt from llm_calls stored once + per-member `sent_prompt_override`; full member raw responses + parsed spd/trace/explanation from forecasts_raw; RC rationale_bullets/trigger_signals from hs_triage.regime_change_json; FULL untruncated grounding packs; adversarial check; ensemble grids; calibration weights; resolutions with realized_bucket + source_desc; scores; sibyl summary), `case_studies/` (worst-10 + best-10 per score_family by `ensemble_mean_v2` mean Brier, WITH sibyl trial traces inlined), and flat tables `scores_flat.csv` / `forecast_vs_outcome.csv` (P(realized bucket), modal bucket) / `rollups.csv` (never mixes binary/spd) / `calibration_weights.csv` (with movement vs previous vintage) / `calibration_advice.md` / `eiv_scores.csv`.

**Package layout**: `scripts/ai_bundle/{common.py,guides.py,build_scored_forecast_bundle.py}` — stdlib+duckdb core (defensive-schema helpers imported from `pythia/tools/_db_utils.py`; `pythia.buckets`/`forecaster.trace_validation` imports are best-effort guarded so sparse envs degrade). CLI: `--db`, `--out-dir`, `--months-back` (default 12, cutoff on `questions.window_start_date`), `--n-case-studies` (10/side/family), `--include-test` (default: excluded, matching calibration), `--include-sibyl-trials {case-studies|all|none}`, `--keep-staging`. `trace_quality` is **recomputed at bundle time** from stored `reasoning_trace_json` via `validate_reasoning_traces` (empty base-rate summary → the prior check returns a neutral 0.7; documented in the guide). Tolerates sparse/pre-Sibyl/pre-source_desc DBs (column/table existence guards). Tests: `scripts/ai_bundle/tests/` (temp-DuckDB, wired into `forecaster-ci.yml` + its `scripts/ai_bundle/**` path trigger).

**Skill extension (interpreter Phase 2, Aug 2026)**: `rollups.csv` carries `climatology_mean` + `skill_vs_climatology` (`skill = 1 − mean/climatology_mean`, computed per (hazard, metric, score_family, score_type) group from the `__ext_climatology` rows `score_baselines.py` writes — never across groups; empty where no climatology reference exists), the digest's model table shows a sample-weighted skill column, and `ANALYST_GUIDE.md` gained a "Skill and the reference forecasters" section (`_SKILL_SEMANTICS` in guides.py) including the Track-1-vs-Track-2 trap: the two tracks are disjoint populations, so compare each track's skill against its own climatology, never raw scores across tracks.

**Current-run bundle (interpreter Phase 2, Aug 2026)**: `scripts/ai_bundle/build_current_run_bundle.py` is the scored bundle's sibling (shares `common.py`/`guides.py`, reuses `build_question_record` with an explicit `run_id` — the shared builder's resolutions/scores reads are table-guarded so a pre-outcome DB degrades) and builds **the interpreter's input pack** at the END of the forecast chain: `run_sibyl.yml` runs it right after `compute_deviation` (non-fatal) and uploads **`pythia-current-run-bundle`** (`ai_bundle/current_run_analysis__{YYYY-MM}.zip`). Contents: `MANIFEST.json` (run ids, window, counts, lineup, cost, `pack_tokens` + the truncation record), generated `ANALYST_GUIDE.md` (deviation metric definitions, blind spots), `attention_index.csv` (per question: js_vs_baserate/log_ev_ratio/eiv from `forecast_deviation` via the bayesmc→mean→track2 preference, rc/tier/track, sibyl_covered, and the four rank columns — `rank_deviation`, `rank_impact_nominal`, `rank_impact_per_capita` (floored by `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_PER_CAPITA_FLOOR`, default 10000 — without it the ordering returns the same small island states every cycle), `rank_rc_disagreement` (|rc_score − js/ln2|) — plus the blended `attention_rank`), `questions/{id}.json` (shared record + `baserate` anchor/`deviation`/`scenarios`/`bucket_labels`; the empty outcome/scores stubs are dropped so absence is never read as zeros), `deltas.json` (matched on (iso3, hazard_code, metric) — epoch-suffixed qids never match across runs: top-N entries/exits, largest SPD movements by JSD of month-mean aggregate SPDs (lazy-reuses the calibration-advice `_js_divergence`; skipped-with-note when unavailable, never re-implemented), previous flagged risks tracking), `blind_spots.json` (no-base-rate questions, structural pending-too-new, standing caveats). **Token budget**: hard cap `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_MAX_PACK_TOKENS` (default 250k, chars/4 estimate recorded in the manifest); question records are written in **REPORT order** (the categorised rows first, via `interpreter.selection.selected_rows`, then the rest by attention rank) and the tail is truncated CONTIGUOUSLY with the dropped ids in `manifest.truncated_question_ids` and any dropped CATEGORISED ids called out separately in `manifest.truncated_categorised_question_ids` — attention order was tried first and gave 9 records against 21 rows the report had to cover. Records are also **trimmed for the interpreter** (`_trim_for_interpreter`: grounding text, the assembled prompt, member `response_text`/`human_explanation`, the adversarial block), every cut marked in place — an analyst's 46-125 KB record carries evidence the interpreter explains rather than re-reads, and a silently shortened prompt would let the report describe evidence that was never there. `interpreter/packs.py::assemble_input_text` applies the same report-order rule to the PROMPT budget, and names any covered row whose record it had to drop. `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_TOP_N` (default 8) sets the deltas' top-N. Tests: `scripts/ai_bundle/tests/test_current_run_bundle.py` (runs via forecaster-ci's existing `scripts/ai_bundle/tests/` step).

**Logging guarantees the bundle depends on** (all July 2026):
- **Exact sent prompt**: `_call_spd_model_for_spec` stashes `usage["sent_prompt_text"]` whenever the sent prompt differs from the base prompt (appended web evidence, self-search evidence, or the NEED_WEB_EVIDENCE no-evidence retry); `_call_spd_members_v2` **pops** it into `raw_call_entry["sent_prompt"]` (the pop is load-bearing — the multi-KB prompt must never be serialized into `usage_json`), and every spd_v2/binary_v2 log site writes `prompt_text=call.get("sent_prompt") or prompt`. The direct `_call_spd_model_compat` path (non-bayesmc else-branch) pops it inline. When adding a new SPD log site, follow this pattern. Tests: `forecaster/tests/test_sent_prompt_logging.py`.
- **HS call purposes are queryable**: `log_hs_llm_call` takes `call_type` (default `"chat"`); the 6 call sites pass `rc_pass_{n}` / `rc_grounding` / `triage_pass_{n}` / `triage_grounding` / `adversarial_search` / `adversarial_synthesis`. `phase` stays `'hs_triage'` for ALL HS rows (costs-page `phase_group` maps by phase — do not change it), and the synthetic UPPERCASED `hazard_code` encoding (`RC_{HZ}_PASS_{N}`, `GROUNDING_{HZ}`, …) is unchanged (debug-bundle emitters parse it). Pre-fix rows carry `call_type='chat'`.
- **`resolutions.source_desc`**: the winning source per resolution (e.g. `facts_resolved:IFRC:2026-03`, `zero_default`) is now persisted (was log-only); NULL on pre-fix rows.
- `pythia/db/util.py::write_llm_call` carries a deprecation docstring: it targets legacy schema.sql columns absent from the live table, Binder-errors on production DBs, and its only caller swallows the error — new call sites must rich-log via `log_forecaster_llm_call`/`log_hs_llm_call` with `call_chat_ms(log_call=False)`.

## LLM model registry and ensemble configuration

**All model choices are centralized in `pythia/config.yaml` (July 2026 model-registry refactor; lineup updated again 2026-07-07).** Two blocks:

1. **`llm.models` — the model registry**: one alias per model family (`gpt: openai:gpt-5.6-sol`, `gpt_mini: openai:gpt-5.6-luna`, `claude: anthropic:claude-opus-5`, `gemini_pro: google:gemini-3.1-pro-preview`, `gemini_flash: google:gemini-3.5-flash`, `gemini_lite: google:gemini-2.5-flash`). **To swap a family (e.g. GPT-5.4 → GPT-5.6), change the id on that ONE line and add a cost entry in `pythia/model_costs.json` — then check `_ANTHROPIC_NO_TEMPERATURE_PREFIXES` (Anthropic) / `_OPENAI_NO_TEMPERATURE_PREFIXES` (OpenAI) still cover the new id, and update `sibyl/config.py`'s `SIBYL_MODEL` if the Anthropic member moved.** Everything downstream references aliases. **xAI/Grok, Kimi (Moonshot), and DeepSeek were fully removed 2026-07-07** — no aliases, no call paths in `forecaster/providers.py` (`call_xai`/`call_kimi`/`call_deepseek`/`_call_openai_compatible` deleted), no env keys (`XAI_API_KEY`/`KIMI_API_KEY`/`DEEPSEEK_API_KEY` dropped from `_PROVIDER_ENV_KEYS`, workflows, `.env.template`), no cost entries. Supported providers are exactly openai/anthropic/google.
2. **`llm.profiles.prod`**: the `ensemble` list (5 members, each `- model: <alias>` plus per-model params like `thinking`/`temperature`) and the `roles` block mapping every non-ensemble purpose to an alias: `hs_default` (triage pass 1 + adversarial checks), `hs_triage_pass1/2`, `rc_pass1/2`, `hs_fallback` (JSON repair, gpt), `track2_spd`, `scenario_writer`, `grounding_gemini`, `grounding_openai`, `grounding_openai_fallback`, `grounding_claude` (pinned to `anthropic:claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` — cheap web-search synthesis; Opus is reserved for forecasts), `crisiswatch`. **All HS triage and RC passes, Track 2 SPD, and the scenario writer point at `gemini_flash` (gemini-3.5-flash).** The three **grounding-chain** roles are the reason non-frontier models legitimately appear on the Costs page and should not be "cleaned up": the RC/triage chain is **Brave → OpenAI → Gemini**, where each fallback fires only when the previous backend returned zero sources, so `grounding_openai` = **`openai:gpt-4.1`** (retry `grounding_openai_fallback` = `openai:gpt-4.1-mini`) and `grounding_gemini` = `gemini_lite` = **`google:gemini-2.5-flash`** (also the `crisiswatch` fallback). Their spend is small *because* Brave covers most countries; grounding rows carry `iso3` + `hs_run_id` but never `question_id`, so they show Countries&gt;0 / Questions=0 on the Costs page by construction. `grounding_claude` is reachable only from the deprecated question-level web-research and forecaster self-search paths, both disabled in prod.

Resolution lives in `pythia/llm_profiles.py` (`get_model_registry`, `resolve_model_ref`, `split_model_ref`, `get_role_model`, `get_ensemble_resolved`). Precedence at every call site: **purpose-specific env var > config role > `_ROLE_FALLBACKS`** (the single code-level fallback table — call sites contain no model-id literals). Legacy ensemble formats (`provider:model_id` strings, `{provider, model_id}` dicts) still parse; `get_purpose_model()` is a compat wrapper over `get_role_model()`.

Current ensemble (5 members; each member's params in config.yaml): gpt = **gpt-5.6-sol** (reasoning_effort=high, no temperature), claude = **claude-opus-5** (temperature dropped by the adapter — opus-4-7+/opus-5/sonnet-5+/fable reject sampling params; the SPD member carries no `thinking:` key, so Opus 5 runs its default adaptive thinking — but **an Anthropic thinking knob IS wired since interpreter Phase 3 (Aug 2026)**: `ModelSpec.thinking` on an Anthropic spec emits `output_config: {effort: <level>}` (low|medium|high|xhigh|max, GA, no beta header), gated by the literal-prefix tuple `_ANTHROPIC_EFFORT_PREFIXES` (opus-5/sonnet-5/fable/mythos — deliberately narrower than the temperature tuple: a missing entry is a safe no-op, a wrong one 400s, and Haiku rejects `effort` outright). Unset → byte-identical bodies to before. Thinking shares `max_tokens`, so any caller raising effort should also raise `max_output_tokens`. Sole production consumer today: the interpreter role), gemini_pro = gemini-3.1-pro-preview (thinkingLevel=medium, temperature 0.2), gemini_flash = gemini-3.5-flash (thinkingLevel=low, temperature 0.2; also Track 2), gpt_mini = **gpt-5.6-luna** (reasoning_effort=medium). GPT-5.6 also offers an `xhigh` effort tier between `high` and `max`; it targets agentic/coding loops, and a single-shot SPD call is not one, so the frontier member stays at `high`.

**Cost tracking is single-sourced** from `pythia/model_costs.json` (**per-1M-token** `[input, output]` USD since 2026-07-07 — previously per-1K; one entry per model_id — the lookup in `forecaster.providers.resolve_price_per_1m` normalizes `provider/model` forms; `estimate_cost_usd` and `llm_logging._compute_costs_for_usage` divide token counts by 1,000,000). Current entries (verified against provider pricing 2026-07-27): gpt-5.6-sol $5/$30, gpt-5.6-luna $1/$6, gpt-5.4 $2.50/$15 and gpt-5.4-mini $0.75/$4.50 (superseded, kept so a rollback still prices), gpt-4.1 $2/$8, gpt-4.1-mini $0.40/$1.60, gemini-3.5-flash $1.50/$9, gemini-3.1-pro-preview $2/$12, gemini-2.5-flash $0.30/$2.50 (was outdated at $0.15/$0.60), claude-opus-5 $5/$25, claude-opus-4-8 $5/$25 (superseded), claude-haiku-4-5-20251001 $1/$5 (the JSON key is the full dated id — the cost table is keyed by exact model_id, and that is also the id pinned in the `grounding_claude` role). The `MODEL_COSTS_JSON` env override (a fallback consulted only for models missing from the JSON) is now also interpreted per-1M; the `secrets.MODEL_COSTS_JSON` injection was removed from `run_horizon_scanner.yml` and `run_sibyl.yml` because a stale per-1K secret would silently misprice fallback lookups. `pythia/tests/test_model_registry.py` enforces: every registry alias/role/ensemble member resolves, and every reachable model has a cost entry (a missing entry means silent $0 cost logging). When adding a role call site, add the role to config.yaml `roles`, `_ROLE_FALLBACKS`, and `CONSUMED_ROLES` in the test.

**Display names are specific model ids** (2026-07-07): `_provider_display_name` in `forecaster/providers.py` now returns the model id itself (e.g. `gemini-3.5-flash`, `gpt-5.6-sol`, `claude-opus-5`) instead of generic family labels (`Gemini Flash`, `Gemini Pro`, `OpenAI-gpt-5.2`). This is the `model_name` stored in `forecasts_raw`/`scores`/`llm_calls`, shown on the dashboard (Performance, Costs, question pages), and used as the calibration-weight key. HS triage/RC/adversarial ModelSpecs likewise use the resolved model id as `name`. Historical rows keep their old names; old-name calibration weights simply don't match the new names, so new members start at weight 1.0 (the documented model-swap behavior). Aggregate names are unchanged and intentionally stable: `ensemble_mean_v2`, `ensemble_bayesmc_v2`, `track2_flash`, `sibyl`. The frontend prettifier `web/src/lib/model_names.ts` renders these ids (e.g. "Gemini 3.5 Flash", "Claude Opus 4.8"); the run-summary API now returns `tracks.track2.model` (the specific track2 model id) and derives `ensemble.expected` from `get_ensemble_resolved()` instead of a hardcoded count.

**HS_MODEL_ID is now a true override** (env > `hs_default` role > first-Google-in-ensemble legacy fallback). Before the refactor it was dead in CI: `resolve_hs_model()` consulted the ensemble-derived `GEMINI_MODEL_ID` first, so the `HS_MODEL_ID` set in `run_horizon_scanner.yml` never took effect. The workflow env was removed to preserve that behavior — do not re-add it without intending to change the HS default model. `hs_default` now resolves to gemini-3.5-flash (was Gemini Pro).

**Config is authoritative for the production ensemble** (since 2026-07): `run_horizon_scanner.yml` no longer sets `PYTHIA_SPD_ENSEMBLE_SPECS` — the SPD ensemble, membership AND per-model params, comes from config.yaml. The `PYTHIA_GOOGLE_SPD_THINKING_LEVEL_*` env vars in the workflow are fallbacks only — `ModelSpec.thinking` from config wins. Do not re-add `PYTHIA_SPD_ENSEMBLE_SPECS` to the workflow except for one-off experiments. The old `PYTHIA_BLOCK_PROVIDERS: xai` kill-switch was removed from the workflow along with the provider itself (the mechanism still works for openai/anthropic/google). **The Google thinking-config gate was fixed 2026-07-07**: `call_google` now sends `thinkingConfig.thinkingLevel` for all Gemini 3.x ids via `_is_gemini_3_family()` (`gemini-3-*`, `gemini-3.1-*`, `gemini-3.5-*`), so gemini_pro's `thinking: medium` actually reaches the API (it was previously dropped because the gate only matched `gemini-3-`). The per-family SPD timeout/thinking env fallbacks in `call_chat_ms` use `_google_model_family()` (substring "flash"/"pro" within the 3.x family) instead of exact `gemini-3-flash`/`gemini-3-pro` substrings, so version bumps don't silently disable the env overrides. Gemini 2.5 models are deliberately excluded (they use `thinkingBudget`, not `thinkingLevel`).

Track 2's stored `model_name` stays `track2_flash` regardless of which model backs the `track2_spd` role (it is a stable aggregate name in `forecasts_ensemble`/`scores`). Note on calibration when swapping ensemble members: per-model calibration weights are keyed by display name (now the specific model id, e.g. `gemini-3.1-pro-preview`); a swapped-in model with a new name simply gets weight 1.0 until it accumulates scores — no migration needed.

| `resolver-ci-fast.yml` | Fast resolver tests | On push/PR + daily 06:00 UTC |

| `poll_llm_batches.yml` | Poll provider batches, dispatch next pipeline stage | Cron `*/15` (throttled to ~hourly; **backstop only** — submit stages ignite it, then it self-re-arms ~every 5.5 min) |

## Supported hazards and metrics

Hazards allowed (from `config.yaml`): FL, DR, TC, HW, ACE, DI, CU, EC, PHE. MULTI/OT disabled; ACO retired in favor of ACE.

**Active pipeline hazards**: Only **ACE, DR, FL, TC** are processed by the pipeline. DI, CU, and HW are fully silenced — they are blocked at the hazard catalog level (`BLOCKED_HAZARDS` in `db_writer.py`) and never enter RC assessment, triage, grounding, or question generation. This saves all LLM/grounding calls previously spent on these unforecastable hazards. The pipeline processes exactly 4 hazards per country (minus seasonal screen-outs for TC).

Question generation (`scripts/create_questions_from_triage.py`) supports:
- **ACE**: FATALITIES, PA
- **DR**: PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED, EVENT_OCCURRENCE (PA remapped to PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED for FEWS NET countries)
- **FL**: PA, EVENT_OCCURRENCE
- **TC**: PA, EVENT_OCCURRENCE

## Structured data bulk ingest

`pythia/tools/ingest_structured_data.py` orchestrates bulk fetch/store for all structured data connectors. The `_SOURCE_GROUPS` dict maps group names to table names. Currently registered groups: `views`, `conflictforecast`, `acledcast`, `acaps_inform_severity`, `acaps_risk_radar`, `acaps_daily_monitoring`, `acaps_humanitarian_access`, `ipc`, `reliefweb`, `acled_political`, `nmme`, `gdacs`, `fewsnet_ipc`, `ipc_api`, `gdelt`. Each group has a `_bulk_fetch_*` function that parallelizes API calls across countries via `ThreadPoolExecutor`. The `acled_political` group fetches event-level political data (protests, riots, strategic developments) from the ACLED API via `pythia.acled_political.fetch_acled_political_events` and stores via `store_acled_political_events`. The `gdacs` group delegates to `resolver.tools.run_pipeline` with `--connectors gdacs`, which writes to `facts_resolved` and `facts_deltas` via the standard Resolver pipeline. GDACS, FEWS NET IPC, and IPC API are self-storing sources (no per-country store function). The `ipc_api` group delegates to `resolver.tools.run_pipeline` with `--connectors ipc_api`, fetching IPC Phase 3+ population estimates from api.ipcinfo.org for countries not covered by FEWS NET; requires `IPC_API_KEY`; backfill depth controlled via `IPC_API_MONTHS` (default 24). The `fewsnet_ipc` group delegates to `resolver.tools.run_pipeline` with `--connectors fewsnet_ipc`, fetching IPC Phase 3+ population estimates from the FEWS NET Data Warehouse; backfill depth controlled via `FEWSNET_MONTHS` (default 12; 120 for backfill to 2016). The `gdelt` group delegates to `pythia.gdelt.fetch_and_store_gdelt_indicators()`, which downloads the last `GDELT_EVENTS_DAYS` days of GDELT 1.0 daily event exports (default 90), computes per-country daily CAMEO-tiered aggregates (T1 direct violence 18/19/20, T2 escalatory posture 15/17, T3 political deterioration 14/16) and writes them to `gdelt_conflict_indicators`. Self-storing. The `ingest-structured-data.yml` workflow runs weekly (Sunday 03:00 UTC) as a mid-cycle refresh for fast-changing sources (`conflict`, `gdacs`, `reliefweb`, `acled_political`, `gdelt`). The primary ingestion of all sources happens in the `resolver_update.yml` backfill workflow (28th monthly). GDACS backfill depth is controlled via the `gdacs_months` workflow input (default 3; set to 135 for full backfill to 2015).
